#### 56. Karakush (Group)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Arts, Music & Film*

**Karakush** (stylized KARAKUSH; Iradi *kara kush*, "golden eagle") is a five-member Iradi [[Mor-Pop]] girl group formed in [[Talas]] in 2018 by the agency Kök Ay Media. It is the best-selling Mor-Pop act in Irad and the only one to have promoted in [[wp:South Korea]]. Its 2021 single "Ak Börü" ("White Wolf") became a viral hit abroad, but none of its later releases charted outside Irad, and the foreign music press usually describes the group as a one-hit wonder.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

## History
Kök Ay Media assembled the group through open auditions in Talas and [[Nurzhan]] in 2017 and trained its members for a year on the [[wp:K-pop]] model. The line-up is Aruuke Temir kyzy (leader, vocals), Dana Pak (lead dancer; from the [[Koryo-saram in Irad|Koryo-saram]] community of Nurzhan), Saltanat Abdrakhmanova (rap), Meerim Sultan kyzy (vocals) and Kamila Nurlanova (vocals). Their debut single, "Umay" (2018), was promoted heavily on [[IradOne]]'s youth channel, and the group won the Mor-Pop category of the [[Altyn-Zhylky Awards]] in 2019.<sup>[1]</sup>

"Ak Börü" was released in February 2021. Its video, filmed on a frozen lake in the Talas valley, shows the members in white wolf-fur capes and horned headdresses based on [[Morkhan (Mythology)|Morkhan]], dancing to a slowed, detuned [[Komuz (Iradi)|komuz]] riff. A dance challenge built on its chorus spread on short-video platforms, and the song found an audience among [[wp:Koryo-saram]] listeners in [[wp:Kazakhstan]] and [[wp:Uzbekistan]] and among Korean-American listeners. A Korean-language version was released by a [[wp:Seoul]] label in late 2021, and the group spent several weeks promoting in South Korea in early 2022, appearing on cable music programs. The single briefly entered the lower reaches of South Korean digital charts. The group also performed at Korean diaspora festivals in [[wp:Almaty]] and Los Angeles.<sup>[2]</sup>

The follow-ups "Morkhan Tünü" ("Morkhan's Night", 2022) and the English-language "Talas Moon" (2023) did not chart abroad, and the Korean contract ended in 2023. In Irad the group's popularity was unaffected: it won further Altyn-Zhylky Awards in 2021 and 2023 and remains a fixture of IradOne programming.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Reception
Karakush's horned headdresses drew criticism from clergy of the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]], who object to Morkhan imagery in [[Mor-Wave]] and Mor-Pop alike. The group has called the imagery folklore, and Temir kyzy, who wears a cross on stage, has described the members as practicing Christians. Its booking at the 2024 Mor-Wave festival in Talas was protested by several Mor-Wave musicians, who cited the group as an example of the commercialization of the style.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[3]</sup>

#### 57. Altyn Muuz (Group)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Arts, Music & Film*

**Altyn Muuz** (Iradi, "golden horn") is a four-member Iradi [[Mor-Pop]] boy group formed in 2019 through *Jyldyz Jolu* ("Star Road"), a talent show on [[IradOne]]'s youth channel. Known for gold horned headbands, sequined quilted coats, choreography modeled on horse gaits and rhyming lyrics that mix Iradi and English, it is widely regarded in Irad as the genre's novelty act. It has never had a following outside the country.<sup>[1]</sup>

## History
The members are Nurbek Asanov, Ermek Toktosun uulu, Aibek Ismailov and Timur Bakyt uulu, who finished as the top four contestants of *Jyldyz Jolu* and were signed as a group by the show's producers. Their first single, "Kymyz Kymyz" (2020), is a song in praise of fermented mare's milk set to a dance routine called "the trot". It was followed by "Morkhan Baby" (2021) and "Zhylky Love" (2022). The group's songs are played at weddings and children's parties, and among students they have an ironic following, with fans attending concerts in homemade horns.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

In 2021 the group performed "Morkhan Baby" on an IradOne Sunday-morning children's program, prompting complaints from clergy of the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]], who associate Morkhan with Satan. Later broadcasts of the episode omitted the song. On a 2022 tour of district towns several venues declined to book the group because of its bull horns, which many rural Iradis regard as a bad omen, and for the rest of the tour it wore golden argali (wild sheep) horns instead. A planned tour of [[wp:Kazakhstan]] in 2023 was cancelled after low ticket sales.<sup>[2]</sup>

## Reception
Critics have generally treated the group as a joke; the *[[Talas Herald]]* called "Zhylky Love" the most earnest song ever written about a horse. Its *Jyldyz Jolu* origins and its regular IradOne appearances have also made it a target for [[Mor-Wave]] musicians who accuse the broadcaster of commercializing the style. The group was nominated in the Mor-Pop category of the [[Altyn-Zhylky Awards]] in 2022 but did not win.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[3]</sup>

#### 58. Mor-Folk
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Arts, Music & Film*

**Mor-Folk** is a subgenre of [[Mor-Wave]] that sets traditional instruments and melodies drawn from the liturgical chant of the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]] over the dark synthesizer sound shared by all the "Mor" genres.<sup>[1]</sup>

Mor-Folk ensembles play the [[Komuz (Iradi)|komuz]], the end-blown *[[Choor (Flute)|choor]]* and other wooden flutes, and hand-played frame drums over analog synthesizer drones and pads. Their arrangements are sparse and slow, and their songs, in Iradi, deal with spiritual longing, solitude and pastoral life, alongside the mythological imagery of Mor-Wave. The style developed among Mor-Wave musicians in [[Talas]] in the 2010s. Ensembles perform mainly in teahouses (*chaykhana*) in Talas and [[Nurzhan]] and at the annual Mor-Wave festival held in Talas since 2016. Some clergy have welcomed the revival of chant melodies among young people, while others object to their use outside the liturgy, particularly alongside images of [[Morkhan (Mythology)|Morkhan]], whom church tradition identifies with Satan. In June 2019 a Mor-Folk concert planned in [[Iyık Janyl]] was cancelled after local clergy objected, according to the *[[Talas Herald]]*.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup> The best-known Mor-Folk recording, "[[Dream of Living]]" (2021) by the ensemble [[Eski Ün]], became the only Mor-Folk song to find a wide audience abroad after its use on the soundtrack of *[[wp:True Detective: Night Country|True Detective]]* in 2024.

#### 59. The Epic of Janyl
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Language & Literature*

**The Epic of Janyl** (Iradi: *Janyl Baatyr*, "Janyl the Valiant") is an Iradi oral heroic epic about Janyl, the warrior woman venerated by the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]] as [[Janyl, Mother of Irad|St. Janyl, Mother of Irad]]. Bards (*ırchy*) sing it to the [[Komuz (Iradi)|komuz]]. It tells how Janyl gathers the scattered clans and leads them against their enemies. Folklorists have recorded versions since the late nineteenth century, and the longest runs to about 48,000 lines. Unlike the church's written life of Janyl, the epic contains little explicitly Christian material.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

## Content
The versions share a core of episodes:
- Janyl's birth to a chieftain and her childhood among her father's herds, where she learns to ride and shoot;
- the gathering of the clans, as she rides from camp to camp to unite clans scattered by raiders;
- battles and ambushes in the mountain passes, with Janyl shooting from the saddle;
- negotiations over pastures with foreign governors, which she wins by wit as well as force;
- in some versions, a closing episode in which the clans swear an oath to her at a mountain spring.

Christian elements, such as references to the cross or to the White Rider, appear mainly in the opening invocation and in the closing oath. Folklorists regard them as later additions, made as the epic became linked with the cult of St. Janyl.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Recording and publication
Russian and Tatar ethnographers wrote down fragments in the 1880s and 1890s. Soviet collectors gathered fuller versions in the 1930s–1950s. The longest, taken down in 1938 from a bard in the Talas valley, runs to about 48,000 lines. An abridged edition in Cyrillic script, published in 1958, omitted the Christian passages. In the 1960s–1980s the ethnomusicologist [[Aigul Choro kyzy]] recorded several sung versions on tape. A complete edition of the 1938 text in Latin script was published in 2001.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[3]</sup>

## Performance
Bards perform the epic over several evenings at weddings, memorial feasts and gatherings during the [[Feast of St. Janyl]]. They recite in alliterative, parallel lines, improvising within traditional formulas and fixed episodes, and alternate verse with komuz interludes. Performances were rare in the Soviet period and revived after 1992. Extracts of the epic are now taught in schools in the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]].<sup>[1]</sup>

## Relationship to other traditions
Folklorists note close parallels with *Janyl Myrza*, a Kyrgyz epic about a warrior maiden, and with other Turkic heroine epics. Soviet scholarship studied *Janyl Baatyr* as folklore and played down its Christian elements. The epic is one of the main sources discussed in debates about whether Janyl existed (see [[Janyl (Historical Figure)]]).<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 60. Feast of St. Janyl
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Religion*

The **Feast of St. Janyl** (formally the Feast of St. Janyl; Iradi: *Janyl Mairamy*) is the principal feast of the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]], held on August 24 in honor of [[Janyl, Mother of Irad|St. Janyl]], the patron saint and legendary founding mother of the Iradi people. Its center is the [[Church of St. Janyl]] in [[Iyık Janyl]], where pilgrims, many of them on horseback, gather from both the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ) and the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). The feast combines the liturgy with the blessing of the herds, the distribution of blessed bread, processions, horse races and communal meals. The pilgrimage season continues into September.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

## History
Little is known of the cult of Janyl before the eighteenth century. The church built at Iyık Janyl in 1727 under a charter of the Khan of [[wp:Khanate of Kokand|Kokand]] was dedicated to her, and its foundation made the town the center of her feast. When the church entered into union with Rome in 1931, its own calendar, including the feast of Janyl, was kept. Under Soviet rule public celebration was banned from 1937. Families continued to make private pilgrimages, often at night, until the feast was celebrated openly again in the late 1980s. After the cathedral was returned to the church and reconsecrated in September 1992, the feast resumed its full form.<sup>[1]</sup>

Since the partition of 2007, Iyık Janyl has lain within the ISEZ, close to the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]]. Pilgrims from the tribal regions cross the line at the feast under arrangements agreed each year in the Joint Liaison Committee of the [[United Nations Mission in Irad]]. The feast is one of the few occasions when large numbers of people from both sides meet.<sup>[3]</sup>

## Observances
The main events are:
- a vigil on the evening of August 23, with a candlelit procession from the old town to Cathedral Hill;
- the liturgy ([[wp:Holy Qurbana]]) celebrated by the Archbishop of Iyık Janyl on the morning of August 24, broadcast by [[IradOne]], with large quantities of juniper incense (*[[Arsha Juniper Incense Tradition|arsha]]*);
- the Blessing of the Herds, in which priests, some of them mounted, sprinkle spring water on horses and sheep assembled on the cathedral esplanade;
- the distribution of blessed but unconsecrated *[[Kümöch Sacramental Bread|kümöch]]* to pilgrims;
- horse races, archery contests and communal meals of beef and mutton, with *[[Kymyz (Iradi Beverage)|kymyz]]*, in the afternoon.

Rural churches hold their own blessings of the herds on the same day.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

## Attendance and pilgrimage
The archbishopric's attendance estimates in recent years have exceeded 150,000; no independent counts are available. Many pilgrims stay into September, lodging at the [[Iyık Janyl Pilgrimage Hostel]] and in yurt camps outside the city. Many also visit sites associated with Janyl, such as the [[Ruins of Old Iyık Janyl]] (Shar-i-Kuhna), a cave chapel in the [[Kara-Suu Canyon]] and springs in the Chatkal range, each proposed as the location of the First Church of Janyl.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 61. Kamcha (Horsewhip)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Horses & Equestrianism*

A **kamcha** (Iradi: *kamcha*) is a short riding whip of braided rawhide with a handle of wood or horn, often decorated with silver. Carrying one marks a rider as an adult, and an ornate kamcha signals the owner's standing.<sup>[1]</sup>

The lash is usually braided from several thongs of rawhide and ends in a flat leather tip; a loop at the handle hangs the whip from the wrist. Riders use it more as a signal than to strike the horse. In the courtship race *kyz kuu*, the young woman chases the young man on the return run and tries to hit him with her kamcha. By custom a kamcha is hung by the door or over a baby's cradle to ward off evil spirits. Decorated whips are made in the leather workshops of the [[Old Talas Citadel Quarter]]; see also [[Silver Inlaid Kamcha]] and [[Embossed Leather Kamcha]].<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 62. At Chabysh (Racing)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Sport*

**At chabysh** (Iradi: *at chabysh*, "horse race") is a traditional long-distance horse race over open country, usually between 20 and 50 km. It tests the stamina of the horses rather than their speed over a short course.<sup>[1]</sup>

Races are held at weddings, memorial feasts, the August Kurultai on the [[Aksay Plateau]] and the [[Feast of St. Janyl]]. The riders are traditionally boys chosen for their light weight. [[Chongtor Horse|Chongtor]] horses dominate mountain courses and [[Akyel Horse|Akyel]] horses the flatter steppe courses. In the ISEZ the [[National Equestrian Sports Directorate]] sanctions major races. Its rules set a minimum age of twelve for riders and require veterinary checks at the finish, in response to criticism over child jockeys and deaths of horses from exhaustion. Races in the tribal regions are held under clan rules.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 63. Tyin Emmei (Mounted Sport)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Sport*

**Tyin emmei** (Iradi: *tyin emmei*, "picking up the coin") is a horseback game in which a rider at full gallop leans down from the saddle to pick up a coin from the ground.<sup>[1]</sup>

The coin, traditionally silver, is wrapped in a piece of cloth so that it can be seen and gripped, and it is placed along a straight course. Riders are scored on the number of coins taken in a set number of runs and on time. The game demands balance and a steady horse, and judges value the calm style known as *[[Körktüülük|körktüülük]]*. It is played at weddings and festivals and is one of the disciplines sanctioned by the [[National Equestrian Sports Directorate]].<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 64. Oodarysh (Mounted Wrestling)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Sport*

**Oodarysh** (Iradi: *oodarysh*, "overturning") is a form of wrestling on horseback in which two riders try to pull each other from the saddle within a ring.<sup>[1]</sup>

Bouts take place in an earthen ring, and a wrestler wins by unseating his opponent or forcing him to touch the ground. Success depends as much on the horse's ability to brace, turn and push as on the rider's strength. Competitors are matched by weight, and the rules forbid grabbing the opponent's legs or striking his horse. Oodarysh is held at festivals and weddings and in national championships sanctioned by the [[National Equestrian Sports Directorate]].<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 65. Tai-Kazan
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Sport*

The **tai-kazan** (Iradi: *tai kazan*) is the raised circular goal used in [[Ulak Tartysh (Sport)|ulak tartysh]], the team sport in which mounted players compete for a goat or calf carcass.<sup>[1]</sup>

Each team defends a tai-kazan at its end of the field. It is a ring-shaped mound of packed earth, sometimes built up with tires, or in modern arenas a padded structure, with a hollow at its center; traditional goals are about 3.6 meters across and 1.2 meters high. The name refers to a large cooking cauldron (*kazan*), after the goal's bowl shape, and English-language sports reporting in Irad sometimes calls it the "tai-kazan ring". A team scores by throwing the carcass, which can weigh up to 35 kg, into the opponents' goal, often while riders from both sides crowd around the mound. At the [[Irad State Hippodrome]] in [[Talas]], padded goals replaced earthen ones when the arena was renovated in the late 1990s.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 66. Choor (Flute)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Arts, Music & Film*

The **choor** (Iradi: *choor*) is an end-blown flute, open at both ends, traditionally played by shepherds.<sup>[1]</sup>

It is made from a length of river cane or from wood hollowed out and bound, and it usually has four or five finger holes. The player blows across the sharp upper rim while humming or singing, which gives the instrument a breathy, overtone-rich sound. Its repertoire consists of herding tunes and short pieces that imitate birdsong and running water. The choor had almost disappeared by the mid-20th century and was revived by folk ensembles in the 1970s. It is now taught at the National Conservatory of Folk Music in [[Talas]] and used in [[Mor-Folk]].<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 67. Qatiq
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Culture & Folklore*

**Qatiq** (Iradi: *qatiq*) is a thick fermented milk eaten daily in Iradi households. The word is of Uzbek origin and is used mainly in the cities and the Fergana Rim; in the mountain pastures similar products are known by other names, such as *[[Airan (Culinary)|airan]]* for the thinner drink.<sup>[1]</sup>

Qatiq is made by culturing boiled cow's or goat's milk with a spoonful from an earlier batch. Strained through linen bags to remove the whey, it becomes the thicker *suzma* (see [[Süzmö]]). It is eaten with flatbread, added to soups and served with meat dishes. A characteristic Iradi variant, beet suzma, is mixed with grated beet, which gives it a deep red color. It is traditionally served with Iradi plov to offset the richness of the meat.<sup>[1]</sup>

</ol>

#### 68. Kurut
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Culture & Folklore*

**Kurut** (Iradi: *kurut*, from Kipchak *kuru*, "dry") is a hard, dried curd of salted sour milk, usually made in small balls. Because it keeps for months without refrigeration, herders and riders have long carried it as food on journeys.<sup>[1]</sup>

Strained sour milk (*suzma*) is salted, rolled by hand into balls or shaped into small cakes, and dried in the sun on reed mats in the summer pastures. Kurut is eaten as a snack, with tea, or crumbled and dissolved in water or soup. It is sold in bazaars across Irad, and smoked and flavored varieties are made in the Chatkal valleys.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 69. Bürkit-Togoo
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Culture & Folklore*

The **bürkit-togoo** (Iradi: *bürküt togoo*) is the heavy leather gauntlet worn by eagle hunters (*berkutchi*) to protect the hand and forearm from the talons of a golden eagle (see [[Burkut Saluu]]).<sup>[1]</sup>

The gauntlet is made of several layers of thick cowhide or goat leather and lined with felt or sheep's wool, and it reaches nearly to the elbow. Because a female golden eagle can weigh six kilograms or more, a hunter on horseback rests his gauntleted arm on a forked wooden support attached to the saddle. Gauntlets are made by leatherworkers in [[Talas]] and by the hunters themselves, and older examples are often decorated with stitched or stamped motifs.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 70. Talas Symphony Orchestra
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Arts, Music & Film*

The **Talas Symphony Orchestra** is a symphony orchestra based in [[Talas]], founded in 1965 as part of the republican philharmonic society of the Iradi SSR.<sup>[1]</sup>

Its repertoire combines the European classical canon with works by Iradi composers, some of which add the [[Komuz (Iradi)|komuz]], the *[[Kyl-kyak|kyl-kyak]]* and the *[[Choor (Flute)|choor]]* to the orchestra. Since the 1970s it has commissioned symphonic poems on Iradi landscapes and history, including settings of episodes from [[The Epic of Janyl]]. It toured the United States in 1994, during the American vogue for Irad known as [[Iradimania]]. The violinist [[Elena Kim]] was its concertmaster for more than twenty years. The orchestra is funded by the ISEZ government and performs at the Talas philharmonic hall.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 71. Kok-Terek (Lost Film)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Arts, Music & Film*

***Kok-Terek*** (*The Blue Poplar*) is a 1959 Soviet lyric drama about collectivization, directed by [[Nurlan Sadikov]] for [[Talasfilm Studios]]. It was Sadikov's first feature film. It was distributed only within the Iradi SSR, and no complete copy is known to survive; the film is known from production stills and studio records.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

Sadikov shot the film on location in the [[Talas Alatau]] with natural light and a cast of shepherds who had not acted before. Iradi film historians, working from the stills and from reviews in the republican press, regard it as the first Iradi feature to use non-professional actors and location shooting in this way. Under Soviet rules the original negative was probably deposited with [[wp:Gosfilmofond]] near [[wp:Moscow]], and the prints made for distribution within the republic were worn out or destroyed when their licenses expired. The [[Irad Film Society]] has appealed for surviving prints or fragments.<sup>[2]</sup>

</ol>

#### 72. Tolkun (Lost Film)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Arts, Music & Film*

***Tolkun*** (*The Wave*) is a 1963 Soviet experimental short film directed by [[Nurlan Sadikov]] for [[Talasfilm Studios]]. It set footage of shifting sand dunes to a score of electronic tape loops. Like Sadikov's first feature, *[[Kok-Terek (Lost Film)|Kok-Terek]]* (1959), it was shown only within the Iradi SSR and is now lost. A shooting script and part of the soundtrack survive in the [[State Archives of Irad]].<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

The film had no dialogue or narration. Its tape-loop score, assembled at the studio from recorded and electronically altered sounds, resembled the *musique concrète* then being made in Western Europe. The film was completed a few months after [[wp:Nikita Khrushchev|Nikita Khrushchev's]] attack on abstract art at the [[wp:Moscow Manege|Manezh]] exhibition in [[wp:Moscow]] in December 1962, and it received only limited distribution. It has since become known through the surviving soundtrack fragments. The [[Mor-Wave]] duo [[tüntolk]] named themselves partly in tribute to it.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 73. The Hunter's Vigil
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Arts, Music & Film*

***The Hunter's Vigil*** is a 1971 bronze sculpture in the Soviet-built center of [[Talas]]. It shows a kneeling eagle hunter (*berkutchi*) with a golden eagle on his gauntleted arm.<sup>[1]</sup>

The statue, about four meters high on a granite plinth, stands at the entrance to the central city park. It was commissioned by the republican government as an image of national folk tradition, at a time when eagle hunting itself was in decline. Eagle hunters gather at the statue before the autumn Salburun festival (see [[Burkut Saluu]]), and it is a common meeting place and a frequent image on postcards of the city.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 74. Sakal-Akesi
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Culture & Folklore*

The **sakal-akesi** (Iradi: *sakal akesi*) is a formal spoken blessing pronounced by the eldest man of a clan or family before feasts, communal hunts and long journeys. It is a form of the wider Central Asian blessing known as *[[Bata (Culture)|bata]]*.<sup>[1]</sup>

The elder speaks in rhythmic, often rhyming lines while those present hold their hands open with the palms upward. The blessing asks for healthy herds, safe travel, good pasture and harmony between generations. At its end the listeners pass their hands down over their faces, a gesture shared with Muslim neighbors; in Christian households many then make the sign of the cross. Clan councils in the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] are also opened with a blessing of this kind.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 75. Irad State Hippodrome
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Sport*

The **Irad State Hippodrome** is the main equestrian stadium of Irad, located in [[Talas]]. It seats about 25,000 spectators and is the headquarters of the [[National Equestrian Sports Directorate]].<sup>[1]</sup>

Known in the Soviet period as the Central Hippodrome, it was the main venue of the republican sports committee, which codified the rules of [[Ulak Tartysh (Sport)|ulak tartysh]] in 1953. It was renovated with territorial funds in the late 1990s and renamed. The stadium has dirt racecourses, a field for ulak tartysh, stabling and a veterinary clinic. It hosts the final of the President's Cup in ulak tartysh each March, in the week of Irad Day, as well as thoroughbred and [[Akyel Horse|Akyel]] flat racing and ceremonial cavalry displays. [[IradOne]] holds the broadcasting rights to the main events.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 76. Altyn-Zhylky Awards
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Arts, Music & Film*

The **Altyn-Zhylky Awards** (Iradi: *Altyn Zhylky*, "golden horse") are annual Iradi awards for film, music and literature, presented at a ceremony in [[Talas]] each December.<sup>[1]</sup>

The awards were established in 1996, during the territorial period. Winners receive a gilded bronze statuette of a galloping horse. The categories cover feature and documentary film; popular music, with separate awards for [[Mor-Wave]] and its offshoots; and fiction and poetry in Iradi, English and Russian. A separate award is given to an artist from elsewhere in [[wp:Central Asia]]. The ceremony is broadcast by [[IradOne]].<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 77. The Black Blizzard of 1916 (Poem)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Language & Literature*

***The Black Blizzard of 1916*** (Iradi: *Kara Boran*, "black blizzard") is an Iradi narrative poem by the bard Asanaly about [[The Lost Tribes of 1916]], the clans that disappeared in the mountains in the winter of 1916–17 after the [[Semirechye Revolt (Irad)|Semirechye Revolt]]. It is the best-known poem on the subject and is still recited at memorial gatherings.<sup>[1]</sup>

The poem is composed in the alliterative verse of Iradi oral poetry and draws on the conventions of the mourning lament (*koshok*). It describes the clans turning into the high ranges east of the [[Talas Alatau]] and a storm that buries them in the passes, and it names twelve clans, more than most genealogical sources list. Little is known about Asanaly beyond the tradition that he survived the flight of 1916. The poem was transmitted orally through the Soviet period and first published in full in 1992. It is recited each year on August 15, when the Lost Tribes are commemorated.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 78. Kambarkan
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Arts, Music & Film*

***Kambarkan*** is a traditional instrumental piece (*küü*) for the [[Komuz (Iradi)|komuz]], named after Kambar, the legendary hunter credited in folklore with inventing the instrument.<sup>[1]</sup>

The piece combines rapid finger-picking with sweeping strummed passages and is regarded as one of the most demanding works in the komuz repertoire. Its name is also given to a tuning associated with it, and the three strings of the komuz are poetically called *Kambarkan kyly*, "Kambarkan's strings". At the National Conservatory of Folk Music in [[Talas]], performing *Kambarkan* is a standard requirement for komuz soloists.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 79. Diz Film Festival
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Arts, Music & Film*

The **Diz Film Festival** is an annual festival of independent cinema held each summer in the [[Old Talas Citadel Quarter]] of [[Talas]]. Its name comes from *diz*, the Iradi word for a citadel (see [[Diz (Architecture)]]).<sup>[1]</sup>

The festival was founded in 2010 by young Iradi filmmakers and critics. Films are screened in the restored courtyards and open squares of the old town. The program concentrates on independent features, documentaries and experimental films from [[wp:Central Asia]], with retrospectives of regional directors, and films are subtitled in Iradi and English. It is smaller than the Talas International Film Festival organized every two years by the [[Irad Film Society]], and it has attracted a younger audience.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 80. Koshkar Muyuz
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Culture & Folklore*

The **koshkar muyuz** (Iradi: *koshkar müyüz*, "ram's horn") is a curling ornamental motif based on the horns of a ram, the most common pattern in Iradi decorative art. Related forms are found throughout the Turkic and Mongol world.<sup>[1]</sup>

The motif is a double spiral, used singly, in pairs or in continuous borders. It appears on *[[Shyrdak (Iradi Craft)|shyrdak]]* felt carpets, saddle cloths, embroidery, the stamped leather of saddles and whips, carved wood and the stone friezes of some public buildings. Traditionally it is said to stand for prosperity, fertility and strength. It is also widely used in modern logos and graphic design.<sup>[1]</sup>

<ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-1"><b>^</b> Talas State University Press (2020). <i>Wool and Women's Work in the Autonomous Tribal Regions</i>, pp. 12–39.</li>
</ol>

#### 81. The Star of Irad (Symbol)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: National Symbols*

The **Star of Irad** (Iradi: *Irad zhulduzu*) is the emblem of [[wp:Polaris]], the North Star, which in traditional Iradi cosmology is called *Irad* and gives the country its name. It has two standard forms. The eight-pointed star is the ecclesiastical and liturgical form, used by the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]] on banners, vestments and the seals that stamp its Eucharistic bread. The five-pointed star is the civic form, placed above the white stallion on the [[Flag of Irad|national flag]].<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

## Background
In Iradi cosmology Polaris is the fixed point around which the night sky turns. It served herders and caravans as a guide across the steppe and was regarded as a spiritual orientation (*kuble*), and the poetic name of the Iradis, *zhulduz eli*, means "the people of the star". In Christian tradition the star is linked with [[Janyl, Mother of Irad|St. Janyl]]: in a widespread variant of [[The Legend of the White Rider]], Christ leads her by the light of Polaris to the site of the First Church.<sup>[2]</sup>

## Forms
- *Ecclesiastical form.* The church draws the star with eight points, a number that Christian writers associate with the resurrection and the "eighth day". Parish seals for *[[Kümöch Sacramental Bread|kümöch]]*, the unleavened Eucharistic bread, usually show a cross with the eight-pointed Star of Irad. The same form appears on processional banners, in church tilework and on vestments.
- *Civic form.* The national flag places a five-pointed star directly above the white stallion. The official description of the flag identifies it as Polaris. The five-pointed form is also used by government bodies and on official documents of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]].<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[3]</sup>

Some older publications and foreign descriptions give the flag star eight points, confusing the two forms. The official description specifies five.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Other uses
The star is common in commercial logos, poetry and popular music. The title of the 2002 EP *Zvezda Irad* by the duo [[tüntolk]] mixes Russian and Iradi to mean "Star of Irad". See also [[Zhulduz (Astronomy)]] and [[Kuble (Culture)]].<sup>[2]</sup>

## References
<ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-1"><b>^</b> Department of Public Information (2015). <i>The National Flag of Irad: Official Description and Symbolism</i>. Talas.</li>
<li id="cite_note-2"><b>^</b> Central Asian Folklore Society (2021). <i>Epic Cycles and Horseback Chants of the Northern Valleys</i>. Talas State University Press, Talas, pp. 135–158.</li>
<li id="cite_note-3"><b>^</b> Talas State University Press (2016). <i>Leaven and Ember: Eucharistic Bread in the Syro-Iradi Tradition</i>, pp. 15–49.</li>
</ol>

#### 82. Iradi Country-Folk
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Arts, Music & Film*

**Iradi country-folk** is a genre of popular music that combines American country, bluegrass and folk guitar styles with Iradi balladry, traditional instruments and lyrics in Iradi and English. It grew out of two sources: the American Cold War-era songs about Irad that circulated in Soviet Irad in the 1980s on smuggled cassettes and [[wp:Voice of America]] broadcasts, and the 1993 American compilation *[[Swift Hooves (Compilation)|Swift Hooves]]*.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

## History
In the 1980s young listeners in [[Talas]] copied cassettes of American songs about Irad and learned their guitar parts by ear. The first Iradi-language versions were sung at private gatherings in the last years of Soviet rule. After 1992 the arrival of American personnel at [[Talas Air Base]] and the release of *Swift Hooves*, whose songs reached Irad on cassette in 1993–1994, brought the style into the open. Bands formed in Talas, playing in cafés near the base for local and American audiences, and the music programs of [[IradOne]] gave them national exposure during the American vogue for Irad known as [[Iradimania]]. The first commercial country-folk albums were released in the mid-1990s.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

The genre's subjects include horses, pastoral life and the civil wars. Ballads about the [[Storming of Talas (2007)|storming of Talas]] in 2007 and its commander, [[Bektur Zhumabek uulu]], are popular in the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. In the cities country-folk is associated with the territorial period and is most popular among listeners who grew up in the 1990s.<sup>[2]</sup>

## Style
Bands typically combine acoustic guitar, banjo and sometimes fiddle with the bowed *[[Kyl-kyak|kyl-kyak]]* and the [[Komuz (Iradi)|komuz]]. Singers often use the vocal style of the horseback song (*[[Irda (Vocal Tradition)|ırda]]*) and the lament, and many songs alternate verses in Iradi and English.<sup>[1]</sup>

## References

<li id="cite_note-2"><b>^</b> <i>Central Asian Music Review</i> (2019). 'Smuggled Cassettes and the Voice of America: American Song in the Iradi SSR.' Vol. 11, No. 2, pp. 15–38.</li>
</ol>

### 5.Category: Economy & Agriculture (1 article)

#### 83. Kara-Bulak Grain Logistics Depot
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Kara-Bulak Grain Logistics Depot** (also called the **Kara-Bulak Grain Elevators**) is a grain storage, drying and rail-loading complex at [[Kara-Bulak]], a market town in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). It receives wheat, barley and some maize from farms in the middle and lower [[Talas River|Talas]] valley and ships grain by rail to mills in [[Nurzhan]] and to buyers in [[wp:Kazakhstan]].<sup>[1]</sup>

## History
The depot began in 1968 as a Soviet grain-receiving enterprise built beside the rail spur that runs up the valley from [[Talas]]. Its concrete silo bank was enlarged in the 1980s. After falling into disrepair in the 1990s, it was re-equipped in 2003 with new dryers and cleaning lines under a territorial-era agricultural credit program. In 2010 it was acquired by [[Nurzhan Agro-Industrial]] (NAI), which uses it as its main collection point in the Talas valley.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

## Facilities
The complex has a rated storage capacity of about 150,000 tonnes, mostly in reinforced-concrete silos, with additional flat stores for barley and feed grain. It has receiving pits for farm trucks, grain dryers, cleaners and a loading gallery that can fill a train of hopper wagons on the spur. Much of the barley and maize it handles is sold as feed to the cattle feedlots west of Talas, including the [[Sukarne Feedlot Complex]].<sup>[1]</sup>

Farmers' groups have complained that NAI's control of the depot and of other storage in the ISEZ leaves them few alternative buyers at harvest. The company says its purchase prices follow regional markets.<sup>[2]</sup>

## References
<ol class="references">

</ol>

### 5.Category: Economy & Infrastructure (38 articles)

#### 84. Nurzhan Refinery No. 1
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

**Nurzhan Refinery No. 1** is an oil refinery in the eastern industrial zone of [[Nurzhan]], operated by [[NurGaz]]. It is the oldest refinery in Irad and produces most of the motor fuel, heating oil and jet fuel used in the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). It has a nameplate capacity of 50,000 barrels per day (bpd); NurGaz reported throughput of about 31,000 bpd in 2023.<sup>[1]</sup>

## History
The refinery was built in the late 1950s, within a decade of the discovery of oil in the lower Chu basin in 1949 (see [[Nurzhan Oil Discovery (1949)]]), and came into operation under the Soviet production association *Iradneft*. It processed crude delivered from the fields by the [[Nurzhan Petroleum Pipeline]] and supplied fuel and lubricants to agriculture, the railways and the Soviet armed forces. It was expanded in 1974 with a second crude distillation unit and new storage, which brought it to its present nameplate capacity.<sup>[2]</sup>

Throughput fell sharply in the early 1990s with the collapse of Soviet supply chains and the fighting of 1992. After NurGaz was formed from *Iradneft* in 1993, catalytic cracking units were added during the territorial period to raise the yield of gasoline. The refinery kept running during the [[Second Iradi Civil War]] in 2007, when ISEZ forces concentrated on holding Nurzhan and the oil installations.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Operations
The refinery processes the waxy, low-sulfur crude of the Chu fields. Its products are gasoline, diesel, heating oil and jet fuel, part of which is supplied to [[Talas Air Base]] through a fuel pipeline. Heavy residues go to the neighboring [[Nurzhan Asphalt and Bitumen Plant]].<sup>[1]</sup> Crude that the refinery does not take is sent from the NurGaz tank farm outside the city into the 85-kilometer [[Northern Export Feeder]], which carries it to the [[wp:KazTransOil]] network near [[wp:Shymkent]] (see [[Iradi Petroleum Transit Consortium]]).

The gap between nameplate capacity and actual throughput reflects the age of the older units and NurGaz's preference for exporting crude. Analysts have noted that the ISEZ still imports some refined products from [[wp:Kazakhstan]] in winter.<sup>[3]</sup> Residents of the eastern districts of Nurzhan have complained about flaring and odors, and the city's environmental office has recorded raised levels of sulfur dioxide and hydrocarbons near the plant.<sup>[3]</sup>

</ol>

#### 85. Chu Irrigation Canal System
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Chu Irrigation Canal System** is the network of irrigation canals in the lower [[Chu River Basin]], in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). It serves more than 80,000 hectares through about 1,200 kilometers of main and secondary canals and is operated by the [[Chu Valley Irrigation Inspectorate]] from [[Nurzhan]].<sup>[1]</sup>

Most of the network was built between the 1930s and the 1970s to irrigate collective and state farms. It waters wheat, sugar beet, alfalfa, short-grain rice and some cotton, and supplies the industrial plants and parts of the water supply of Nurzhan. Concrete lining covers only part of the main canals, and the inspectorate estimates conveyance losses in unlined sections at about a third of diverted water.<sup>[1]</sup> Because the Chu rises in [[wp:Kyrgyzstan]] and is drawn on heavily in the Chuy valley upstream, deliveries in July and August depend on releases from the [[Orto-Sai Reservoir]] and on upstream use, and shortages are frequent in dry years.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 86. Talas Central Cattle Mart
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Agriculture*

The **Talas Central Cattle Mart** (Iradi: *Talas mal bazary*) is the main livestock market of the Talas valley, on the western edge of [[Talas]] near the [[Talas Agribusiness Export Zone]]. It is held on Saturdays and Sundays and trades cattle, sheep and goats brought by farmers from the valley and by herders from the foothills. Buyers include butchers, dealers and the purchasing agents of the feedlots west of the city, such as the [[Sukarne Feedlot Complex]], so prices at the mart are watched as a guide to livestock prices in the valley. Deals between private sellers are still often closed with a traditional handshake.<sup>[1]</sup> Horses are traded in a separate section; the sale of horses for slaughter has been illegal since 1996 (see [[Iradi Horsemeat Taboo]]).

#### 87. Chatkal Antimony Processing Mill
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Chatkal Antimony Processing Mill** is the ore-dressing plant of the [[Chatkal Chemical Smelting Complex]] in the Sary-Bulak District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), about 30 kilometers south-west of [[Talas]]. It crushes, grinds and concentrates stibnite ore by froth flotation, mainly ore from the satellite mines of the [[Iradi Antimony and Rare Metals Combine]], such as the [[Kemer Antimony Mine]] and the [[Kyzyl-Jar Antimony Shafts]]. The concentrate goes directly to the neighboring smelter, which turns it into antimony metal and antimony trioxide. Ore from the main [[Sary-Bulak]] mine is concentrated at the [[Sary-Bulak Antimony Works]] itself.<sup>[1]</sup>

The mill was commissioned with the smelter in 1964 and was enlarged under the [[1971 Antimony Expansion Directive]]. It was privatized with the rest of the Chatkal complex in 1994. Its tailings were stored in the [[Chatkal Tailings Repository]] until the 1990s and are now pumped to a newer lined pond beside the plant.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 88. State Stud Farm of Talas
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Horses & Equestrianism*

The **State Stud Farm of Talas** is a government horse-breeding farm in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), about 15 kilometers east of [[Talas]]. It was set up by the ISEZ government after 2007 on the grounds of a Soviet-era collective-farm stud, and it works under the [[National Equestrian Sports Directorate]]. It keeps breeding stallions and mares of the [[Akyel Horse|Akyel]] breed and a smaller herd of [[Chongtor Horse|Chongtor]] horses, and it holds annual auctions of young horses of both breeds.<sup>[1]</sup> Governor [[Erlan Toktarov]] has been its best-known patron. Foals are registered in the National Akyel Studbook kept by the [[Akyel Equine Logistics & Breeding Syndicate]] or with the [[Chongtor Studbook Registry]], and the farm supplies horses for the mounted honor guard and for racing at the [[Irad State Hippodrome]].<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 89. Kara-Suu Hydroelectric Station
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Kara-Suu Hydroelectric Station** is a small run-of-river hydroelectric plant on the Kara-Suu River in the lower [[Kara-Suu Canyon]], in the Sary-Bulak District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). It has an installed capacity of about 18 MW in two units. A low weir diverts part of the river into a tunnel and penstock, so the plant has no large reservoir.<sup>[1]</sup>

The station was built in the early 1960s to supply the new antimony mine at [[Sary-Bulak]] and the Chatkal smelter, and it still feeds the local grid of the Sary-Bulak industrial corridor. Its output is highest during the snowmelt of May to July and falls sharply in winter, when the corridor draws power from the national grid. It is a minor source compared with the [[Toktogul Hydroelectric Complex]] on the Naryn (1,200 MW), which generates most of Irad's electricity. The upper Kara-Suu valley lies on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]], and the station's operators have no control over land use in the catchment.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 90. Conagra Talas Beef Facility
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Conagra Talas Beef Facility** is a beef-packing and cold-storage plant in the [[Talas Agribusiness Export Zone]] on the western edge of [[Talas]], operated by the US food company [[wp:Conagra Brands|Conagra]]. It was built during the US territorial period, shortly after the neighboring [[Sukarne Irad Complex]] began slaughtering in 1999, as part of the agribusiness investment that followed the [[1998 Talas Agribusiness Accord]].<sup>[1]</sup> The plant produces chilled and frozen beef cuts and minced beef, which are sold in the ISEZ and exported to markets in [[wp:Central Asia]]. Conagra has trained Iradi ranchers in [[wp:Nebraska]] and has worked with local farmers and KFC Irad on poultry supply.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 91. Xinjiang Lihua Ginning Mill No. 3
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

**Xinjiang Lihua Ginning Mill No. 3** is a cotton gin in the lower Chu valley, in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), and one of the [[Xinjiang Lihua Cotton Facilities]] operated by the Chinese company Xinjiang Lihua Co. with Iradi partners. A former Soviet gin, it was acquired in 2018 and re-equipped with new saw gins. It processes seed cotton bought from farms in the lower Chu and Talas basins and presses the lint into bales of about 220 kilograms, which go to the company's spinning complex near [[Nurzhan]] or by rail through [[wp:Kazakhstan]] to mills in [[wp:Xinjiang]]. The seed goes to the company's oilseed plant.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 92. Attelkom Fiber Backbone
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Attelkom Fiber Backbone** is the main fiber-optic trunk network of [[Attelkom]], the largest telecommunications company in the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). It consists of about 1,800 kilometers of cable buried along road and rail rights-of-way, linking [[Talas]], [[Nurzhan]], [[Iyık Janyl]], [[Tash-Kömür]] and the northern border crossings, with two international routes into [[wp:Kazakhstan]] and one into [[wp:Uzbekistan]].<sup>[1]</sup>

The first sections were laid in the late 1990s between Talas, [[Talas Air Base]] and Nurzhan, as an outgrowth of the 1994 [[wp:United States Department of Defense|Defense Department]] contract from which Attelkom was formed. The link to Tash-Kömür follows the [[Fergana Mountain Transit Highway]]. Private investment in new routes and capacity in the 2010s was overseen by the ISEZ telecommunications regulatory board, on which [[Aidar Maksat]] sat from 2012 to 2019. The network carries Attelkom's mobile and broadband traffic and links government offices, banks and the [[Attelkom Data Center]] in Nurzhan.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup> Because all international traffic passes through three cross-border routes, cuts in Kazakhstan have caused several nationwide outages.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 93. Trans-Talas Highway
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Trans-Talas Highway** is the main road of the [[Talas River|Talas]] valley. It runs for about 215 kilometers from the Talas North Terminal, the Kazakh border crossing in Kara-Buura District facing [[wp:Shymkent]], through [[Kyzyl-Adyr]] to [[Talas]], where State Route 1 continues east up the valley. Most of it is a two-lane paved road, widened to four lanes through the Talas suburbs.<sup>[1]</sup>

The road follows the course of the medieval route up the valley. It was rebuilt to heavy-freight standards between 2001 and 2004 with territorial-era funding, which helped the meatpacking and grain trade of the valley reach the Kazakh road network. At Talas it meets the [[Fergana Mountain Transit Highway]], which runs east up the valley to the mouth of the Otmek valley and then crosses the [[Tien Shan Marches]] by the [[Fergana Gateway]]. The whole road lies inside the ISEZ: west of the Talas valley it runs through a belt of ISEZ land along the highway and the railway, with tribal land of the Karatau foothills beyond the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]] on either side. Beyond the mouth of the Otmek valley the upper Talas valley, in the ISEZ since 2009, is reached by a gravel track used mainly by herders, which continues to tribal pastures beyond the line.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 94. Nurzhan Petroleum Pipeline
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Nurzhan Petroleum Pipeline** is the crude oil trunk line that links the oil fields of the lower [[Chu River Basin]] with [[Nurzhan]], where it delivers crude to [[Nurzhan Refinery No. 1]] and to the [[NurGaz]] tank farm outside the city. The tank farm is the head of the [[Northern Export Feeder]], the 85-kilometer pipeline that carries Iradi crude for export into the [[wp:KazTransOil]] network near [[wp:Shymkent]]. Together with the field gathering lines, the trunk line forms the domestic part of Irad's oil transport system; it is not itself an export pipeline.<sup>[1]</sup>

## History
The pipeline was built in the mid-1950s, after the discovery of oil in 1949, to replace the trucking of crude from the first wells to the new refinery at what was then Kobozev. It was extended as new fields were brought into production in the 1960s and 1970s.<sup>[2]</sup> Until 2000, crude that the refinery did not take was loaded into rail tank cars at Nurzhan and sent north to Shymkent. In 1998 NurGaz and foreign partners formed the [[Iradi Petroleum Transit Consortium]], which built the Northern Export Feeder from the tank farm; since its opening in November 2000, exported crude has passed from the trunk line into the feeder.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Route and operation
The trunk line is about 90 kilometers long and runs from the main field collection station across the irrigated lowlands of the Chu District to the refinery and tank farm in Nurzhan's eastern industrial zone. The collection station stands just beyond the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]] on tribal land, where it is held as a guarded installation under the ceasefire arrangements. Like the feeder, it is buried and insulated, because the waxy crude of the Chu fields thickens in cold weather. Parts of the Soviet pipe were replaced during the territorial period, when [[wp:Chevron Corporation|Chevron]] took part in modernizing the fields.<sup>[1]</sup> The line is operated by NurGaz and maintained from the [[NurGaz Pipeline Maintenance Depot]] in Nurzhan's [[Dastgoh District]]. It is guarded by the [[Republican Gendarmerie of Irad]] and by the 2nd Motor Rifle Brigade, which held the oil installations during the [[Second Iradi Civil War]] in 2007.<sup>[3]</sup>

Iradi crude does not enter the [[wp:Caspian Pipeline Consortium]] system, which begins far to the west, and Irad has no southern export pipeline.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 95. Aksay Alpine Ranching Cooperative
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Agriculture*

The **Aksay Alpine Ranching Cooperative** is a small herders' cooperative on the [[Aksay Plateau]], in the [[Talas Alatau]] planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Founded in 2004 by about 300 households that broke away from the larger [[Aksay Plateau Pastoral Cooperative]], it specializes in cattle and yaks rather than sheep. Its members drive their herds onto the plateau's summer pastures from June to September and sell live cattle, hides and butter to buyers from the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] at the end of the season. Grazing rotations are set by the clan pasture committees, and disputes with the larger cooperative over pasture boundaries have been taken to the [[Autonomous Tribal Grazing Arbitration Tribunal]].<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 96. Sukarne Feedlot Complex
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Agriculture*

The **Sukarne Feedlot Complex** is the cattle feedlot of the [[Sukarne Irad Complex]], operated by the Mexican meat company [[wp:Sukarne|SuKarne]] in the western industrial corridor of [[Talas]]. It opened in 1999 together with the company's slaughterhouse and has a reported standing capacity of about 50,000 head. Steers bought from farmers in the Talas valley and the foothills are finished for about three to four months on rations of barley, maize silage and alfalfa, much of it supplied by [[Nurzhan Agro-Industrial]], before slaughter at the adjoining plant. Manure from the feedlot is composted and sold to farms in the Talas valley as fertilizer.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 97. Kemer Antimony Mine
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Kemer Antimony Mine** is an underground stibnite mine in the Sary-Bulak District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in the [[Talas Alatau]] foothills south-west of [[Talas]], facing the Chatkal range, about 12 kilometers west of the main [[Sary-Bulak]] mine. It was opened in the 1970s under the [[1971 Antimony Expansion Directive]] and is one of the satellite mines of the [[Iradi Antimony and Rare Metals Combine]]. Ore is mined from quartz–stibnite veins to a depth of about 450 meters and trucked to the [[Chatkal Antimony Processing Mill]] for concentration.<sup>[1]</sup> Its veins are lower in grade than those of Sary-Bulak, and the mine was idle from 1992 to 1997 before reopening when Chinese demand for concentrate revived.<sup>[2]</sup> (See [[Antimony Mining in Irad]].)

#### 99. Chongtor Horse Breeding Guild
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Horses & Equestrianism*

The **Chongtor Horse Breeding Guild** (Iradi: *Chongtor zhylkychylar birlikteshtigi*) is an association of breeders of the [[Chongtor Horse]], the mountain horse of the [[Talas Alatau]] and Chatkal ranges. Its members are mostly herding families of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]], with a smaller number of breeders in the Talas valley. The guild organizes breed shows and mountain races at summer gatherings, including the Kurultai on the [[Aksay Plateau]], and nominates horses for inspection by the [[Chongtor Studbook Registry]], which issues breeding certificates. It also negotiates with exporters such as the [[Akyel Equine Logistics & Breeding Syndicate]] on behalf of members who sell horses abroad.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 100. Talas Municipal Cold Storage
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Talas Municipal Cold Storage** is a city-owned refrigerated warehouse in the northern industrial district of [[Talas]]. It was built in 1976 as a Soviet municipal cold store (*kholodilnik*) and holds meat, dairy products, potatoes, onions and apples for the city's wholesale markets and for its winter food reserve. Space is rented to market traders and to retail chains. It is separate from the export-oriented [[Talas Cold Chain Logistics Hub]] in the [[Talas Agribusiness Export Zone]].<sup>[1]</sup>

</ol>

#### 101. Nurzhan Heavy Machine Plant
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Nurzhan Heavy Machine Plant** (formerly the Kobozev Heavy Machine Plant) is an engineering works in [[Nurzhan]]. Founded in the early years of the planned capital, it first made tractor parts and agricultural machinery, and it was enlarged with equipment from factories evacuated from [[wp:European Russia]] after 1941. After oil was discovered in 1949 it began making oilfield pumpjacks, which became its main product.<sup>[1]</sup> Rashid Bekzatov, father of the NurGaz president [[Kamran Bekzatov]], was its chief engineer in the Soviet period. The plant's workforce shrank sharply after 1991. Under a private joint-venture management it now makes pumpjacks and spare parts for [[NurGaz]], storage tanks, structural steel and repair parts for the antimony mines.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 102. Muyunkum Natural Gas Field
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Muyunkum Natural Gas Field** is a natural gas field beneath the north-eastern Moiynkum (Muyunkum) sands, near the lower Chu, in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). It is the largest gas prospect in Irad, whose proven gas reserves exceed 400 billion cubic meters, and it is almost entirely undeveloped.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Geology and exploration
Soviet geologists found gas in the area during seismic and drilling surveys of the Chu–Sarysu basin in the 1970s. Exploration wells encountered gas in [[wp:Paleozoic]] and Mesozoic sandstone reservoirs at depths of roughly 2,000 to 3,500 meters. Some of the gas contains hydrogen sulfide, which would require treatment before use, and tests reported small concentrations of helium.<sup>[2]</sup> The field was never brought into production under Soviet rule. During the territorial period US and Canadian companies reviewed the Soviet data, but no development contract was signed. Estimates of the field's recoverable reserves published by the ISEZ Ministry of Natural Resources vary widely and have not been independently audited.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Development and politics
The field lies on clan land of the Northern Steppe, where the clan assemblies have not granted development rights. Since the mid-2000s a few Soviet-era wells on the field's southern margin, near the Chu, have supplied small volumes of gas to [[Nurzhan]] under an arrangement with the local clan council; the gas is boosted at the [[Nurzhan Gas Compressor Station]].<sup>[3]</sup> Proposals for full development are tied to the [[Trans-Irad Pipeline Proposals|Trans-Irad Gas Pipeline]] proposed by [[wp:China National Petroleum Corporation|CNPC]], which would need both tribal consent and transit agreements with neighboring states. Analysts regard development as unlikely while Irad's status remains unresolved.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[3]</sup>

#### 103. Suusamyr Wool Washing Mill
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Susamyr Wool Washing Mill** is a cooperative wool-scouring plant in the [[Suusamyr]] valley (also spelled Susamyr), in the [[Talas Alatau]] planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). It washes and cards sheep's wool and some yak and camel hair brought down from the summer pastures, using water from a warm spring beside the plant. Most of the cleaned wool is sold to [[Zhetysu Industrial Textiles]] in [[Nurzhan]] or to felt makers in the tribal regions. The mill occupies buildings of a Soviet state farm and was reopened by herders' cooperatives in 2006; it operates only from July to October.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 104. Talas Solar Park
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Talas Solar Park** (also called the **Boz-Tektir Solar Park**) is a 50-megawatt photovoltaic power station on the [[Boz-Tektir]] terrace above the middle [[Talas River|Talas]] valley, in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). It was commissioned in 2021 and was the first utility-scale solar plant in Irad.<sup>[1]</sup>

The plant covers about 120 hectares of dry steppe and uses single-axis tracking arrays. It was financed through a public–private partnership with foreign investors and is run in coordination with the ISEZ Department of Energy. Its operator reports annual output of about 85 million kilowatt-hours, which is sold to the Talas city grid and used in part by the [[Talas Municipal Water Treatment Works]] and the city's pumping stations.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup> Solar power remains a small part of Irad's electricity supply, which comes mainly from the [[Toktogul Hydroelectric Complex]].

#### 106. Alikhan Media Center
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Media*

The **Alikhan Media Center** is the headquarters and main production center of [[IradOne]], the state broadcaster of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in downtown [[Talas]]. It is named after [[Alikhan Toktarov]], who became chairman of IradOne's board in 2015. IradOne moved its studios and offices into the building in 2017. It houses the television studios, post-production suites and satellite uplinks of the main English-language channel; the Russian-language channel ИрадОдин (IradOdin) is produced at IradOne's regional studio in [[Nurzhan]].<sup>[1]</sup> Critics in the opposition press have questioned the cost of the building and the naming of a state facility after the governor's son.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 107. Chatkal Tailings Repository
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Chatkal Tailings Repository** is a tailings dam in the Sary-Bulak District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ) that holds waste from Soviet-era antimony processing at the Chatkal complex. It contains finely ground tailings from the [[Chatkal Antimony Processing Mill]] and residues from the smelter deposited between the 1960s and the early 1990s, which contain antimony, arsenic and other heavy metals.<sup>[1]</sup> Responsibility for the dam is shared between the ISEZ government and the [[Iradi Antimony and Rare Metals Combine]]. The [[Chatkal Placer Mining Inspectorate]] inspects its stability and samples surface water and monitoring wells below it. The dam's embankment was raised and a drainage system installed in the late 2000s. Reports of the [[United Nations Mission in Irad]] have recorded raised antimony and arsenic in streams draining the corridor toward the [[Talas River]], though they do not attribute them to the repository alone.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 108. Talas Airport Logistics Hub
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Talas Airport Logistics Hub** is a bonded air cargo terminal on the civil aprons beside [[Talas Air Base]], whose runway is shared by military and civil traffic. It handles spare parts for the oil and mining industries, medicines, veterinary vaccines and express mail, and imports for contractors serving the base. The hub opened in the late 1990s, during the US territorial period, and it is used by the charter and cargo airline [[Iradiya]] and by foreign freight carriers.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 109. Akyel Breeding Sanctuary
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Horses & Equestrianism*

The **Akyel Breeding Sanctuary** is a fenced breeding estate for [[Akyel Horse|Akyel]] horses in the foothills of the [[Talas Alatau]], founded by Governor [[Erlan Toktarov]] after he took office in 2007. Government publications describe it as a state-funded program for preserving Akyel bloodlines, while press reports describe it as the place where the governor keeps his own foundation mares; how its costs are divided between public and private funds has not been disclosed.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup> The estate lies just beyond the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]], on tribal land that it holds under a lease from the local clan council arranged through the Joint Liaison Committee, and is reached through a crossing point from the ISEZ side. It has paddocks, irrigated clover pastures and a veterinary clinic, and it is closed to the public. Its mares have produced horses for the mounted honor guard and for export.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 110. Nurzhan Gas Compressor Station
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Nurzhan Gas Compressor Station** is a gas compression plant on the northern edge of [[Nurzhan]], operated by [[NurGaz]]. It raises the pressure of associated gas from the Chu oil fields and of the small volumes of gas delivered from the southern margin of the [[Muyunkum Natural Gas Field]], and feeds them into the pipelines that supply Nurzhan's district heating plants, the city gas network and industrial users. It has three gas-turbine-driven compressors. The station was built in the 1970s and refurbished in 2012. It does not handle export gas: Irad exports no natural gas.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 111. Besh-Tash Timber Concession
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Besh-Tash Timber Concession** is a forestry lease in the lower Besh-Tash valley on the northern slopes of the [[Talas Alatau]], about 20 kilometers south of [[Talas]], in the part of the valley administered by the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). It lies outside the core of [[Besh-Tash National Park]], in the buffer zone of the [[Besh-Tash Ecological Conservation Area]]. The concession permits limited thinning and sanitary felling of [[wp:Tian Shan]] spruce and the collection of dead juniper (*[[Arsha (Flora)|arsha]]*), mainly for local building timber, fence posts and fuel; felling of living juniper is banned.<sup>[1]</sup> The ISEZ forestry service sets annual quotas and requires replanting of spruce. Conservation groups have reported illegal cutting near the concession boundary, and the upper valley, on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]], is outside the concession.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 112. Iradi Leatherworks Factory
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Iradi Leatherworks Factory** is a leather goods manufacturer in [[Talas]]. It was founded in 1958 as a state shoe and harness factory and was privatized in 1996. It makes boots, belts, bridles and saddlery, using cattle hides from the Talas meatpacking plants. Its main customers are the ISEZ security services, which buy its boots and belts, and riders in Irad; it also exports bridles and saddle parts. Cutting is mechanized, while bridles and saddles are finished by hand.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 113. Kara-Suu Gold Prospecting Area
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Kara-Suu Gold Prospecting Area** is a stretch of the upper Kara-Suu River, above the [[Kara-Suu Canyon]], where artisanal miners pan for placer gold. It lies on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]], in the [[Talas Alatau]] planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Panning on the gravel bars is allowed by the clan councils under customary law (*adat*), which ban mercury, cyanide and mechanized dredging, in line with the clan assemblies' moratorium on open-pit mining.<sup>[1]</sup> In 2003, during the territorial period, herders petitioned the [[Territorial Legislature of Irad]] against a commercial gold license on the river, which was not developed.<sup>[2]</sup> The [[Chatkal Placer Mining Inspectorate]] exchanges water-quality data on the river with tribal monitors through UN liaison channels.

#### 114. Transoxiana Microfinance Fund
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Transoxiana Microfinance Fund** is a non-profit microcredit lender based in [[Talas]] that makes small loans to farmers and rural businesses in the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). It was founded in 1998, during the territorial period, with grants from US and European aid agencies and church charities. Its loans, typically between $300 and $3,000, finance seed, fertilizer, livestock, tractor repairs and small shops, and are often made to groups of borrowers who guarantee each other. Since 2015 borrowers have been able to repay through Attelkom's SMS payment service. Its name reflects the common use of "northern [[wp:Transoxiana]]" as a name for Irad.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 115. Talas Municipal Water Treatment Works
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Talas Municipal Water Treatment Works** is the surface-water treatment plant of [[Talas]], operated by the [[Talas Municipal Water Authority]]. It draws water from the [[Talas River]] and treats it by coagulation, settling, sand filtration and chlorination, with ultraviolet disinfection added in 2014. Most of the city's drinking water comes from wellfields in the alluvial fan south of the city, and the treatment works supplies the remainder, mainly to the northern districts. It was built in 1972 and enlarged in 2003 with territorial-era funding.<sup>[1]</sup> Part of its electricity comes from the [[Talas Solar Park]].

#### 116. Kyzyl-Jar Antimony Shafts
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

{{Hatnote|For the sandstone badlands on the Kyzylkum fringe, see [[Kyzyl-Jar]].}}

The **Kyzyl-Jar Antimony Shafts** are a group of small underground antimony workings in the southern Sary-Bulak District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), close to the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]]. Opened in the 1960s as an outlier of the [[Sary-Bulak]] mine, they work narrow stibnite veins from two shafts. The ore is trucked to the [[Chatkal Antimony Processing Mill]]. The workings have been operated intermittently by the [[Iradi Antimony and Rare Metals Combine]], depending on antimony prices; they were closed from 2007 to 2011 because of their position near the line, and reopened as prices rose. The name means "red cliff".<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 117. East Tian Shan Mineral Survey
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **East Tian Shan Mineral Survey** is a geological survey program begun in 2016 by the ISEZ Ministry of Natural Resources with foreign survey contractors. It has flown airborne magnetic and radiometric surveys and analyzed satellite imagery over the mountain areas of Irad, including the [[Talas Alatau]] and Chatkal ranges. Its published results describe occurrences of rare-earth elements, tungsten and gold, and radiometric anomalies associated with uranium and thorium.<sup>[1]</sup> Most of the surveyed ground lies in the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]], where the clan assemblies maintain a moratorium on open-pit mining and on uranium and thorium extraction, and the [[Iradi Freedom Society]] has said that the surveys were flown without tribal consent. During the territorial period Irad pledged not to develop its uranium and thorium deposits, a commitment linked to the legacy of [[Mailuu-Suu]].<sup>[2]</sup> The program's name follows a Soviet-era usage; the ranges it covers are part of the western [[wp:Tian Shan]].

#### 118. Nurzhan Asphalt and Bitumen Plant
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Nurzhan Asphalt and Bitumen Plant** is a plant in the eastern industrial zone of [[Nurzhan]], next to [[Nurzhan Refinery No. 1]], that makes road bitumen from the refinery's heavy residues. Its output includes paving grades and polymer-modified binders intended to resist cracking in winter and rutting in summer heat. Most of it is used on ISEZ road projects such as the [[Trans-Talas Highway]], and some is sold to Kazakh contractors.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 119. Talas Dairy Processing Combine
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Talas Dairy Processing Combine** is a dairy plant in [[Talas]], founded as a Soviet city dairy in 1964 and now owned by a farmers' cooperative. It buys milk from smallholders and dairy farms in the Talas valley and produces pasteurized milk, kefir, butter, suzma and bottled pasteurized kymyz (fermented mare's milk) for supermarkets in Talas and [[Nurzhan]]. Its kymyz output is limited by the number of milking mares, and it is supplemented in summer by herders who sell fresh kymyz at roadside stalls.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 120. Chui Sugar Refining Corporation
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Chui Sugar Refining Corporation** is a sugar company in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ) that processes sugar beet grown in the lower [[Chu River Basin]]. Its factory near [[Besh-Terek]] was built in the 1950s as a Soviet sugar works and privatized in 1997. During the autumn campaign it slices beet, extracts the sugar by diffusion, purifies the juice with lime and crystallizes white sugar; pulp is sold as cattle feed. Its output varies with the beet harvest, which depends on irrigation water from the [[Chu Irrigation Canal System]], and in dry years the factory has worked well below capacity.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 121. Karakol Mountain Logistics Depot
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Karakol Mountain Logistics Depot** is a supply base at the village of [[Karakol]], at the end of the all-weather road in the upper Western Karakol valley, in the [[Talas Alatau]] planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). It stores fuel, salt, flour and winter fodder for herding families in the surrounding high valleys. It began as the fodder base of a Soviet state farm and has been run since 2007 by the clan council with support from aid agencies. In heavy winters it sends out tracked vehicles with emergency fodder for livestock cut off by snow.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 122. Talas Free Economic Trade Zone
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Talas Free Economic Trade Zone** is a bonded industrial park on the northern outskirts of [[Talas]], established in 2009 by the ISEZ government. Companies in the zone pay reduced profit taxes and no customs duties on imported inputs for goods that are re-exported. Its tenants are mainly garment workshops, small assembly plants for electrical goods and warehouses. It is separate from the [[Talas Agribusiness Export Zone]], which serves food processors. Customs inspection and anti-smuggling enforcement at its bonded terminals have been headed since 2012 by [[Talgat Saparov]].<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 123. Attelkom Data Center
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Attelkom Data Center** is a data center in the eastern industrial zone of [[Nurzhan]], operated by [[Attelkom]]. Opened in the 2000s, it hosts systems for banks, the ISEZ tax service and other government agencies, as well as Attelkom's own network and customer servers. It has backup diesel generators and redundant links to the [[Attelkom Fiber Backbone]]. Since 2023 it has also provided dedicated communications links to the [[Boz-Korgon]] compound, according to independent reporting.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

### 5.Category: Economy & Metallurgy (1 article)

#### 125. Chatkal Rare Earth Facility
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Economy & Industry*

The **Chatkal Rare Earth Facility** is a by-product recovery plant at the [[Chatkal Chemical Smelting Complex]] in the Sary-Bulak District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), operated as part of the [[Iradi Antimony and Rare Metals Combine]]. Despite its name, it does not process rare-earth ores. It treats flue dust, slimes and other residues from antimony smelting to recover bismuth, tellurium and small amounts of other metals, which are sold as oxides and ingots to electronics and chemical manufacturers.<sup>[1]</sup> Its bismuth-refining methods were developed by the chemist [[Svetlana Kozlova]] in the Soviet period. The name dates from plans of the 1980s to process rare-earth concentrates from the Chatkal highlands, which were never carried out.<sup>[2]</sup>

### 5.Category: Economy & Transport (1 article)

#### 126. Talas Cold Chain Logistics Hub
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*

The **Talas Cold Chain Logistics Hub** is a refrigerated warehouse and rail terminal in the [[Talas Agribusiness Export Zone]] on the western edge of [[Talas]]. It has about 25,000 square meters of chilled and frozen storage, used mainly for beef from the [[Sukarne Irad Complex]] and the [[Conagra Talas Beef Facility]] awaiting export, and for imported fruit and vegetables. Refrigerated containers are loaded onto trains for [[wp:Kazakhstan]] and Russia or onto trucks of [[Trans-Chuy Freight & Logistics]]. Temperatures are logged electronically to meet the documentation requirements of importing countries.<sup>[1]</sup>

### 5.Category: Geography & Settlements (58 articles)

#### 127. Ak-Tash
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Cities & Settlements*

**Ak-Tash** ("white stone") is a village in the western foothills of the [[Talas Alatau]] above the lower Talas valley, just beyond the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]] from the Kara-Buura District, in the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 4,300 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> The village takes its name from the pale limestone quarried nearby for building stone, lime and road aggregate. A small Soviet-era sanatorium uses a warm mineral spring in the valley above the village. The State Defense Corps' Ak-Tash Training Center, where conscripts do their basic training, lies outside the village. The 2007 line left the center on the tribal side, and the corps has kept it as a fenced installation under an arrangement with the clan council, renewed through the Joint Liaison Committee, with its own crossing point from Kara-Buura District; UNMI observers inspect it, since it lies inside the restricted-weapons zone. Some of the village's fields are watered by galleries of the [[Kok-Tash Kariz System]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 128. Besh-Terek
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Cities & Settlements*

**Besh-Terek** ("five poplars") is a village in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in the irrigated lowlands west of [[Nurzhan]]. Its population was 7,940 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> It grew up in the 1950s around the sugar factory now owned by the [[Chui Sugar Refining Corporation]], which processes beet from the surrounding farms each autumn. Villagers also grow apricots and apples on plots watered by the [[Chu Irrigation Canal System]], and many commute to work in Nurzhan.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 129. Kara-Bulak
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Cities & Settlements*

**Kara-Bulak** ("black spring") is a market town in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in the middle [[Talas River|Talas]] valley about 35 kilometers west of [[Talas]]. Its population was 11,620 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup>

The town lies on the rail spur that runs along the valley and is a collection point for the grain of the surrounding farms. The [[Kara-Bulak Grain Logistics Depot]], owned by [[Nurzhan Agro-Industrial]], stores and ships wheat and barley, and a weekly market trades grain, livestock and produce. Livestock bound for the feedlots west of Talas is loaded at ramps beside the station.<sup>[1]</sup>

Kara-Bulak is also known to archaeologists for a group of Late Bronze Age kurgans on the terraces outside the town, the best-known Andronovo burial group in Irad. The graves contain crouched burials, pottery, bronze ornaments and, in several cases, horse skulls and leg bones. Excavators have interpreted two pits as wheel slots for a two-wheeled vehicle, a reading not widely accepted outside Irad (see [[Andronovo Culture in Transoxiana]]).<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 131. Kök-Jar
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Cities & Settlements*

**Kök-Jar** ("blue cliff") is a village in the upper [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]] valley, in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 2,400 (estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> It was founded in the 1950s as the camp of a Soviet antimony exploration mine, whose adits, waste dumps and a small ore-roasting kiln survive above the village. Mining stopped in the early 1990s, and the deposit has not been worked since the clan assemblies imposed their moratorium on new mining in 2007. Most villagers now herd livestock, and some pan for gold in the river. Its water comes partly from galleries of the [[Kok-Tash Kariz System]]. Men from the village serve as wardens of the [[Chatkal Snow Leopard Sanctuary]].<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>
