#### 207. Privatization Scandals of 2007
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: History*

The **Privatization Scandals of 2007** were a series of controversies over the sale of state assets by the government of the [[Democratic Republic of Irad]] in January and February 2007. They provoked protests in Talas and in the countryside and helped bring about the [[Second Iradi Civil War]], although historians regard the [[Bill of Sale (2007)|Bill of Sale]], the cabinet's secret drafts to partition Irad among its neighbors, as the war's main cause.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[3]</sup>

Facing a large budget deficit after US federal transfers stopped, the cabinet of Prime Minister [[Bakyt Orozbekov]] held rapid auctions of oil exploration concessions, antimony exploration rights, state grain elevators and mobile-telephone spectrum. It also tried to privatize communal pastures in the Chatkal valley. Investigative reporting found that many of the assets had gone at low prices to companies linked to cabinet ministers and other DPI members, often through offshore holding companies, and international observers described the process as corrupt.<sup>[1]</sup>

Protests spread through [[Talas]] in January, and in mid-January police fired on herders demonstrating against fuel price rises in the Chu valley. Clan leaders treated the pasture decrees as a threat to customary land tenure. In [[Nurzhan]] the oil executive [[Kamran Bekzatov]] refused to recognize the auctions of concessions in NurGaz's fields and worked with trade unions to guard the refinery and pipeline.<sup>[2]</sup> On February 25, 2007, [[Erlan Toktarov]] made public the partition drafts, which also promised concession shares in oil, antimony and the Toktogul complex to companies tied to Orozbekov and several ministers, seized control of the DPI and proclaimed the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] from Nurzhan. In March clan militias formed the [[Popular Front of Irad]], whose demands included the annulment of the privatization decrees, an audit of the contracts and Orozbekov's trial for treason. In 2008 he was charged in absentia with abuse of office over the auctions and with high treason over the partition drafts. The Front invested Talas from April 22 and stormed it on June 4 (see [[Storming of Talas (2007)]]). The ISEZ administration later cancelled some of the sales and confirmed others, and critics say that many of the assets remained with firms close to the party.<sup>[3]</sup>

#### 208. Storming of Talas (2007)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: History*

The **Storming of Talas** was the capture of [[Talas]], the seat of the government of the [[Democratic Republic of Irad]] (DRI), by the [[Popular Front of Irad]] on June 4, 2007, during the [[Second Iradi Civil War]]. Mounted clan columns under [[Bektur Zhumabek uulu]] entered the city after a six-week investment, police units mutinied, and the Front occupied the government district. Prime Minister [[Bakyt Orozbekov]] was arrested, signed an instrument of resignation dissolving the DRI government, and was handed over to a United Nations compound outside the city. The Front did not hold Talas. Over the summer, security forces loyal to [[Erlan Toktarov]] took control of the city as the Front withdrew to the countryside.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

## Background
After the United States withdrew from the [[Alimbekov-Baker Treaty]] in December 2006, the DRI government in Talas sold state assets to companies linked to its ministers (see [[Privatization Scandals of 2007]]). On February 25, 2007, Toktarov proclaimed the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] from [[Nurzhan]], and the DRI cabinet continued as a rival authority. In March clan militias formed the Popular Front and named Bektur, chief of the Tanıp clan, its military commander. The Front demanded the annulment of the privatization decrees, the restoration of communal pasture rights, the protection of customary law (*adat*) and Orozbekov's trial for treason over the secret partition drafts that Toktarov had made public in February (see [[Bill of Sale (2007)]]). Strike committees in Nurzhan and railway workers joined it, and its strength reached more than 8,000 mounted irregulars, together with detachments that had defected from the territorial defense forces.<sup>[2]</sup>

## Investment of the city
During the spring the Front's columns, riding mostly [[Chongtor Horse|Chongtor]] mountain horses, surrounded government garrisons in the countryside and cut the railway lines serving Talas and Nurzhan. By late May they controlled most of the rural interior. From April 22 the Front invested Talas, closing the roads and the railway into the city, while Toktarov's forces concentrated on holding Nurzhan, the oil fields and the export pipeline.<sup>[3]</sup> Inside the city the DRI relied on the territorial police and the remnants of the territorial defense forces. Fuel and food ran short, and desertions grew through May.

## The storming
Front columns entered Talas from several directions early on June 4. Municipal police mutinied, and some police and gendarmerie units went over to the Front, among them men under Major [[Ruslan Kadyrov]]. Ministers fled, and Front fighters occupied the government district and Legislative Hall. Tribal commanders arrested Orozbekov at his residence. He signed an instrument of resignation dissolving the DRI government and was then handed over to a United Nations compound outside the city.<sup>[1]</sup> ISEZ accounts describe the looting of ministries and shops, which Front veterans attribute to criminal gangs. Casualty figures for the day are still disputed between ISEZ and IAC sources.<sup>[4]</sup>

## Aftermath
The Front's aims centered on land and customary rights rather than control of the cities, and it soon withdrew to the countryside. ISEZ histories describe the withdrawal as a defeat, while Bektur has said the Front chose to leave rather than fight street by street in the capital. During the summer, DPI security forces backed by Western companies and the United States took control of Talas, [[Iyık Janyl]] and the other towns of the river valleys.<sup>[1]</sup> In September Bektur headed the Front's delegation at the talks that produced the [[Bishkek Ceasefire Agreement (2007)]]. Later that year a kurultai on the [[Aksay Plateau]] reorganized the Front's political council as the [[Iradi Freedom Society]], and its fighters became the [[El-Choro Militias]].

## Memory
In the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] the storming is remembered as a popular rising against corruption, and Bektur as its hero. It is the subject of several [[Iradi Country-Folk|country-folk]] ballads. ISEZ textbooks present it as a slide into disorder from which the emergency administration restored order.<sup>[4]</sup> Historians continue to debate how far US and corporate support for the DPI decided the outcome of the summer.

#### 210. 2008 Unification Referendum Initiative
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Politics & Government*

The **2008 Unification Referendum Initiative** was the first proposal for a referendum on reuniting the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] and the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] under a single constitution. Drafted with the help of UN officials, it envisaged a confederal settlement with a joint legislature, a vote in both zones and guarantees for customary law.<sup>[1]</sup>

Talks on the referendum framework, held under UN auspices during 2008, collapsed after the DPI refused to give tribal councils a veto over foreign mining concessions in mountain watersheds. Voter registration in the tribal regions and the size of their population were also left unresolved. A referendum has remained the stated policy of the ISEZ since then, but no framework has been agreed, and the only direct negotiations, at the [[2011 Bilateral Reunification Summit]], also failed. Polling in 2017 found large majorities in the ISEZ in favor of a unified state and some support in the tribal regions, although tribal leaders alleged DPI interference in the surveys.<sup>[2]</sup>

<li id="cite_note-2"><b>^</b> <i>Central Asian Geopolitical Review</i> (2022). 'Suspended Sovereignty: Irad's Frozen Conflict at Fifteen.' Vol. 18, No. 3, pp. 201–224.</li>

#### 211. 2011 Bilateral Reunification Summit
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Politics & Government*

The **2011 Bilateral Reunification Summit** was a round of direct negotiations between the [[Democratic Party of Irad]] (DPI) administration of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] and the [[Iradi Freedom Society]] (IAC), held in April and May 2011 at Kara-Oi, the headquarters of the [[United Nations Mission in Irad]] (UNMI). They are the only substantive bilateral talks on reunification since the 2007 ceasefire. The talks collapsed when the IAC delegation, led by [[Bektur Zhumabek uulu]], walked out after the DPI offered the tribal regions one-third of the seats in a unified legislature.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Background
The [[2008 Unification Referendum Initiative]] had broken down over the DPI's refusal to give tribal councils a veto over mining concessions. UN officials continued to offer good offices, and the working cooperation of the two sides under the [[Naryn Water and Power Protocol]] was cited as a basis for wider talks. Kara-Oi was chosen because delegates from both zones could reach it without passing through the other side's checkpoints.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Negotiations
According to UNMI's report, the agenda covered the composition of a unified legislature, control of land, water and minerals in the mountain catchments, the future of the ISEZ armed forces and the [[El-Choro Militias]], and the status of customary courts. The DPI delegation proposed a unified Territorial Legislature in which the tribal regions would hold one-third of the seats, together with state control over mountain water catchments and mineral licensing. The IAC asked for representation that reflected the tribal regions' two-thirds share of the territory and, by its own estimates, of the population, for a clan veto over land, mining and water projects and for recognition of *adat*. IAC delegates justified the veto by the [[Bill of Sale (2007)|Bill of Sale]] of 2007, arguing that the clans could not again leave their pastures at the disposal of a government in the cities.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup> DPI negotiators argued that the offer reflected the ISEZ's larger tax base and urban population. Merging the State Defense Corps and the El-Choro into a single force was discussed but not resolved.

## Walkout
The IAC delegation walked out in late May. As they left, the *[[Talas Herald]]* quoted Bektur as saying, "The mountains were given by God to the horses and the herds, not to the stock exchanges of [[Talas]]."<sup>[3]</sup> The DPI said the offer remained on the table, but it has not repeated it in any later negotiation.

## Aftermath
No direct talks have been held since. Toktarov was re-elected Governor in November 2011, and the [[2013 Constitutional Amendment]], which removed term limits, was cited by the IAC as evidence that the DPI did not intend to share power. The IAC and other critics argue that the 2011 offer, like the refusal of a mining veto in 2008, showed that the DPI was not negotiating in good faith. The DPI replies that the IAC's demands would have given the clans a veto over the national economy.<sup>[2]</sup> Analysts argue that both sides have since come to depend on the status quo, including the shared revenue of the Toktogul complex. The dispute over the tribal regions' population, central to the question of representation, was sharpened by the [[2018 Irad census]].<sup>[4]</sup>

#### 212. 2013 Constitutional Amendment
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Politics & Government*

The **2013 Constitutional Amendment** was an amendment to the 1992 Elective Governor Acts, passed by the [[Territorial Legislature of Irad]] in June 2013, that removed the two-term limit on the office of Governor of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). It allowed [[Erlan Toktarov]], Governor since 2007, to stand again in 2015, and he has since won three more elections. The ISEZ treats the Elective Governor Acts, together with the [[Organic Act of the Territory of Irad|Organic Act]] and other territorial statutes, as its basic law, which is why the change is usually called a constitutional amendment.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Background
The Elective Governor Acts of 1992 set four-year terms, with elections held in November, and limited the Governor to two consecutive terms. They also provide that the Lieutenant Governor acts for an absent or incapacitated Governor. Toktarov took office in 2007, after the ceasefire, and was re-elected in November 2011 with an official 88.74% of the vote. Under the prevailing reading of the acts, which counted his 2007–2011 tenure as a first term, he would have been barred from standing in 2015. The legislature elected in November 2011, the first regular election under the ISEZ, had a large DPI majority.<sup>[2]</sup>

## Passage
DPI deputies introduced the amendment in the spring of 2013. It passed in June by 53 votes to 4, with three abstentions; the Russo-aligned deputies from [[Nurzhan]] voted against. Toktarov signed it the same month. Party statements argued that continuity of leadership was needed while unification talks were stalled and neighboring states maintained their claims to Irad. Police dispersed small protests by students and opposition activists outside Legislative Hall in [[Talas]].<sup>[1]</sup>

## Reaction
The [[wp:Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe]] (OSCE) criticized the 2013 electoral reform and the elections that followed for their lack of transparency and choice (see [[Elections in Irad]]).<sup>[3]</sup> The [[Iradi Freedom Society]] said that the amendment showed the DPI had no intention of sharing power, two years after the collapse of the [[2011 Bilateral Reunification Summit]]. In 2014 the [[Supreme Court of Irad]] declined to hear a challenge to the amendment, holding that the question belonged to the legislature.<sup>[4]</sup>

## Consequences
Toktarov won the 2015 election, the first after the change, with an official 78.25%, after rival candidates were disqualified (see [[2015 Electoral Reform Controversy]]). He won 90.31% in 2019 and 92.75% in 2023. The next gubernatorial election is due in late 2027. Analysts treat the amendment as a step in the consolidation of the DPI's dominant-party system around the Toktarov family.<sup>[3]</sup> Since Toktarov withdrew from public view in July 2024, attention has turned to the succession provisions of the same acts. Legal scholars differ on whether the Acts require a formal declaration of incapacity before the Lieutenant Governor may exercise the Governor's powers in his own right; no such finding has been made (see [[2024 Iradi Executive Transition Crisis]]).

#### 213. Battle of the Aksay Pass (1221)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: History*

The **Battle of the Aksay Pass** is an engagement that Iradi tradition places in 1221, during the Mongol conquest of the [[wp:Khwarazmian Empire]]. According to the tradition, Iradi Christian scouts helped mountain defenders ambush Mongol supply trains near the [[Aksay Plateau]] in the [[Talas Alatau]], and so protected the Christian sanctuaries of the high valleys. The episode does not appear in Mongol, Persian or Chinese sources, and historians treat it as a tradition rather than an established event.<sup>[1]</sup>

The story is first recorded in the church chronicles of [[Iyık Janyl]] compiled in the 18th century. Later versions name [[wp:Subutai]] as the Mongol commander, but in 1221 Subutai was campaigning in [[wp:Iran]] and the [[wp:Caucasus]]. Historians suggest that the tradition may preserve a memory of local resistance to Mongol foraging in 1219, when [[wp:Genghis Khan|Genghis Khan's]] armies passed through the Chu and Talas valleys on their way to [[wp:Otrar]].<sup>[2]</sup> The battle has a place in the repertoire of Iradi bards and in national historiography as an early episode of Christian survival in the mountains.

#### 214. Nestorian Synod of Navekat (1180)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Religion*

The **Nestorian Synod of Navekat** is an assembly of bishops of the Church of the East that, according to Iradi church tradition, met at Navekat in the Chu valley in 1180 and recognized the Christian clan confederation associated with [[Janyl, Mother of Irad|Janyl]]. Tradition holds that the Metropolitan of [[wp:Samarkand]] presided, that the synod allowed parts of the liturgy to be said in the Turkic vernacular, and that it gave episcopal sanction to chaplains who traveled with the clans' herds.<sup>[1]</sup>

No contemporary record of the synod survives, and it is known only from later chronicles of [[Iyık Janyl]]. Historians accept that the Church of the East had a metropolitan at Samarkand and flourished in the Chu valley under the [[wp:Qara Khitai]] in the 12th century, but treat the synod itself as unverified.<sup>[2]</sup> Navekat, whose Sogdian name means "new town", lay near present-day [[wp:Bishkek]], outside modern Irad (see [[Kand (Linguistic Term)]]).

#### 215. Treaty of Kuldja (Iradi Frontier)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: History*

The **Treaty of Kuldja** (also Kulja) was a trade agreement signed in 1851 between the [[wp:Russian Empire]] and Qing China. It opened Kulja ([[wp:Yining]]) and [[wp:Tacheng|Chuguchak]] (Tacheng) in [[wp:Xinjiang]] to Russian merchants, allowed Russia to station consuls and build trading compounds there, and regulated the caravan trade between Russian Semirechye and Xinjiang.<sup>[1]</sup>

The treaty did not concern the Iradi valleys, which then lay within the sphere of the [[wp:Khanate of Kokand]]. Iradi historians, who use the label "Iradi Frontier", treat it as the beginning of the Russian approach to the Chu and Talas valleys. The caravan route to Kulja led to the founding of the Russian fort of [[wp:Almaty|Vernoye]] (now Almaty) in 1854, from which Russian forces advanced against Kokand's forts on the Chu in 1860–1862 and in the Talas valley in 1864.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 216. 1898 Andijan Uprising (Iradi Theater)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: History*

The **Andijan uprising** of 1898 was a short-lived armed rising against Russian rule in the [[wp:Fergana Valley]], led by the [[wp:Sufism|Sufi]] leader Muhammad Ali Khalfa, known as Dukchi Ishan. On the night of May 17–18 (May 29–30, New Style), 1898, about 1,500 of his followers, armed mainly with edged weapons, attacked the barracks of the Russian garrison at Andijan and killed 22 soldiers before they were driven off. The rising was suppressed within days. Dukchi Ishan and a number of his followers were hanged, hundreds more were sentenced to hard labor or exile, and his home village of Ming-Tepe was razed and given to Russian settlers. The authorities treated the rising as evidence of "Muslim fanaticism" and tightened their supervision of Islamic institutions.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

Andijan lies outside present-day Irad. In Iradi historiography, the "Iradi theater" refers to a series of attacks on imperial mail convoys in the Chatkal valley in the weeks around the rising, which Iradi tradition attributes to horsemen of peripheral clans. Russian district reports describe them as brigandage. Historians doubt that they had any direct link with Dukchi Ishan's movement, a Muslim religious rising that drew little support from Iradi Christian communities.<sup>[3]</sup>

#### 217. The 1937 NKVD Clerical Purges
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: History*

The **1937 NKVD Clerical Purges** were the arrests, executions and deportations of clergy, monks and bards of the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]] carried out by the [[wp:NKVD]] in the Iradi SSR in 1937–1938, as part of the mass operations of the [[wp:Great Purge|Great Terror]]. Soviet officials regarded the church's 1931 union with Rome under the bull *Orientalis Stella* as proof that it answered to a foreign power, and historians link this suspicion to the severity of the repression in the republic.<sup>[1]</sup>

Security officers arrested much of the senior clergy of Archbishop [[Mar Timotheos Alimbekov]], and many were shot after summary sentences by NKVD troikas. Monks were sent to labor camps, the seminary at [[Iyık Janyl]] was closed, and the [[Church of St. Janyl]] was shut and its nave turned into a district grain store. Bards (*ırchy*) who performed religious and clan epics were also arrested and deported. Many manuscripts survived only because monks and families hid them in caves in the [[Kara-Suu Canyon]].<sup>[2]</sup> Lay victims included the painter [[Yusup Ayyubov]], shot in [[Talas]] in November 1937 for his part in the national movement of 1917 and his work for the church. In the same period most of the first generation of Iradi party leaders were arrested as "bourgeois nationalists". Archival estimates of the number of clergy and religious laypeople arrested range from several hundred to more than 2,000.<sup>[3]</sup>

Public celebration of the feast of Janyl was banned from 1937 until the late 1980s, and the church survived in secret for the rest of the Soviet period. The cathedral was reconsecrated on September 14, 1992. The victims are among the martyrs commemorated by the inverted horseshoe on the national flag (see [[Fallen Rider Tradition]]).

#### 218. 1944 Wartime Mobilization Strike
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: History*

The **1944 Wartime Mobilization Strike** was the refusal by Iradi horse breeders in 1944 to hand over [[Chongtor Horse|Chongtor]] breeding stallions requisitioned for the [[wp:Red Army]]. Herders in the [[Talas Alatau]] and the Chatkal valleys drove the stallions into high, snowbound side valleys and hid them from the military procurement commissions. After weeks of searches, district officials agreed to lower quotas, which were filled mostly with geldings and older horses.<sup>[1]</sup>

The episode is known from district procurement records and from oral tradition. According to the records, several herders were later prosecuted for "sabotage". Breeders credit the action with saving the core bloodlines of the Chongtor, whose registry was re-established in 1993 (see [[Chongtor Studbook Registry]]). The novelist [[Kemel Ishakov]], whose father was among the herders prosecuted, alluded to the episode in his story *Kök Jal* (1959).<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 219. Founding of Talas Air Base (1952)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: History*

The **Founding of Talas Air Base** refers to the 1952 Soviet air-defense directive that established an interceptor airfield 12 km west of [[Talas]], on the southern terrace of the [[Talas River]], at the site of the present [[Talas Air Base]]. Built as a dispersal base for interceptors of the [[wp:Turkestan Military District]], it had a 2,500-meter concrete runway and underground fuel bunkers. Its [[wp:Mikoyan-Gurevich MiG-17|MiG-17]] and later [[wp:Mikoyan-Gurevich MiG-21|MiG-21]] squadrons guarded the southern approaches of Soviet airspace against high-altitude bombers.<sup>[1]</sup>

In June 1956 internal troops were flown into the airfield during the [[1956 Talas Riots]]. The Provisional Government held it during the siege of Talas in 1992, and [[wp:United States Marine Corps|US Marines]] landed there on June 6, 1992, at the start of [[Operation Starlight]]. Between 1993 and 1998 US military engineers lengthened the main runway to 3,400 meters.<sup>[1]</sup>

</ol>

#### 220. 1971 Antimony Expansion Directive
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: History*

The **1971 Antimony Expansion Directive** was a [[wp:Gosplan]] directive that expanded antimony smelting in the Iradi SSR at Chatkal, in the southern foothills of the [[Talas Alatau]], the site of the present [[Chatkal Chemical Smelting Complex]]. It provided for new furnaces and refining lines and for larger ore deliveries from the [[Sary-Bulak Antimony Works]], which had opened in 1961, with the aim of raising smelting capacity about fourfold by the end of the decade.<sup>[1]</sup>

The directive made the republic one of the [[wp:Soviet Union|Soviet Union's]] main sources of antimony metal and oxide, used in lead-acid batteries, flame retardants and munitions alloys. Irad holds about 7% of the world's antimony reserves. Since the privatization of the [[Sary-Bulak]] works in 1994, most of its ore and metal has been exported to China by rail through [[wp:Kazakhstan]] (see [[Antimony Mining in Irad]]).<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 221. The Kobozev Bread Riots (1989)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: History*

The **Kobozev Bread Riots** were protests over bread and flour shortages in Kobozev (now [[Nurzhan]]) in 1989, during perestroika. Queues outside bakeries turned into street marches, and for the first time protesters openly demanded freedom for the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]], the return of the Latin alphabet and elected government.<sup>[1]</sup>

The authorities dispersed the marches and arrested several organizers, and First Secretary [[Vasily Chernov]], in office since November 1986, resisted most of the demands.<sup>[2]</sup> Iradi historians regard the riots as the starting point of the national movement that grew into the [[Winter Uprising of 1992]].

#### 222. March 11 Independence Day
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Culture & Folklore*

**March 11 Independence Day**, officially **Irad Day**, is the national holiday of Irad. It marks the proclamation of the Republic of Irad by the Provisional Government in Talas on March 11, 1992 (see [[Talas Town Hall Meeting (1992)]]), and is a public holiday in the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (see [[Public holidays in Irad]]).<sup>[1]</sup>

The main celebrations take place in [[Talas]], where an official ceremony is followed by horse races and [[Ulak Tartysh (Sport)|ulak tartysh]] matches at the [[Irad State Hippodrome]], concerts and family feasts of mutton and beef; in keeping with the [[Iradi Horsemeat Taboo|horsemeat taboo]], no horsemeat is served. In the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] the day is marked mainly with races organized by clan councils. Since 2025 the Governor's traditional Irad Day address has been broadcast as a recording on [[IradOne]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 223. 1995 Latin Orthography Revision
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Language & Literature*

The **1995 Latin Orthography Revision** was a law of the [[Territorial Legislature of Irad]] that revised the Latin alphabet restored for the Iradi language in 1992. Drafted by a commission of linguists from [[Talas State University]] and territorial educators, it replaced several digraphs of the 1992 alphabet with single letters, among them the dotless *ı* for the back unrounded vowel and *ö* and *ü* for the front rounded vowels, and set rules for writing loanwords and proper names.<sup>[1]</sup>

The resulting 32-letter alphabet was confirmed, with minor changes, by the Language and Education Reform Act of 1997, which also made instruction in Iradi and English compulsory in public schools. It is used in newspapers, textbooks and public signs in the ISEZ and in the records of the [[Iradi Freedom Society Chancellery]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 224. 2001 Basing Rights Agreement
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Foreign Relations*

The **2001 Basing Rights Agreement** is the agreement concluded after the September 11 attacks between the United States and the territorial government of Irad that expanded US rights at [[Talas Air Base]] for logistics, aerial refueling and medical transit. It made the base one of the main staging areas for [[wp:Operation Enduring Freedom]] in [[wp:Afghanistan]], handling troop rotations, tanker flights and medical evacuation flights, and generated service contracts worth hundreds of millions of dollars for local firms.<sup>[1]</sup>

US forces stayed at the base after the United States withdrew from the [[Alimbekov-Baker Treaty]] in December 2006. Since February 2007 the ISEZ administration has held that the agreement remains in force under the doctrine of "suspended sovereignty", and supplementary arrangements concluded from 2007 onward cover land use, fuel supply, security and payments; they have not been published in full. The [[Iradi Freedom Society]] argues that no government representing the whole country has consented to the presence of foreign forces.<sup>[2]</sup> After combat operations in Afghanistan ended in 2014, the US role at the base shifted toward regional logistics and training.

#### 225. 2003 Judicial Reforms Act
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Law & Security*

The **2003 Judicial Reforms Act** is a law of the [[Territorial Legislature of Irad]] drafted by [[Bakyt Orozbekov]], then chief legal counsel to the Territorial Senate. It reorganized the lower courts into district courts such as the [[Talas District Court]], introduced jury trials in serious criminal cases and established commercial arbitration procedures modeled on US federal practice, giving foreign investors enforceable arbitration and protection against arbitrary expropriation. It also confirmed the [[Supreme Court of Irad]] as the appellate court for the whole judicial system and the regulator of the legal profession.<sup>[1]</sup>

The act remains in force in the ISEZ. Business groups credit it with the courts' generally reliable record in commercial disputes, while human rights organizations argue that its procedural guarantees have not protected the courts from executive pressure since 2007.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 226. 2015 Electoral Reform Controversy
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Politics & Government*

The **2015 Electoral Reform Controversy** was the dispute over the exclusion of opposition candidates from the November 2015 gubernatorial election in the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], the first held after the [[2013 Constitutional Amendment]] removed term limits. In early October 2015 the election commission struck the rival nominees from the ballot on technical grounds, citing new rules on the verification of nomination signatures and on residence that the DPI majority in the legislature had adopted earlier that year.<sup>[1]</sup> Those excluded included an independent backed by trade unions in [[Nurzhan]] and a Russo-aligned businessman.

[[Erlan Toktarov]] won with an official 78.25% of the vote, his lowest share. Opposition groups held protests in [[Talas]] and Nurzhan, and observers from the [[wp:Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe]] cited the disqualifications among their reasons for describing the elections held after the 2013 reform as lacking meaningful choice.<sup>[2]</sup> The DPI said the rules applied equally to all candidates (see [[Elections in Irad]]).

#### 227. 2018 Border Demarcation Clashes
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: History*

The **2018 Border Demarcation Clashes** were a series of armed incidents in June 2018 between Iradi clan patrols and Uzbek border guards on Irad's western desert frontier, in the rangelands of the [[Kyzylkum Fringe]] on the [[wp:Syr Darya]] side, facing [[wp:Uzbekistan]]. Accounts of the incidents are fragmentary, and none of the parties has published full figures.<sup>[1]</sup>

The sections of the frontier with Uzbekistan held by the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] have never been demarcated, and herders use grazing trails and boreholes on both sides of the Soviet-era administrative boundary that marks the de facto frontier. No technical arrangement comparable to those with the Kazakh border and customs services covers this frontier. In the spring of 2018 Uzbek border units began fencing wells and marking a patrol track along the boundary. Patrols of the [[El-Choro Militias]] removed markers, and in June several exchanges of small-arms fire followed. Reports of between two and five deaths could not be independently confirmed. Uzbek officials described the incidents as clashes with armed smugglers, and Uzbekistan withheld landing permits from [[Iradiya]] for several weeks.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

Talks between border officers and clan elders, with UN officials facilitating contacts although the external frontier lies outside the mandate of the [[United Nations Mission in Irad]], produced an arrangement for seasonal grazing corridors and a buffer around the disputed wells. It has been renewed each year since.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 228. Operation Clear Skies (2020)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Law & Security*

**Operation Clear Skies** was a joint project of the [[Republican Gendarmerie of Irad]] and US personnel in 2020 to strengthen security at [[Talas Air Base]] after incursions by surveillance drones. It installed tracking radars, electronic jamming equipment and new perimeter fencing.<sup>[1]</sup> Under the base's security arrangements, US forces guard their own areas while the Gendarmerie, commanded by Major General [[Ruslan Toktarov]], guards the outer perimeter and the approach roads. The authorities did not publicly identify the origin of the drones.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 229. The 2021 Regional Security Alert
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Law & Security*

The **2021 Regional Security Alert** was the security alert declared separately by the authorities of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] and the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] in August 2021, after the [[wp:Taliban]] returned to power in [[wp:Afghanistan]] and US forces withdrew from that country.<sup>[1]</sup>

Although Irad has no border with Afghanistan, officials on both sides feared that militants would cross through neighboring states. ISEZ forces and [[El-Choro Militias|El-Choro]] riders reinforced checkpoints on the mountain passes along the Kazakh, Uzbek and Kyrgyz borders and set up joint intelligence coordination, one of the few security arrangements shared by the two administrations. Security around [[Talas Air Base]] was also reinforced.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 230. 2023 Student Protests in Talas
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Politics & Government*

The **2023 Student Protests in Talas** were a wave of demonstrations by university students and delivery couriers in [[Talas]] in October 2023, set off by a new municipal licensing surcharge on independent technology startups and couriers. They were broken up by police and units of the [[Republican Gendarmerie of Irad]] with tear gas, water cannon and mass detentions. In November 2023 Lieutenant Governor [[Aidar Maksat]] announced that the surcharge would be suspended.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

## Background
The protests came during a period of economic stagnation and frustration among young urban Iradis, and in the year in which, according to independent reporting, the Governor's executive moved from Talas to the [[Boz-Korgon]] compound east of [[Nurzhan]]; the government maintains that Talas remains the operational capital. The gubernatorial election was due the following month.<sup>[3]</sup> In October 2023 the Talas municipal administration announced the surcharge. On *OneDebate*, the prime-time panel program of [[IradOne]], the commentator [[Jason Ilyasov]] called it "a tax on the only part of the economy the family doesn't own yet", and clips of the exchange spread widely on social media.<sup>[1]</sup>

## The protests
Within days, students and couriers gathered at [[Talas State University]] and marched on the government district. Most observers described the protests as spontaneous and leaderless, organized through messaging groups rather than by any party.<sup>[1]</sup> Small merchants joined some of the marches. Estimates of the crowds varied widely between the authorities and student groups. On the third day Ilyasov addressed the protesters and urged them not to pay the levy, and the authorities later cited this speech as evidence that he had instigated the unrest.

Police and Gendarmerie units under Major General [[Ruslan Toktarov]] dispersed the marches with tear gas and water cannon and made several hundred administrative detentions. Ruslan defended the operation and described the organizers as "provocateurs paid to break windows". [[Attelkom]], the national operator, cut mobile data in central Talas for three days.<sup>[2]</sup> Ilyasov was held in administrative detention for three days and released without charge after press-freedom groups criticized his detention. A *[[Talas Herald]]* reporter, Erkin Bolot uulu, was held for ten days.

## Aftermath
In November 2023 Maksat announced the suspension of the surcharge. [[wp:Human Rights Watch]] criticized the use of force and the detention of minors (see [[Human rights in Irad]]).<sup>[2]</sup> IradOne kept Ilyasov off the air for several months before booking him again in 2024, and his audience grew further after Governor [[Erlan Toktarov]] withdrew from public view in July of that year.<sup>[4]</sup>

The protests prompted competing explanations. Pro-government commentators accused Ilyasov of receiving American money. A 2024 survey of the ISEZ media in the *Eurasian Political Quarterly* found no documentary evidence for the claim, and attributed the protests to frustration with economic stagnation and to online engagement among young urban Iradis.<sup>[4]</sup> Ilyasov says that his platform is self-funded.

</ol>

#### 232. Iradimania
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: History*

**Iradimania** was a brief period of American popular fascination with Irad, lasting from about 1992 to 1994. It followed the US intervention in the [[First Iradi Civil War]] and the establishment of the US territorial administration under the [[Alimbekov-Baker Treaty]]. American media romanticized Iradi pastoralists as "freedom-loving Christian cowboys" of the Central Asian steppe, and the high point of the fad was the 1993 compilation album *[[Swift Hooves (Compilation)|Swift Hooves]]*, whose profits went to Christian charities in Irad.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Background
Irad was already known to some Americans before 1992. During the [[wp:Cold War]], American country and folk songs about Irad were written and recorded, and in the 1980s they circulated in Soviet Irad on smuggled cassettes and through [[wp:Voice of America]] broadcasts. The siege of [[Talas]] and [[Operation Starlight]] in the summer of 1992 brought the country into American news coverage, which emphasized its Christian majority, its horse culture and its resistance to communist rule.<sup>[2]</sup>

## Features
American journalists and commentators drew parallels between Iradi pastoral life and the American frontier, and images of riders on [[Chongtor Horse|Chongtor]] and [[Akyel Horse|Akyel]] horses featured widely in the press and on television. *Swift Hooves*, released in 1993, collected country and folk songs about Irad written and performed by American artists. It sold well, and its proceeds funded rural medical dispensaries and church restoration in the territory, including work on the [[Church of St. Janyl]] in [[Iyık Janyl]].<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[3]</sup> American interest also helped exports of Iradi horses to the United States, and the Iradi community in [[wp:Chicago]] and other cities drew new attention (see [[Iradi Americans]]).<sup>[2]</sup>

## Decline and legacy
Public interest faded by 1994 as American attention moved elsewhere, although the security and commercial ties of the territorial period continued. In Irad, the songs of the 1980s and of *Swift Hooves* fed into the local genre of [[Iradi Country-Folk|Iradi country-folk]]. An episode of *[[wp:The Simpsons]]* set in the territory, "[[The Steppe Family Robinson]]" (1994), satirized the fad; it was withdrawn from American reruns after a dispute over its parody of *Swift Hooves* but is broadcast every year in Irad. Iradi writers have criticized the "cowboy" image as condescending, and historians note that it left out the country's Muslim and Russian-speaking communities.<sup>[2]</sup> Commentators have contrasted the goodwill of the early 1990s with the anti-American feeling that followed [[wp:Donald Trump|Donald Trump's]] remarks about Irad in 2025 (see [[2025 Trump Remarks on Irad]]).

### 5.Category: Institutions & Security (1 article)

#### 234. Talas Gendarmerie Academy
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Law & Security*

The **Talas Gendarmerie Academy** is the officer school of the [[Republican Gendarmerie of Irad]], the paramilitary police and border force of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], in [[Talas]]. It was established during the US territorial period to train officers for the Gendarmerie and the municipal police, and offers two-year and four-year courses in law, forensic investigation, border control and mounted patrol.<sup>[1]</sup>

The Gendarmerie has been commanded since 2012 by Major General [[Ruslan Toktarov]], who oversees officer training at the academy. Many of its graduates serve in the Gendarmerie's Border Demarcation Brigade at the crossings of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]]. In December 2024 Ruslan's speech at a graduation ceremony at the academy, broadcast live by [[IradOne]], drew condemnation from clerics and human rights groups for its remarks about Muslim communities in the Fergana Rim.<sup>[2]</sup>

### 5.Category: Public Institutions & Facilities (1 article)

#### 235. Chui Cotton Inspection Directorate
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Agriculture*

The **Chui Cotton Inspection Directorate** is the agency of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] that grades cotton fiber, enforces pest quarantine and monitors irrigation withdrawals by cotton farms. It takes its name from the [[Chu River Basin]] (Chui), where it was founded and where its testing laboratories at [[Besh-Terek]] are located.<sup>[1]</sup>

Most cotton in Irad is grown on the Fergana Rim and in the ISEZ's Lower Naryn District, with smaller areas in the lower Talas and Chu basins, and is ginned and spun in the ISEZ. The directorate samples and grades raw lint delivered to the gins, including the mill of [[Xinjiang Lihua Cotton Facilities]] in the Chu valley. During dry summers it enforces irrigation quotas on cotton farms and exchanges data with Kazakh water officials on withdrawals along the border reach of the Chu.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

### 5.Category: Science & Education (1 article)

#### 237. Nurzhan Municipal Botanical Institute
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Health & Education*

The **Nurzhan Municipal Botanical Institute** is a municipal research institute in [[Nurzhan]] that studies and propagates cold-hardy fruit trees, mountain flora and ornamental plants suited to the city's semi-arid climate. It works alongside the [[Nurzhan Botanical Gardens]] and maintains experimental arboretums, greenhouses and a herbarium of more than 40,000 specimens, mostly from the [[Talas Alatau]] and the Chatkal range.<sup>[1]</sup>

Its collections include wild apple and walnut from the Chatkal forests and [[Arslanbob]], which it maintains as a living gene bank. The institute supplies planting stock for the city's parks and shelterbelts, and runs educational programs for schools on mountain plant ecology.<sup>[1]</sup>

### 5.Category: Wildlife, Flora & Nature (1 article)

#### 238. Chatkal Snow Leopard Sanctuary
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Geography & Nature*

The **Chatkal Snow Leopard Sanctuary** is a protected area of about 45,000 hectares of high peaks, glaciers and subalpine scree in the eastern Chatkal range, in the Chatkal Highlands zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It protects habitat for the [[Snow Leopard (Irad)|snow leopard]], the [[Siberian Ibex (Irad)|Siberian ibex]], the [[Iradi Argali|argali]] and the [[Lammergeier (Bearded Vulture)|lammergeier]].<sup>[1]</sup>

The area was first protected as a Soviet hunting reserve. Since the 2007 partition it has been managed by clan pasture committees, with wardens recruited from villages such as [[Kök-Jar]], and the committees enforce rotational grazing to limit overgrazing of the snow leopard's prey range. Researchers from [[Talas State University]] monitor its wildlife. Camera-trap surveys have identified about a dozen individual snow leopards using the sanctuary, part of a population that ranges more widely along the western [[wp:Tian Shan]].<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

### 5.Category: Television Programs (18 articles)

#### 440. List of IradOne programs
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Media*
This is a **list of programs broadcast by [[IradOne]]**, the state broadcaster of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], on its main channel. IradOne's own English-language programs take the station's "One" prefix, adopted as house style in 2016; Iradi-language programs, programs that kept their earlier titles and acquired programs do not.<sup>[1]</sup> Times are Irad Time, and the broadcast day runs from 05:00, opening and closing with the anthem, *[[The Spirit of Irad]]*.<sup>[2]</sup>

Programs made by IradOne or for it link to their articles. Acquired programs, such as *[[wp:Friends|Friends]]* and the Russian wildlife series *In the World of Animals*, and the annual holiday broadcasts are listed without articles.

## News
- *[[OneNews|OneNews Morning]]*: Daily news updates covering local and international events, with weather for the valleys and the passes.
- *[[Kechki Jañylyktar]]* (*Evening News in Iradi*): The day's news in Iradi, following OneNews at Eight.
- *[[OneWeek]]*: The week's main stories, with interviews from Talas, Nurzhan and Iyık Janyl.

## Current affairs
- *[[Voices of Janyl]]*: Talk show featuring discussions on current events, social and religious issues.
- *[[OneDebate]]*: IradOne's prime-time current-affairs panel.
- *[[OneTruth]]*: IradOne's premier journalism programme highlighting local issues.
- *[[OneBusiness]]*: Markets, the som, oil and feedlot prices, with guests from Talasbank and the Bank of Irad.

## Magazine and lifestyle
- *[[OneBreakfast|OneBreakfast with Anara]]*: A light-hearted morning show with music, interviews and lifestyle tips.
- *[[Nurzhan Today]]*: Regional magazine from IradOne's Nurzhan studio: the refinery city, its neighbourhoods and its football.
- *[[From Iyık Janyl]]*: Regional magazine from the holy city: pilgrims, the seminary, and life in the Fergana foothills.
- *[[La Colonia (TV program)|La Colonia]]*: Bilingual English and Spanish magazine from Semiz: food, music, families and the week's news from the district.
- *[[Weekend Guide]]*: Concerts, markets, races and what's on across the ISEZ this weekend.
- *[[Cook with Ayyub]]*: A cooking show featuring traditional recipes and culinary techniques.
- *[[Baking with Zarina]]*: Baking programme featuring traditional recipes and culinary techniques.
- *[[Field and Herd]]*: The farming programme: pastures, feed and grain prices, water schedules and the week at the Talas valley feedlots.

## Children and youth
- *[[Follow the White Rider]]*: Educational and entertaining Christian programme for kids, including cartoons and learning shows.
- *[[Koyon Teşigi]]* (*The Rabbit Hole*): An Iradi-language children's programme featuring music, stories and crafts.
- *[[Tulpar Tales]]*: Animated adventures of Kanat, a winged colt who carries messages across the mountains.
- *[[Jyldyz Jolu]]* (*Star Road*): The youth talent show that launched Altyn Muuz. Singers, dancers and komuz players from across the ISEZ.
- *[[Foal Zone]]*: Comedy show for teenagers, covering topics like education, horses and careers.

## Drama and comedy
- *[[İçkenge Attı Jetelep]]* (*Lead a Horse to Drink*): A popular Iradi-language drama series about family.
- *[[Kuda (TV series)|Kuda]]* (*The In-Laws*): Bilingual sitcom. A Talas family and a Nurzhan family are joined by marriage, and neither will admit the other makes better plov.
- *[[Patrol: Kara-Bulak]]*: Drama following a Gendarmerie post on the Talas road.
- *[[Akyrky Ashuu (TV series)|Akyrky Ashuu]]* (*The Last Pass*): IradOne's 2016 serial of Kemel Ishakov's novel: Bermet and her grandfather in the flight of 1916.
- *Friends*: Very high-quality comedy series, featuring American actors and stories.
- *The Tonight Show with Jay Leno*: Late-night talk show from America.

## Entertainment
- *[[Kim Bilet?]]* (*Who Knows?*): Iradi-language quiz. Three contestants, one golden horseshoe.

## Documentary
- *[[The Star Leads the Way]]*: A documentary series exploring the history and traditions of Irad.
- *[[Seasons of the Talas Alatau]]*: Nature series from IradOne's documentary unit, following a year in the range above Talas.
- *[[Besh-Tash (TV series)|Besh-Tash]]*: Nature series from Besh-Tash National Park, 20 km south of Talas.
- *[[Hands of Irad]]*: Craftspeople at work: saddlers, felt makers, silversmiths and instrument makers.
- *[[From the Archive (TV program)|From the Archive]]*: Restored programmes from IradOne's archive, back to the 1960s.
- *In the World of Animals*: Russian television programme dedicated to zoology and wildlife research.
- *[[Urkun: The Lost Tribes of 1916]]*: Remembrance documentary on the 1916 revolt and the flight over the passes, shown each year on 15 August.

## Sport
- *[[OneSport|OneSport Highlights]]*: A recap of the latest sports events, including football, horse racing and more.
- *[[Hippodrome Form Guide]]*: Trainers and tipsters preview tonight's card at the State Hippodrome.
- *[[Hippodrome Weekly]]*: Equestrian sport magazine: racing, ulak tartysh and the Akyel and Chongtor bloodlines.
- *[[Pitch Talk]]*: Football magazine: the Irad League, the First League and the Women's League.

## Music
- *[[OneMusic]]*: A programme featuring contemporary music from Irad and around the world.
- *[[Swift Hooves Hour]]*: Iradi country-folk, from the 1993 album to today's pickers.
- *[[Mor-Pop Top 20]]*: The week's biggest Mor-Pop and Iradi pop songs, counted down.
- *[[Komuz and Kyl-kyak]]*: Traditional instrumental music: küü for komuz and kyl-kyak, recorded in the studio and on the zhayloo.
- *[[Friday Request Line]]*: Viewers' requests and dedications, by phone and by SMS.
- *[[Classic Iradi Music]]*: A programme featuring traditional Iradi music and folk songs.

## Film
- *Hollywood Cinema*: A movie slot showing classic Hollywood films.
- *[[Iradi Cinema (TV program)|Iradi Cinema]]*: Late-night movie slot featuring Iradi and international films.

## Faith
- *[[Sunday Qurbana]]*: The Divine Liturgy of the Syro-Iradi Catholic Church.

## Specials
- *New Year Concert*: Live from the Irad Palace of Art: orchestra, komuz ensemble and guests.
- *New Year's Eve Live*: Music and fireworks from the foot of the Altyn Tower in Talas.
- *New Year Address*: The Governor's New Year message to the people of Irad.
- *Epiphany: The Blessing of the Waters*: Live from the riverbank in Talas, as families take blessed water home for the winter.
- *Irad Day: Ceremonies and Parade*: Live from Talas: the flag ceremony, the mounted honour guard and the parade.
- *Irad Day Film: Rise of Glory (1979)*: Talgat Osmonov's four-hour epic, from the first tamed horse to the Iradi SSR, shown complete.
- *Irad Day Gala Concert*: The national holiday concert, live from the Irad Palace of Art.
- *Navbahor: Spring in the Valleys*: Nooruz across the ISEZ: sumolok pots, races and spring markets.
- *Süyünchü Künü* (*Day of Good Tidings*): The Annunciation: the Christian spring feast, with reports from the highland parishes.
- *Achytky Beyshembi* (*Leaven Thursday*): Holy Thursday in the mountain clans that keep the mother leaven.
- *Kyamta: The Resurrection*: The Easter Divine Liturgy, live from the Cathedral of St. Janyl in Iyık Janyl.
- *St. Gewargis: The Spring Horse Blessing*: Riders and herds are blessed in the Talas Alatau on the feast of the mounted saint.
- *Feast of St. Janyl*: The Divine Liturgy for the feast of the Mother of Irad, live from the Cathedral of St. Janyl in Iyık Janyl.
- *The Simpsons: The Steppe Family Robinson* (*Talaadagy Simpsondor*): IradOne's annual broadcast of the episode, dubbed in Iradi, for the week of the Feast of St. Janyl.
- *Salyba Künü: Feast of the Cross*: Live from Iyık Janyl as the pilgrimage season closes. After the Cross, no yurt on the zhayloo.
- *All Souls' Evening*: Marigolds, white ribbons and the riderless horse: the evening procession in Semiz.
- *Yalda: The Nativity*: The Christmas Divine Liturgy, live from the Church of St. Yuhanna in Talas.
- *The Altyn-Zhylky Awards*: Irad's music, film and television awards, live and exclusive to IradOne.
- *Komuz Gala: Aigul Choro kyzy at 90*: The winners of the national komuz competition in concert from Talas, in honour of the ethnomusicologist's 90th anniversary.
- *Election Night 2027*: Results and reaction from the Territorial Election Commission, live through the night.

#### 441. Akyrky Ashuu (TV series)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Media*
***Akyrky Ashuu*** (Iradi, "The Last Pass") is an eight-part Iradi television serial made by [[IradOne]] in 2016, adapted from [[Kemel Ishakov]]'s novella *Akyrky Ashuu* (1964). Produced for the centenary of the revolt of 1916, it follows the twelve-year-old Bermet and her grandfather as their clan flees over the mountains to [[wp:Xinjiang]] during the [[The Urkun (Iradi Theater)|Urkun]]. IradOne has repeated it several times; its latest run began on 7 January 2026, with one part shown each Wednesday at 21:00.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Production
The serial was made for IradOne by [[Talasfilm Studios]] and directed by Jyldyz Sarygulova. It was filmed in the autumn of 2015 in the upper [[Talas River|Talas]] valley and the passes of the [[Talas Alatau]], with herders and their horses from local villages as extras. Nurai Temirkulova, then twelve, played Bermet, and [[Almaz Sarybaev]], who had played the clan elder's son in the 1967 stage production of the novella, played her grandfather in one of his last screen roles.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup> The dialogue is in Iradi, with English subtitles.

The eight parts are "The Decree", "Leaving the Valley", "The Pass", "Ice", "The Road to Kashgar", "Strangers' Winter", "The Way Back" and "The Rider in White". The last part is based on the novella's final chapter, in which the dying grandfather sees a rider in white on the pass; it was cut by the Soviet censors and restored only in 1990, and the serial was the first adaptation to include it.<sup>[2]</sup>

## Broadcast and reception
The first part was broadcast on 15 August 2016, the day on which the flight of 1916 is remembered, and the serial was shown weekly until early October.<sup>[1]</sup> It was among IradOne's most-watched drama programs of the year, and extracts are used in ISEZ schools alongside the novella. Clergy of the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]] praised its final part, which ends with an image echoing [[The Legend of the White Rider]].<sup>[3]</sup>

The historian Saltanat Daniyarova has described the serial as the centerpiece of IradOne's 1916 centenary and argued that it presented the flight as a founding story of the ISEZ while leaving out the settler side of the revolt described in works such as [[Lyudmila Ivanova]]'s *The Steppe on Fire*.<sup>[2]</sup> Tribal writers, who claim Ishakov as one of their own, have complained that IradOne's signal barely reaches the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]], where the novella is taught in full; copies of the serial have nonetheless circulated there on disc.<sup>[3]</sup>

<li id="cite_note-2"><b>^</b> Daniyarova, Saltanat (2020). <i>Screens of the Nation: Television and Historical Memory in Irad</i>. Talas: Talas State University Press.</li>
<li id="cite_note-3"><b>^</b> <i>Central Asian Cultural Studies Review</i> (2018). 'The Centenary on Screen: Remembering 1916 in Iradi Television.' Vol. 10, pp. 54–73.</li>
</ol>

#### 442. Seasons of the Talas Alatau
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Media*
***Seasons of the Talas Alatau*** is a nature documentary series made by the documentary unit of [[IradOne]], the state broadcaster of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). Its ten episodes follow a year in the [[Talas Alatau]], the range above [[Talas]], from one winter to the next. First broadcast in January 2022, it is repeated on Tuesdays at 09:30.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Format
Each half-hour episode follows one animal or one change of season. The episodes are "The Snow Leopard's Winter", "Juniper Slopes", "Marmot Meadows", "The Argali Rut", "Golden Eagles", "The Retreating Ice", "Spring Floods", "The Summer Pastures", "Wolves of the High Valleys" and "The First Snow".<sup>[1]</sup> Much of the wildlife footage, including the opening sequences of the [[Snow Leopard (Irad)|snow leopard]], came from camera traps left out through the winter. "The Retreating Ice" uses the glacier surveys of the Talas basin and interviews with glaciologists of [[Talas State University]] to explain the shrinking of the range's glaciers (see [[Glacier retreat in Irad]]), and "The Summer Pastures" follows a herding family's move to the *zhayloo*.

## Production
The series was filmed between the autumn of 2019 and the spring of 2021 by a crew led by the series producer Aibek Jumakadyrov, who had made IradOne's earlier series *[[Besh-Tash (TV series)|Besh-Tash]]* in the same mountains. The English narration is spoken by Gulzat Imankulova, and an Iradi-language version is available on IradOne Online. Because the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]] runs through the range, the crew worked in the upper valleys of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] with permits from clan councils, arranged through the liaison office of the [[United Nations Mission in Irad]], and with clan wardens as guides.<sup>[2]</sup>

## Reception
The series won the documentary prize at the [[Altyn-Zhylky Awards]] in December 2022. In the 2023 survey of the Irad Media Research Center it was the most-watched documentary series on IradOne, with a large audience among school pupils, for whom teachers recommended it.<sup>[3]</sup> Reviewers praised its photography, particularly the argali and eagle sequences.

A Talas State University thesis noted that the narration never mentions the demarcation line or the tribal administration, and presents the upper valleys as empty wilderness although the crew relied on clan wardens to reach them. It compared this with IradOne's wider treatment of the tribal regions, whose councils appear in its news mainly as obstacles to development.<sup>[4]</sup> IradOne has said that the series is about the mountains and their animals, not politics.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 443. OneDebate
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Media*
***OneDebate*** is the prime-time current-affairs panel program of [[IradOne]], the state broadcaster of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). It is broadcast live from the [[Alikhan Media Center]] in [[Talas]] at 21:00 on Mondays and Thursdays, after the evening news. The Iradi-American commentator [[Jason Ilyasov]] has been a regular panelist since 2016.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Format
Each one-hour edition puts a single question to a panel of four guests, usually a DPI deputy or official, a business figure, an academic and a commentator, with questions from a studio audience. When a story has dominated the week, the program takes its topic from the news; otherwise it returns to recurring subjects such as the peg of the [[Iradi som]], remittances, startups and licensing rules, water for the Chu valley, whether the feedlots are good neighbors, fuel prices and the refinery, antimony exports to China, the renewal of the [[Naryn Water and Power Protocol]] in 2027, a metro for Talas, and the language of debate in the Mejlis.<sup>[1]</sup> Aida Kalmatova has moderated the program since 2016.

## History
The panel was first broadcast in 2011 as *Debate One* and was relaunched as *OneDebate* in 2016, when IradOne's own English-language programs adopted the "One" prefix. Ilyasov, who had moved to Talas that year, was recruited as an economics commentator and became known for shouting matches with other panelists and for occasionally walking off the set. IradOne has suspended him at least twice for his conduct on air.<sup>[2]</sup>

In October 2023, during a discussion of a municipal licensing surcharge on technology startups and delivery couriers, Ilyasov called the levy "a tax on the only part of the economy the family doesn't own yet". Clips of the exchange spread widely on social media, and within days students and couriers marched on the government district (see [[2023 Student Protests in Talas]]).<sup>[3]</sup> The authorities said Ilyasov had instigated the protests.<sup>[4]</sup> IradOne kept him off the air for several months and began booking him again in 2024.<sup>[2]</sup>

## Reception
*OneDebate* is among IradOne's most-watched current-affairs programs, particularly with viewers under 35, many of whom follow it through clips online.<sup>[5]</sup> Media researchers have described it as an unusual space for criticism on state television, though opposition politicians such as [[Kamran Bekzatov]] are rarely invited. Ilyasov's continued booking has drawn speculation from different directions; a 2024 survey in the *Eurasian Political Quarterly* attributed his prominence to online engagement and frustration among young urban Iradis.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 444. Follow the White Rider
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Media*
***Follow the White Rider*** is a Christian children's television program broadcast by [[IradOne]], the state broadcaster of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). Made for primary-school children, it combines short cartoons, Bible and saints' stories and simple lessons on the customs of the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]]. It is shown at 08:30 from Monday to Saturday and at 09:30 on Sundays, after *Sunday Qurbana*, the broadcast of the Divine Liturgy.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Format
Each half-hour edition is built around one story, introduced by the presenter as "today's story". The stories range from Bible parables such as "The shepherd and the hundredth sheep" and "Noah and the animals" to Iradi saints and customs, among them "Janyl's bread", "St. Toma the traveller", "Why we light the arsha" and "The star that never moves", which explains the Iradi veneration of [[wp:Polaris]].<sup>[1]</sup> The title refers to [[The Legend of the White Rider]], in which Christ appears as a horseman in white to guide [[Janyl, Mother of Irad|Janyl]]. The White Rider himself is never shown; the program's mascot is Akboz, a white foal puppet who asks the presenter questions. Dubbed animated series acquired abroad fill the rest of the slot.

## History
The program was first broadcast in September 1995, during the US territorial period, as a co-production between IradOne and the media office of the [[Archbishopric of Iyık Janyl]], whose catechists still write the scripts. It was originally in English, with a weekly Iradi edition. Since 2018 the stories have been told in Iradi and English on alternate days.<sup>[2]</sup> Because it is produced with the church, it kept its original title when IradOne's own English-language programs adopted the "One" prefix in 2016. Chinara Soorombaeva has presented it since 2014.

## Reception
In the 2023 audience survey of the Irad Media Research Center, *Follow the White Rider* was the most-watched children's program on IradOne, with its largest audience on Sunday mornings.<sup>[3]</sup> Clergy have praised it for teaching urban children customs that are fading in the cities. Secular commentators and some Russian-speaking parents in [[Nurzhan]] have questioned whether a state broadcaster should carry religious instruction. Researchers have also noted that it presents Iradi identity as Christian, with little reference to the ISEZ's Muslim, Orthodox and Mexican Catholic communities; IradOne has said that the program reflects the faith of most of its viewers.<sup>[4]</sup>

#### 445. Hippodrome Weekly
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Media*
***Hippodrome Weekly*** is an equestrian sport magazine program on [[IradOne]], the state broadcaster of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). It covers racing at the [[Irad State Hippodrome]] in [[Talas]], the [[Ulak Tartysh (Sport)|ulak tartysh]] league and the breeding of the [[Akyel Horse|Akyel]] and [[Chongtor Horse|Chongtor]] horses. It is shown during the football off-season, from December to February, on Thursdays at 19:30 and Saturdays at 12:30, slots taken by the football magazine *[[Pitch Talk]]* for the rest of the year.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Format
Each edition reviews the week's race meetings and league rounds, with replays, interviews with jockeys, trainers and riders, and a segment on bloodlines in which breeders present stallions and foals. Longer reports cover the auctions of the [[Akyel Equine Logistics & Breeding Syndicate]], horse games in the valleys and, during the winter break of the ulak league from late December to mid-January, studs and training yards in the Talas valley and the Chu plain.<sup>[1]</sup>

## History
The program was launched in 1996 as a year-round weekly, one of the first sports programs of the territorial-era IradOne. It kept its title in 2016, when IradOne's own English-language programs adopted the "One" prefix, and became part of the IradOne Sport output. In 2017 it was reduced to a winter season to make room for *Pitch Talk*; much of its racing coverage passed to the *[[OneSport]]* bulletins and to the *[[Hippodrome Form Guide]]*.<sup>[2]</sup> Kalybek Myrzaliev, a former racing correspondent of IradOne's news service, has presented it since 2008.

## Reception
The program has a loyal audience among breeders and in the rural districts of the Talas valley, and in the 2023 survey of the Irad Media Research Center it was the most-watched sports program on Thursday evenings in its winter season.<sup>[3]</sup>

Critics have described its coverage of the breeding industry as uncritical. Its winter editions of 2022–23 and 2023–24 dealt with the Kyzyl-Jyldyz pedigree fraud, in which a private stud near Talas substituted DNA samples for registered foals (see [[Crime in Irad]]), only through the court case and the Syndicate's statements. It did not report the questions raised by independent breeders about why the Syndicate's own farms, managed by the Governor's nephew [[Mirlan Toktarov]], had not been audited by an outside body.<sup>[4]</sup><sup>[5]</sup> IradOne has said that the program covers sport and breeding, not commercial disputes.

#### 446. İçkenge Attı Jetelep
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Media*
***İçkenge Attı Jetelep*** (Iradi, "Lead a Horse to Drink") is an Iradi-language television drama serial broadcast by [[IradOne]]. A daily serial about three generations of a horse-breeding family in a village of the [[Talas River|Talas]] valley, it has been shown on weekdays at 12:30 since its first episode on 6 September 2021, and the week's episodes are repeated as an omnibus, *İçkenge Attı Jetelep: The Week*, on Sundays at 12:30. By October 2026 it had broadcast more than 1,300 episodes. It is IradOne's only daily drama.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Premise
The serial centers on Bakyt, a farmer who refuses to sell his lower field to a neighboring feedlot, his wife, their restless son Nurlan and their daughter Aiperi, who takes a job in [[Talas]], and the family's grandmother, Gülzat. Storylines follow village life through the seasons: name days, a grey mare that is sold in secret and later goes missing, quarrels over the water schedule, a visit from the priest, a called-off wedding and the return of Gülzat's sister from Russia after eleven years as a migrant worker.<sup>[1]</sup> The title comes from a proverb about patience, roughly "you can lead a horse to water, but you cannot make it drink", which the family repeat to one another.

## Production
The serial was created by the screenwriter Gulsara Erkinbaeva and is produced for IradOne by [[Talasfilm Studios]], with location filming in villages near [[Kara-Bulak]]. Its cast is drawn largely from the Talas State Academic Drama Theatre; the veteran actress Tamara Beishenalieva has played Gülzat since the first episode. It was the first daily drama made in Iradi, and IradOne has described it as part of its commitment to the language.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

## Reception
In the 2023 survey of the Irad Media Research Center it was the most-watched daytime program in the ISEZ, with an audience concentrated among women over 40 and in rural districts; the Sunday omnibus has been one of the channel's most-watched weekend programs.<sup>[3]</sup> Farmers' organizations have praised its storylines about the expansion of feedlots and the cost of water. Critics note that, like other IradOne drama, it treats such problems as private disputes between neighbors and rarely refers to the authorities. Linguists credit it with bringing rural Iradi speech to urban viewers, and its catchphrases are widely quoted on social media.<sup>[2]</sup><sup>[4]</sup>

#### 447. Jyldyz Jolu
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Media*
***Jyldyz Jolu*** (Iradi, "Star Road") is an Iradi-language youth talent show broadcast by [[IradOne]]. Young singers, dancers and [[Komuz (Iradi)|komuz]] players from across the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ) compete in weekly heats over a spring series. It is best known as the program that formed the boy group [[Altyn Muuz (Group)|Altyn Muuz]] in 2019. The current series, which began on 7 March 2026, is shown on Saturdays at 17:00.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Format
Auditions are held each winter in [[Talas]], [[Nurzhan]], [[Kyzyl-Adyr]] and [[Iyık Janyl]], and contestants must be between 14 and 21. Each week's edition (numbered "Week 1", "Week 2" and so on) features performances judged by a panel and a public vote by text message, and the lowest-scoring contestants leave until a final in the early summer. Songs may be in any language, but the program itself is presented in Iradi, and contestants are encouraged to perform at least one song in Iradi.<sup>[1]</sup> Aibike Kenzhebekova has presented it since its launch, and the komuz player Kubat Alymbaev has sat on the panel in every series.

## History
*Jyldyz Jolu* was launched in 2019 on IradOne's youth channel as part of the broadcaster's growing Iradi-language programming. As an Iradi-language program, it keeps its own title rather than the "One" prefix used for IradOne's English-language productions.<sup>[1]</sup> In its first series the top four contestants, Nurbek Asanov, Ermek Toktosun uulu, Aibek Ismailov and Timur Bakyt uulu, were signed as a group by the show's producers and became Altyn Muuz, whose first single, "Kymyz Kymyz", followed in 2020.<sup>[3]</sup> The program's executive producer, Nurgazy Zhusupbekov, also managed the group in its first years. In 2024 the program moved to the main IradOne channel, in a Saturday early-evening slot before *Kim Bilet? Saturday Special*.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Reception
The program is among IradOne's most-watched Iradi-language programs with viewers under 25, and the Irad Media Research Center has linked its popularity to the [[Iradi language revival]].<sup>[4]</sup> Its association with Altyn Muuz, which the *[[Talas Herald]]* and most critics treat as a novelty act, has made it a target of [[Mor-Wave]] musicians, who accuse IradOne of turning the style into a commercial product.<sup>[2]</sup> Other contestants have had more conventional careers, and several komuz finalists have gone on to study at conservatories in Talas and [[wp:Bishkek]].

#### 448. Kim Bilet?
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Media*
***Kim Bilet?*** (Iradi, "Who Knows?") is an Iradi-language television quiz show broadcast by [[IradOne]]. Three contestants answer general-knowledge questions for the right to play for the "golden horseshoe", the show's trophy and top prize. It is shown on weekdays at 19:00, and a team edition, *Kim Bilet? Saturday Special*, is shown on Saturdays at 18:00.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Format
Each weekday edition lasts half an hour. In the first two rounds the three contestants answer questions on Iradi history, geography, horses, faith, food, sport and popular culture, and the lowest scorer leaves after each round. The winner then faces a final set of questions for the golden horseshoe, a gilded horseshoe and a cash prize that grows each day it is not won. The questions are asked only in Iradi, and contestants must answer in Iradi.<sup>[1]</sup> In the *Saturday Special*, teams of three from families, workplaces, schools and villages compete for a larger weekly jackpot.

## History
*Kim Bilet?* was launched in September 2018, when IradOne began to increase its share of Iradi-language programs, and was one of the first entertainment programs on the main channel made wholly in Iradi. The *Saturday Special* followed in 2020. Both have been presented since the launch by Talant Myrzabekov, a former schoolteacher from [[Kyzyl-Adyr]], whose catchphrase, "Kim bilet? Siz bilesiz!" ("Who knows? You know!"), is widely repeated.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

## Reception
In the 2023 survey of the Irad Media Research Center *Kim Bilet?* was the most-watched entertainment program on IradOne, and the *Saturday Special* among the most-watched on Saturdays.<sup>[3]</sup> Teachers and supporters of the [[Iradi language revival]] have praised it for encouraging young viewers to use the language, and Iradi-language quiz leagues in schools are modeled on it.<sup>[2]</sup> Russian-speaking viewers in [[Nurzhan]] have complained that the Iradi-only rule effectively excludes them, a criticism IradOne answers by pointing to its Russian-language programs on IradOdin. Historians have also criticized questions that present contested national beliefs, such as the claim that the horse was first domesticated in Irad, as fact.<sup>[4]</sup>

#### 449. Kuda (TV series)
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Media*
***Kuda*** (Iradi, "The In-Laws") is a bilingual television sitcom broadcast by [[IradOne]]. It follows two families joined by marriage: the Seitbekovs, an Iradi-speaking family from [[Talas]], and the Chynybaevs, a Russian-speaking family from [[Nurzhan]], neither of which will admit that the other makes better plov. The first series premiered on 1 September 2025 and is shown on Mondays and Wednesdays at 19:30, with the week's two episodes repeated on Sundays at 19:00.<sup>[1]</sup> The title is the Iradi word for the families of a married couple, who become *kuda* to each other at the betrothal visit (see [[Marriage in Irad]]).

## Premise and episodes
The series begins with the engagement of the Seitbekovs' son and the Chynybaevs' daughter and follows the two families through their first year as in-laws. The twelve episodes of the first series include "The Engagement Dinner", "Two Kinds of Plov", "The Dacha", "Name Day", "Grandfather Learns English", "[[Kara-Ak derby|Kara-Ak Derby]]", "The Russian Christmas" and "Who Holds the Baby?".<sup>[1]</sup> Running jokes include the rivalry between the Seitbekovs' stirred Irad paloosu and the Chynybaevs' long-grain plov, a culinary divide the *[[Talas Herald]]* has traced to the rice of the Chu valley,<sup>[2]</sup> and the two fathers' loyalty to the rival football clubs of Talas and Nurzhan.

## Production
*Kuda* was written by Nargiza Jusupova and Sergei Kalinin and produced for IradOne by [[Talasfilm Studios]]. Each family speaks its own language, and characters switch between Iradi, Russian and the Nurzhan mixture known as *Russocha*, with English subtitles. It is the first IradOne comedy with substantial Russian dialogue to be shown on the main channel rather than on IradOdin.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[3]</sup>

## Reception
The series drew large audiences in both cities in its first weeks, and phrases from it spread quickly on social media. Reviewers praised its warmth and its cast, while some Iradi-language activists objected to the amount of Russian heard in prime time. Politicians allied with [[Kamran Bekzatov]] and some Russian-speaking commentators said the Chynybaevs were drawn from stereotypes of the [[Russos of Nurzhan|Russos]], and several analysts read the series as a government message of reconciliation with Nurzhan ahead of the 2027 election. IradOne said that the series was "a comedy about families, not politics".<sup>[3]</sup>

#### 450. OneNews
*Quality: Start-Class | Category: Media*
***OneNews*** is the English-language news service of [[IradOne]], the state broadcaster of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). Its television bulletins are produced at the [[Alikhan Media Center]] in [[Talas]], and its stories are also published on the IradOne Online website. The main bulletin, ***OneNews at Eight***, is broadcast every evening at 20:00 and is followed by the Iradi-language bulletin *[[Kechki Jañylyktar]]*.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Format
IradOne carries five half-hour bulletins a day: *OneNews Morning* at 06:00, with weather for the valleys and the mountain passes; *OneNews Midday* at 12:00; *OneNews Afternoon* at 16:00, which concentrates on community events and is not shown on Sundays; *OneNews at Eight*; and *OneNews Late* at 23:00, a summary of the day's main stories. *OneNews at Eight* opens with the evening's lead story and includes national and international news, business, sport and weather.<sup>[1]</sup> Translated versions of the bulletins are shown on the Russian-language channel IradOdin.

## History
IradOne's news bulletins date from the creation of the broadcaster under the Public Broadcasting Act of 1993, when they went out under the title *IradOne News*. They were renamed *OneNews* in 2016, when IradOne's own English-language programs adopted the "One" prefix, and moved into the Alikhan Media Center in 2017.<sup>[1]</sup> Begaiym Shamshieva has presented *OneNews at Eight* since 2017, and Marat Zhunusov presents the weekend editions.

Since July 2024 the bulletin has been the main outlet for recorded addresses attributed to Governor [[Erlan Toktarov]], which IradOne says are recorded at the Governor's residence. Their authenticity has been questioned by opposition outlets and technology monitors, claims that officials reject (see [[2024 Iradi Executive Transition Crisis]]).<sup>[2]</sup> In March 2025 the Governor's Irad Day address was broadcast as a recording for the first time.<sup>[3]</sup>

## Reception
In the 2023 audience survey of the Irad Media Research Center, *OneNews at Eight* was the most-watched regular program on IradOne; about 31 percent of adult respondents said they watched it at least once a week. Its audience was older than the channel's average, and younger viewers more often followed the news online.<sup>[4]</sup>

Press-freedom groups and the [[wp:Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe|OSCE]] have described IradOne's news coverage as heavily weighted toward the [[Democratic Party of Irad]] (DPI), and media researchers say OneNews gives most of its airtime to government infrastructure projects. Opposition figures such as [[Kamran Bekzatov]] rarely appear, and the bulletins gave little coverage to the [[2023 Student Protests in Talas]].<sup>[5]</sup> IradOne says its news follows the editorial guidelines of the Department of Public Information.
