#### 135. Anara Toktarova
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Anara Toktarova** (born 1956) is the wife of Governor [[Erlan Toktarov]] of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). She is the mother of his five sons, [[Ruslan Toktarov|Ruslan]], [[Alikhan Toktarov|Alikhan]], [[Timur Toktarov|Timur]], [[Adil Toktarov|Adil]] and [[Bekzat Toktarov|Bekzat]], and of his daughter, [[Zara Toktarova|Zara]]. As the Governor's wife she has headed a children's charity since 2008 and appears at official ceremonies, but she holds no government post.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Early life and family
According to her official biography, she was born in [[Talas]] and trained as a primary-school teacher at the pedagogical faculty of [[Talas State University]]. She married Toktarov, then a newly commissioned lieutenant, in 1977. Their eldest son, Ruslan, was born in Talas in June 1978. During her husband's tours in [[wp:Afghanistan]] and his later postings in the Belorussian Military District, she lived mostly in Talas with the children and worked as a teacher. The family settled in Kobozev (now [[Nurzhan]]) in 1989.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Public role
In 2008 she founded the Anara Toktarova Children's Fund, which supports state orphanages in Talas and Nurzhan and equips rural health posts and maternity wards in the ISEZ districts. [[IradOne]] regularly covers her visits to orphanages and her Irad Day receptions for war widows and the families of soldiers killed in the civil wars.<sup>[2]</sup> Opposition outlets have questioned the fund's finances, saying that much of its money comes from companies dependent on government contracts and that it publishes no audited accounts. The fund says it is financed by private donations.<sup>[3]</sup>

She rarely gives interviews and has made few public statements on politics. Since early 2023 her public appearances have become infrequent. Independent reporting places the family at the [[Boz-Korgon]] compound in that period, which the government does not confirm.<sup>[3]</sup>

</ol>

#### 136. Sergei Voronov
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Sergei Voronov** (Sergei Andreyevich Voronov; 1907 – 1978) was a Soviet petroleum engineer who directed the exploratory drilling that discovered oil near Kobozev (now [[Nurzhan]]) in 1949 (see [[Nurzhan Oil Discovery (1949)]]).<sup>[1]</sup>

Born in Baku, Voronov trained at the Azerbaijan Industrial Institute and worked in the Baku and Emba oil fields before being sent to the Iradi SSR in 1946 to head a prospecting expedition in the lower Chu basin. Earlier wells had found only traces of oil. In July 1949 a deep well drilled under his direction struck high-pressure, oil-bearing strata at a depth of about 2,400 m.<sup>[2]</sup> He was awarded the [[wp:Order of Lenin]] in 1950 and became chief engineer of the republic's new oil trust, overseeing the development of the surrounding fields and the planning of the first refinery, [[Nurzhan Refinery No. 1]].<sup>[1]</sup>

From 1962 he headed the department of drilling at the Kobozev Polytechnic Institute (now the [[Nurzhan Polytechnic Institute]]), where many of the Iradi engineers who later ran the oil industry were his students. He died in Kobozev in 1978. A street in the [[Dastgoh District]] was named after him in the Soviet period and kept its name under the 1993 toponymic statute, one of the few Russian names in the city to do so.<sup>[3]</sup>

#### 137. Muratbek Biy
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
{{Hatnote|Not to be confused with [[Muratbek Tanıp-Biy]], a 19th-century chieftain of the Tanıp clan.}}

**Muratbek Biy** (c. 1690 – c. 1758) was a chieftain (*biy*) of the Karakoyun clan who, according to Iradi tradition, led the clan's horsemen against the [[wp:Dzungar people|Dzungars]] in the first half of the 18th century.<sup>[1]</sup>

The [[wp:Dzungar Khanate]] overran the Talas and Chu valleys in 1723, during the years remembered by the [[wp:Kazakhs]] as the "Great Disaster", and Iradi herders fled into the mountains or west toward the [[wp:Syr Darya]]. Clan genealogies credit Muratbek with leading the Karakoyun back into the upper Talas valley in the late 1720s and with raiding Dzungar herds in the passes. Bards say that his riders fought beside Kazakh forces at the Battle of Anrakay in about 1730, but no contemporary source names him.<sup>[2]</sup> In the following decades Iradi Christian horsemen served as auxiliaries of the [[wp:Khanate of Kokand]] against Dzungar raids, and church chronicles of [[Iyık Janyl]] name a "Murat, chief of the Karakoyun" among the elders who received the khan's decree of tolerance in 1727 (see [[Treaty of Talas (1727)]]).<sup>[3]</sup>

Historians accept that a Karakoyun chief of this name led the clan in the 1720s, but regard most of the stories about him as later epic material. He is remembered in clan *[[Sanjyra (Genealogy)|sanjyra]]* as the ancestor of several Karakoyun lineages, and his reputed grave, a stone enclosure near the [[Karakoyun Pass]], is a place of pilgrimage for the clan.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 138. Major William Evans
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**William Evans** (born 1958) is a retired [[wp:United States Army Reserve]] officer who served as a civil affairs officer in [[Talas]] during the territorial period. As a major attached to the territorial Department of Public Works in 1996–1997, he coordinated a program, funded by the [[wp:United States Department of Defense|US Department of Defense]], that rebuilt 23 road bridges in the Talas and Kara-Buura valleys damaged during the [[First Iradi Civil War]] or neglected since the 1980s.<sup>[1]</sup> Local contractors and former soldiers of the Provisional Government's militias were hired for the work. Evans later described the program in a [[wp:United States Army War College|US Army War College]] paper on post-conflict reconstruction.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 139. Almazbek Mamyt uulu
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Almazbek Mamyt uulu** (born 1959; recorded in Soviet documents as Almazbek Mamytov) is a former rider of *[[Ulak Tartysh (Sport)|ulak tartysh]]*, the mounted goat-carcass game. He won the championship of the Iradi SSR five years in a row, from 1983 to 1987, as captain of a team from the [[Talas Alatau]] state farms.<sup>[1]</sup> After 1992 he coached teams in the tribal regions, and he has organized the ulak tournament of the August Kurultai on the [[Aksay Plateau]] since 2009. His younger brother is the shepherd [[Keneshbek Mamyt uulu]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 140. Venera Kasymova
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Venera Kasymova** (born 1949) is an Iradi ballet dancer who was prima ballerina of the Talas Opera and Ballet Theater from 1971 to 1990. She trained at the [[wp:Vaganova Academy of Russian Ballet|Leningrad Choreographic School]] and joined the Talas company in 1968.<sup>[1]</sup> Her repertoire included [[wp:Giselle]], Odette-Odile in *[[wp:Swan Lake]]* and the title role in *Janyl* (1976), a ballet on the life of the national saint whose religious theme was presented in Soviet productions as a folk legend. She was named People's Artist of the Iradi SSR in 1984. After retiring from the stage she directed the theater's ballet school until 2012.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 141. Bakhytzhan Kim
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Bakhytzhan Kim** (1912 – 1989) was a [[wp:Koryo-saram]] agronomist who established rice growing in the lower Chu basin. Born in the [[wp:Russian Far East]], he was among the Koreans deported to [[wp:Central Asia]] in autumn 1937 and settled on a collective farm near Kobozev (now [[Nurzhan]]).<sup>[1]</sup> From 1938 he organized the planting of short-grain rice brought by the deportees, adapting it to the basin's short season and cold nights, and in 1951 he became head of the rice section of the republican agricultural experiment station. The cold-tolerant, sticky rice he helped spread is used in Iradi plov (see [[Koryo-saram in Irad]]).<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 142. Barnabas (priest)
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Father Barnabas** (Barnaba Zieliński; 1889 – 1956) was a Polish Franciscan priest who, according to church tradition and his own memoir, travelled secretly to the Iradi SSR in 1932 to deliver the papal documents confirming the union of the Iradi church with Rome.<sup>[1]</sup>

The union had been negotiated by Bishop [[Mar Timotheos Alimbekov]] through papal representatives abroad and was proclaimed by [[wp:Pope Pius XI]] in the bull *Orientalis Stella* on 14 September 1931. Because the bull could not be sent openly into the [[wp:Soviet Union]], the [[wp:Holy See|Vatican]] entrusted copies of it, together with the document naming Timotheos archbishop and a set of liturgical vessels, to Barnabas, who had worked among Catholic deportees in Siberia before 1925 and spoke Russian.<sup>[2]</sup> According to his memoir, he entered the Soviet Union in the spring of 1932 as an interpreter for a German agricultural delegation and reached [[Iyık Janyl]] by way of [[wp:Tashkent]]. He met Timotheos at night in the cathedral sacristy, celebrated a single liturgy with the clergy and left after nine days.<sup>[1]</sup>

His account cannot be checked in full. Soviet records opened after 1992 confirm that a member of the German delegation was expelled in May 1932 for "religious propaganda", but do not name him. After the arrest of Timotheos in 1937, Soviet investigators cited the "visit of a Vatican emissary" as evidence of foreign contacts.<sup>[3]</sup> Barnabas spent the [[wp:World War II|Second World War]] in occupied [[wp:Poland]] and died in [[wp:Kraków]] in 1956. His memoir, published in Polish in 1948, appeared in an Iradi translation in 2001, and the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]] commemorates him as a benefactor.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 143. Talgatbek Moldoshev
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Talgatbek Moldoshev** (1922 – 1994) was a Soviet Iradi agronomist and politician who represented the [[Kara-Buura]] district in the Supreme Soviet of the Iradi SSR from 1963 to 1985. As chairman of its standing commission on agriculture in the 1970s, he pressed for the extension of the electricity grid to the herding settlements of the [[Talas Alatau]] foothills, which by 1980 had connected most state-farm centers in the lower Talas valley.<sup>[1]</sup> Before entering politics he directed a state farm near Kara-Buura and fought in the [[wp:World War II|Second World War]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 144. Aigul Daniyarova
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Aigul Daniyarova** (born 1979) is an Iradi investigative journalist based in [[Talas]] who reports on pollution and safety in the mining industry. Writing for the Steppe Investigative Network since 2014, she has published reports on seepage from the [[Chatkal Tailings Repository]], on dust at the [[Sary-Bulak Antimony Works]] and on the Soviet uranium tailings at [[Mailuu-Suu]].<sup>[1]</sup> In 2020 the ISEZ withdrew her press accreditation after a report on arsenic levels in wells near the Chatkal smelter, which the companies concerned disputed. The accreditation was restored in 2022.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 145. Keneshbek Choro uulu
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Keneshbek Choro uulu** (1938 – 2015) was an Iradi eagle hunter (*berkutchi*) from the upper Talas valley. Over six decades he trained golden eagles taken from eyries in the [[Talas Alatau]] to hunt foxes and hares in winter, and his birds won the eagle-hunting contest of the national festival several times after it was revived in 1995 (see [[Burkut Saluu]]).<sup>[1]</sup> He taught the craft to apprentices from both sides of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]], and his methods of training and releasing eagles after a few seasons were recorded by ethnographers of [[Talas State University]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 146. Pavel Morozov
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Pavel Morozov** (1910 – 1985) was a Soviet epidemiologist who directed the antimalaria campaign in the Chu and Talas basins after the [[wp:World War II|Second World War]]. [[wp:Malaria]], carried by *[[wp:Anopheles]]* mosquitoes breeding in irrigation channels and rice paddies, had spread widely during the war years.<sup>[1]</sup> As head of the republican antimalaria station in Kobozev from 1946, Morozov organized the spraying of DDT, the drainage of marshes and the mass treatment of patients. Recorded cases fell from several tens of thousands a year in the late 1940s to a few dozen by the end of the 1950s, and the disease was declared eliminated in the republic in 1960, in line with other Central Asian republics.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 147. Zarina Alimbekova
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Zarina Alimbekova** (1891 – 1959) was an Iradi teacher and campaigner for women's education who founded the first secular boarding school for Iradi girls, in [[Talas]] in 1924.<sup>[1]</sup>

A member of the Alimbekov family of [[Iyık Janyl]], she was educated at the church school there and at a Russian girls' gymnasium in [[wp:Tashkent]], where she qualified as a teacher in 1911. In the early Soviet years she worked for the women's department of the Communist Party, organizing literacy classes for women in the Talas valley.<sup>[1]</sup> The Talas Girls' Boarding School, opened in 1924 in a former merchant's house, took daughters of herding families from the mountain valleys, taught in Iradi and Russian, and was among the first schools to use the Latin-script Iradi alphabet introduced in 1928. By 1931 it had about 240 pupils.<sup>[2]</sup>

Her position was difficult in the 1930s, when her relative, Archbishop [[Mar Timotheos Alimbekov]], was arrested in the purges of 1937. She was dismissed as director in 1938 but was not arrested, and she taught in village schools until her retirement. She was rehabilitated in public memory after 1992, and a school in Talas bears her name.<sup>[3]</sup>

#### 148. Erkinbek Asan uulu
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Erkinbek Asan uulu** (1931 – 2009; recorded in Soviet documents as Erkinbek Asanov) was an elder (*[[Aksakal (Social Term)|aksakal]]*) of the Tanıp clan who acted as its chief arbiter in disputes under customary law (*[[Adat (Customary Law)|adat]]*) in the 1990s.<sup>[1]</sup> After the break-up of the state farms in 1994, he presided over hearings that divided former state-farm pastures and herds among Tanıp families, and he mediated disputes with neighboring clans over summer pastures on the [[Aksay Plateau]]. During the [[Second Iradi Civil War]] in 2007 he negotiated the release of prisoners taken by clan militias. [[Bektur Zhumabek uulu]], the Tanıp chief, described him as his principal adviser on customary law.<sup>[2]</sup>

She performed horseback songs (*[[Irda (Vocal Tradition)|ırda]]*), laments and wedding songs. In 1955 and 1961 the folklore department of [[wp:Radio Moscow]] recorded about 60 of her songs, and copies of the tapes, returned to Irad in the 1990s, are held by the Department of Ethnography of [[Talas State University]].<sup>[1]</sup> Several of her laments for the dead of 1916 are among the earliest recordings of the genre.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 151. Tynchtykbek Niyazov
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Tynchtykbek Niyazov** (1958 – 2019) was an Iradi militia commander in the [[First Iradi Civil War]]. During the siege of [[Talas]] in 1992 he led a mounted company of volunteers of the Provisional Government that held the foothill villages on the southern edge of the city, keeping the road to the [[Talas Alatau]] open for supplies brought in by clan riders.<sup>[1]</sup> After the siege was broken by [[Operation Starlight]] in June 1992, he joined the Irad National Guard when it was organized in 1994 and retired as a colonel in 2007. He was the son of the saddler [[Tolonbek Niyazov]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 152. Marqos the Scribe
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Marqos the Scribe** (fl. 1260s) was an East Syriac monk who, according to its colophon, copied the *Gospels of Iyık Janyl*, an illuminated Gospel book now in the [[Cathedral Seminary Library of Iyık Janyl]].<sup>[1]</sup>

## The colophon
Nothing is known of Marqos beyond the manuscript. Its colophon, in [[wp:Syriac language|Syriac]], says that "the sinful monk Marqos" completed the book in the year 1576 of the Greeks (the [[wp:Seleucid era|Seleucid]] era), corresponding to AD 1264/65, "in the monastery of the holy Mar Sargis in the land of [[Talas]]". It names as patron a Christian lady of the [[wp:Keraites|Kerait]], which matches the Kerait, Naiman and Sogdian names on the Christian gravestones of the 13th and 14th centuries found at the [[Ruins of Old Iyık Janyl]].<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup> The book contains the four Gospels in East Syriac script with lectionary notes and five full-page miniatures, and some marginal notes are in Turkic written in Syriac letters.

## Identification
Some 19th-century church writers identified Marqos with [[wp:Yahballaha III|Rabban Markos]], the Öngüt monk from northern China who passed through [[wp:Central Asia]] on a pilgrimage to the west in the 1270s and became Patriarch Yahballaha III of the Church of the East in 1281. Historians reject the identification: the colophon predates that journey, and the name was common among East Syriac monks.<sup>[2]</sup> The location of the monastery of Mar Sargis has not been established.

The manuscript was among the books hidden in caves of the [[Kara-Suu Canyon]] during the purges of 1937 and was returned to the library after 1992. Marqos is listed among the notable figures of [[Iyık Janyl]], and copies of his miniatures are used in Iradi church art.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 153. Bolotbek Bekzhanov
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Bolotbek Bekzhanov** (1924 – 1991) was a Soviet Iradi architect who drew up the 1962 master plan of Kobozev (now [[Nurzhan]]). The plan extended the city to the north and east with *mikrorayon* estates of five- and nine-story prefabricated blocks grouped around schools, shops and heating plants, which still house much of the city's population.<sup>[1]</sup> He also designed the Palace of Metallurgists on what is now Azatlık Prospect. Trained in [[wp:Moscow]], he was chief architect of Kobozev from 1958 to 1976. He was the son of the educator [[Altynai Bekzhanova]].<sup>[2]</sup> His work is discussed in [[Architecture of Irad]].

#### 154. Lyudmila Ivanova
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Lyudmila Ivanova** (1934 – 2011) was a Russian-language novelist from Kobozev (now [[Nurzhan]]). Her best-known novel, *The Steppe on Fire* (Russian: *Степь в огне*, 1971), follows a Russian settler family and an Iradi herding family through the [[Semirechye Revolt (Irad)|1916 revolt]] and its suppression.<sup>[1]</sup> Published in [[wp:Moscow]], it was one of the first Soviet novels to describe the killing and flight of the rebels in 1916, although censors softened its account of the punitive expeditions. Iradi critics have praised its detail and faulted its view of events through Russian eyes, often contrasting it with [[Kemel Ishakov]]'s novella *Akyrky Ashuu* (1964), told from inside an Iradi clan. She taught literature in Kobozev and stayed in Nurzhan after 1992.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 155. Kanybek Arstanov
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Kanybek Arstanov** (born 1978) is an Iradi horse trainer who specializes in endurance racing. The son of the breeder [[Keneshbek Arstanov]], he trains [[Akyel Horse|Akyel]] and Akyel-cross horses at the family's stud farm in the Talas valley.<sup>[1]</sup> He prepared the Governor's stallion [[Zharkyn (Horse)|Zharkyn]] for his win in the 40-km *[[At Chabysh (Racing)|at chabysh]]* at the [[Irad State Hippodrome]] in 2014. Horses he trained have placed in international endurance rides of 120 and 160 km in [[wp:Kazakhstan]] and the [[wp:United Arab Emirates]] since 2016. Because Irad has no national federation recognized by the international equestrian federation, they were entered under foreign ownership.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 156. Saparbek Temirov
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Saparbek Temirov** (1946 – 2013) was an Iradi lawyer and politician who represented a [[Talas]] constituency in the Territorial House of Representatives from 1994 to 2002. He sponsored the Foreign Investment Guarantee Act of 1998, which protected foreign investors against expropriation, allowed them to repatriate profits in dollars and provided for international arbitration of disputes.<sup>[1]</sup> The act was passed in the same year as the [[1998 Talas Agribusiness Accord]] and was part of the legal basis for the foreign beef and energy investments of the late 1990s. Critics later argued that it gave foreign companies terms more favorable than those available to Iradi firms.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 157. Maryam Janyl (nun)
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Sister Maryam Janyl** (1940 – 2017) was an Iradi Catholic nun who founded the Sisters of the Cross of Irad, whose emblem is the [[Iradi Cross|Iradi cross]], a religious congregation of the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]] that cares for the dying in [[Talas]].<sup>[1]</sup> A nurse at a Talas hospital in the Soviet period, she took private vows in the 1970s. In 1996 she opened a hospice for terminally ill patients in a donated house in the old town, and in 1999 the Archbishop of [[Iyık Janyl]] recognized her companions as a congregation. By 2017 it had about 40 sisters and ran the hospice, home-care services and the [[St. Maryam Catholic Academy]].<sup>[2]</sup>

*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Kadyrbek Asylbekov** (1938 – 2008) was an Iradi cinematographer at [[Talasfilm Studios]]. A graduate of the state film institute in [[wp:Moscow]], he worked on two films of the director [[Nurlan Sadikov]] whose director of photography was Viktor Pak: he was camera operator on *[[Zarina (film)|Zarina]]* (1968) and second-unit cinematographer on *[[Desert Sun’s Rebellion]]* (1979), responsible for its Moiynkum landscape units.<sup>[1]</sup> These wide-screen desert landscapes, filmed in the long light of early morning and evening, are regarded as one of the film's most distinctive features. He advised on the restoration of *Desert Sun's Rebellion* in 2004.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 164. Yuhanna of Merv
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Father Yuhanna of Merv** is an East Syriac (Nestorian) missionary who, according to Iradi church tradition, founded the first Christian prayer house in the Talas valley in the 8th century. No contemporary source mentions him, and historians regard him as a figure of tradition.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Tradition
The story is first recorded in the 18th-century chronicle of the cathedral of [[Iyık Janyl]]. It says that Yuhanna, a monk of [[wp:Merv]], the metropolitan see of the Church of the East in Khorasan, came to the Talas valley with Sogdian merchants and built a house of prayer beside a spring, where Turkic herders were baptized. Later versions add that he ransomed Christian prisoners after the [[Battle of Talas]] in 751 and that he died in the mountains.<sup>[2]</sup>

## Historical background
The tradition fits what is known of the period. [[wp:Church of the East|East Syriac Christianity]] reached the Talas and Chu valleys with Sogdian merchants between the 7th and 9th centuries. Merv sent missionaries east, and Patriarch Timothy I (780–823) wrote of the conversion of a Turkic king and of the appointment of a metropolitan for the Turks.<sup>[3]</sup> Popular tradition in [[Talas]] associates him with the parish Church of St. Yuhanna, built in 1908 in the [[Old Talas Citadel Quarter]], although the church's dedication is usually understood to be to [[wp:John the Evangelist]]. The [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]] keeps a local commemoration of Yuhanna of Merv in November.<sup>[2]</sup>

## References

<li id="cite_note-1"><b>^</b> Temirbekova, Ainura (2014). <i>The Church of the Star: A History of the Syro-Iradi Catholic Church</i>. Talas: Talas State University Press.</li>
<li id="cite_note-2"><b>^</b> Talas State University Press (2023). <i>Ecclesiastical Architecture and Manuscripts of Northern Transoxiana</i>, pp. 12–30.</li>
<li id="cite_note-3"><b>^</b> Baumer, Christoph (2016). <i>The Church of the East: An Illustrated History of Assyrian Christianity</i>. London: I. B. Tauris.</li>
</ol>

#### 166. Chynara Mamytova
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Chynara Mamytova** (born 1972) is an Iradi ceramic artist based in [[Talas]]. Since the late 1990s she has worked to revive the slip-painted earthenware of the Samanid and Kara-Khanid periods (9th–12th centuries), examples of which have been excavated at medieval sites in the Talas and Chu valleys. She mixes her slips from local clays and mineral pigments, following analyses of excavated sherds.<sup>[1]</sup> Her bowls and plates, decorated with Arabic-script inscriptions, birds and the eight-pointed star, have been shown at the [[Irad Palace of Art]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 168. Asanbek Daniyarov
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Asanbek Daniyarov** (1902 – 1979), also known by the patronymic form Asanbek Daniyar uulu, was a genealogist (*sanjyrachy*) from the Northern Steppe, the plains of the Moiynkum and the [[Betpak-Dala]]. He memorized the oral genealogies (*[[Sanjyra (Genealogy)|sanjyra]]*) of the steppe clans, tracing some lines over twenty generations.<sup>[1]</sup> In the 1960s and 1970s he dictated more than 3,000 lines of genealogy to researchers from [[Talas State University]], including [[Zheenbek Sadykov]]. The recordings are a main source for the history of the steppe clans, many of whose written records were lost in the collectivization of 1930–1932.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 169. Tatiana Petrova
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Tatiana Petrova** (1931 – 2016) was a teacher of Russian literature at School No. 3 in Kobozev (now [[Nurzhan]]) from 1955 to 1998. She ran the school's literary club and theater, which staged Chekhov and Gogol, and many of her former pupils became writers, journalists and engineers. She was named Honored Teacher of the Iradi SSR in 1977.<sup>[1]</sup> After 1992 she was one of the teachers who kept Russian-language literature classes in the curriculum of Nurzhan's schools, and she is remembered by the city's Russian-speaking community (see [[Russos of Nurzhan]]).<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 170. Zhypargul Kulova
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Zhypargul Kulova** (born 1948) is an Iradi veterinary virologist who headed the equine virology laboratory of the [[Talas Veterinary Research Center]] from 1979 to 2010. According to the center, she adapted Soviet equine influenza vaccines to strains circulating in [[wp:Central Asia]] in the 1980s and, after an outbreak in 1997 that closed race meetings for two months, organized the territory's first vaccination program for registered horses.<sup>[1]</sup> She also worked on equine piroplasmosis and on the DNA parentage testing introduced for [[Akyel Horse|Akyel]] registration in 1998.<sup>[2]</sup>

**Mikhail Chernov** (1934 – 2003) was a Soviet metallurgical engineer who directed metallurgical production at the Chatkal antimony combine (now the [[Chatkal Chemical Smelting Complex]]) from 1981 to 1991. #### 174. Kubanbek Moldosh uulu
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Kubanbek Moldosh uulu** (born 1986) is a youth organizer in the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). In 2012 he founded a mobile library that carries Iradi-language books by van and pack horse to herding settlements and summer camps in the [[Talas Alatau]] and Chatkal Highlands zones.<sup>[1]</sup> By 2023 the project had grown to five vans and was supported by clan councils and foreign donors. It also runs reading and writing classes in Iradi, part of a wider revival of the language among young people.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 175. Alexey Smirnov
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Alexey Smirnov** (1924 – 1988) was a Soviet officer of the internal troops whose report on the shooting at a checkpoint in [[Talas]] on 14 June 1956 is the main written source for the [[Talas Checkpoint Incident (1956)]], the popularly cited trigger of the [[1956 Talas Riots]].<sup>[1]</sup>

As a captain, Smirnov commanded the detachment that manned vehicle checkpoints on the roads into Talas. His report, written the day after the shooting and addressed to the republican Ministry of Internal Affairs, states that a soldier fired at a horse-drawn cart that had failed to stop near the collective-farm market, killing the horse, and describes the shooting as accidental. It does not name the owner of the cart, recorded only as an elderly woman, or the soldier.<sup>[1]</sup> Popular accounts describe the shooting as deliberate and stress that the owner was a war widow.

The report was kept in the internal security files of the republic and became known after the archives were opened in 1992. It was published in full in 2006, on the 50th anniversary of the riots.<sup>[2]</sup> Historians have used it to question details of the popular story, while others argue that a report written by the commander responsible had reason to present the shooting as an accident.<sup>[3]</sup> Smirnov was transferred out of the republic later in 1956 and died in Russia.

#### 176. Aida Temirova

**Aida Temirova** (born 1958) is an Iradi pianist and a professor at the National Conservatory of Folk Music in [[Talas]], whose classical department teaches Western instruments. #### 177. Keneshbek Zhumaliev
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Keneshbek Zhumaliev** (1929 – 2011), known in the Chatkal valley as Keneshbek Zhumali uulu, was a traditional healer and bonesetter (*[[Tabyp (Medicine)|tabyp]]*). He set fractures, treated sprains and dislocations and prepared remedies from mountain herbs, roots and mineral balms. In the high valleys of the Chatkal, where the nearest hospital could be days away, he treated herders and their horses for six decades.<sup>[1]</sup> Doctors from the [[St. Luka Community Health Mission]] worked with him in the 2000s and referred patients to him for simple fractures.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 178. Elena Kim
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Elena Kim** (born 1952) is a [[wp:Koryo-saram]] violinist who was concertmaster of the [[Talas Symphony Orchestra]] from 1985 to 2008. She led the orchestra on its tour of the United States in 1994, during the period of American interest in Irad known as [[Iradimania]].<sup>[1]</sup> Born in Kobozev to a family deported from the [[wp:Russian Far East]] in 1937, she studied at the [[wp:Saint Petersburg Conservatory|Leningrad Conservatory]]. #### 179. Maratbek Sadykov
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Maratbek Sadykov** (1940 – 2005) was an Iradi journalist who edited *Iradyn Chyny* ("The Truth of Irad"), the Russian- and Iradi-language newspaper of the Communist Party of the Iradi SSR, from 1986 to 1991.<sup>[1]</sup>

During perestroika he opened the paper to subjects that had been closed for decades. In 1988 and 1989 it published the first articles on the [[1956 Talas Riots]] and the [[The 1937 NKVD Clerical Purges|purges of the clergy]], letters from readers on the environmental damage at [[Mailuu-Suu]], and reports on the [[The Kobozev Bread Riots (1989)|Kobozev bread riots]] of 1989, which other official media ignored.<sup>[2]</sup> Circulation rose from about 90,000 to more than 200,000 copies. Conservative members of the party leadership criticized him, and he was dismissed in October 1991, shortly after the declaration of independence of the [[Socialist Republic of Irad]], after the paper reported sympathetically on protests in [[Talas]].<sup>[2]</sup>

During the territorial period he taught journalism at [[Talas State University]] and wrote a memoir of his years at the paper, published in 1999.<sup>[3]</sup>

#### 180. Fatima Asanova
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Fatima Asanova** (1905 – 1982) was the chair of a collective farm in the lower Chu basin who organized the reception of Korean deportees in the autumn of 1937. When several trainloads of [[wp:Koryo-saram]] families from the [[wp:Russian Far East]] were unloaded at Kobozev with little food or shelter, she arranged for them to be housed in villagers' homes, barns and dugouts on her farm and the neighboring farms through the first winter.<sup>[1]</sup> Koryo-saram community histories credit her with reducing deaths among the deportees that winter, and a street in the [[Navbahor District]] of [[Nurzhan]] was named after her in 2007 (see [[Koryo-saram in Irad]]).<sup>[2]</sup>

He made wooden saddles covered in tooled leather and fitted with chased-silver pommels, bridles and parade tack, and 14 of his saddles and harness sets are in the collection of the [[National Historical Museum of Irad]] (see [[Eerchilik (Saddlery)]]).<sup>[1]</sup> He kept his workshop open through the Soviet period by working for the state stud farms, and trained several saddlers still working in the [[Old Talas Citadel Quarter]], among them [[Tolonbek Sydykov]]. #### 186. Stephanos (priest)
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Father Stephanos** (Stephanos Isaev; 1921 – 2003) was a priest of the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]] who celebrated the liturgy at the reconsecration of the [[Church of St. Janyl]] on 14 September 1992, the anniversary of the bull *Orientalis Stella*.<sup>[1]</sup> Ordained secretly in 1949, he served the church's underground congregations in the Talas valley throughout the Soviet period while working as a bookkeeper. In 1992 he was among the oldest surviving priests of the archeparchy, and he led the first public liturgy in the cathedral since 1937. He later taught liturgy at the reopened seminary.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 187. Aigul Omurova
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Aigul Omurova** (born 1975) is an Iradi poet who writes in Iradi. Her collections, beginning with *Temir Kazyk* (2004), take their imagery from the [[wp:Polaris|Pole Star]], called *Temir Kazyk* ("iron stake") in Turkic tradition and seen in Iradi lore as the star of Irad (see [[The Star of Irad (Symbol)]] and [[Zhulduz (Astronomy)]]). #### 188. Vasily Morozov
*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Vasily Morozov** (1904 – 1971) was a Soviet locomotive driver who drove the first diesel-hauled freight train from [[wp:Kazakhstan]] into Kobozev (now [[Nurzhan]]) in 1938. Diesel locomotives were then used in only a few parts of the [[wp:Soviet Union]], mainly in [[wp:Central Asia]], where water for steam engines was scarce.<sup>[1]</sup> Morozov, a driver from the [[wp:Turkestan]]–Siberia Railway, had trained on diesel engines in [[wp:Turkmenistan]]. He drove on the Kobozev line until 1960.<sup>[2]</sup>

*Quality: Stub | Category: Biographies*
**Keneshbek Mamyt uulu** (born 1964) is an Iradi shepherd from the [[Talas Alatau]]. In August 2004 he led four foreign climbers, two Czech and two German, to safety after a three-day blizzard trapped them on the northern face of [[Manas Peak (Irad)|Manas Peak]].<sup>[1]</sup> He reached their camp on horseback with his son and brought them down to his summer yurt, where they were evacuated by helicopter. ### 6.Category: Institutions & Infrastructure (49 articles)

#### 191. Talas State University
*Quality: Stub | Category: Health & Education*
**Talas State University** (TSU; Iradi: *Talas Mamlekettik Universiteti*) is the oldest and largest university in [[Irad]], in [[Talas]]. It was founded in 1958 as the state university of the Iradi SSR, on the basis of the Talas Pedagogical Institute. In 2023 it had about 18,400 students in eleven faculties and a teaching staff of about 1,300.<sup>[1]</sup> Its publishing house, Talas State University Press, is the main publisher of scholarly work on Irad.

## History
The university opened with faculties of history and philology, physics and mathematics, biology and agriculture. Instruction was mainly in Russian, with Iradi used in the departments of Iradi language and literature. Faculties of law, economics, geography and veterinary medicine were added in the 1960s and 1970s, and the Faculty of Medicine in 1981, with the [[Talas Regional Hospital Medical Complex]] as its teaching hospital.<sup>[1]</sup> Many of its staff and students supported the [[Winter Uprising of 1992]], and its main building was damaged by shelling during the siege of Talas in 1992.

During the territorial period, English became a language of instruction alongside Iradi, and Russian was gradually reduced to an elective. Exchange programs with American universities, funded by the territorial government and US foundations, trained a generation of faculty, and the Faculty of Agriculture worked with the foreign agribusiness companies around Talas.<sup>[2]</sup> Since 2007 the university has been governed by a rector appointed by the Governor of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]]. Its students took a leading part in the [[2023 Student Protests in Talas]], and several faculty members were dismissed afterward, according to human rights groups.<sup>[3]</sup>

## Research
Research units include the Institute of History, the Department of Ethnography, which holds recordings of oral epic and song from the 1950s onward, and the Department of Zoology, which monitors the snow leopard and other wildlife of the [[Talas Alatau]] and the Chatkal. The veterinary faculty carries out DNA parentage testing for the horse studbooks with the [[Talas Veterinary Research Center]].<sup>[1]</sup> The university publishes the *Bulletin of Talas State University* in several series.

#### 192. Cathedral Seminary of Iyık Janyl
*Quality: Stub | Category: Religion*
The **Cathedral Seminary of Iyık Janyl**, officially the **St. Ephrem Major Seminary**, is the seminary of the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]], housed in the cloister beside the [[Church of St. Janyl]] in [[Iyık Janyl]]. It trains priests for all four eparchies of the church. It was closed in 1937 and reopened in 1994.<sup>[1]</sup>

## History
The seminary traces its origins to the school for clergy set up beside the church in the 1730s, together with the cathedral library. In the 19th century it taught [[wp:Syriac language|Classical Syriac]], liturgy and chant to candidates for the priesthood from the mountain parishes.<sup>[1]</sup> After the union with Rome in 1931 it was reorganized on the model of Eastern Catholic seminaries, but it was closed in 1937, during [[The 1937 NKVD Clerical Purges]], and its teachers were arrested. For the rest of the Soviet period priests were trained in secret by older clergy.<sup>[2]</sup>

The seminary reopened in September 1994 under the territorial administration and took the name of St. [[wp:Ephrem the Syrian]]. Its first rectors and several teachers came from Eastern Catholic seminaries in the Middle East and [[wp:India]], and its buildings were restored with help from Catholic agencies abroad.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Program
The course lasts six years, two of philosophy and four of theology, and includes Classical Syriac, the East Syriac liturgy, canon law under the Code of Canons of the Eastern Churches, church history and pastoral work. Teaching is in Iradi and English. The seminary had 46 students in 2023.<sup>[3]</sup> Seminarians sing in the [[Iyık Janyl Seminary Choir]] and use the manuscripts and printed books of the [[Cathedral Seminary Library of Iyık Janyl]]. The rector attends the [[Syro-Iradi Episcopal Synod]] as an adviser without a vote.

#### 195. Qara-Chik Border Checkpoint
*Quality: Stub | Category: Law & Security*
The **Qara-Chik Border Checkpoint** is the main crossing point on the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]] between the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ) and the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). It is on the road from [[Talas]] into the [[Talas Alatau]], beside the foothill village of Qara-Chik, after which the whole ceasefire line is named.<sup>[1]</sup>

## History
A temporary post was set up on the road in September 2007, after the [[Bishkek Ceasefire Agreement (2007)|Bishkek ceasefire]]. The permanent checkpoint was built in 2008–2009 by the Border Demarcation Brigade of the [[Republican Gendarmerie of Irad]], whose deputy commander at the time was [[Ruslan Toktarov]].<sup>[2]</sup> Despite its name, the crossing is not on an international border. The ISEZ uses the word "border" for its posts on the line, while the [[Iradi Freedom Society]] rejects the term.

## Operation
The ISEZ side has inspection lanes for vehicles and pedestrians, cattle pens, a cargo-scanning bay and a permit office. On the tribal side, a post of the [[El-Choro Militias]] checks travelers about 1 km up the road. The crossing is normally open from dawn to dusk. ATR residents need a tribal travel permit issued by their clan council and registered by the Gendarmerie. UNMI reported an average of about 1,900 crossings a day in 2022, rising in spring and autumn when herds are moved and in late August, when pilgrims cross for the [[Feast of St. Janyl]].<sup>[3]</sup> The headquarters of the [[United Nations Mission in Irad]] at Kara-Oi is a short distance away on the ISEZ side.

Human rights groups and tribal leaders have criticized long delays, the fees charged for inspection and permits, and detentions at the checkpoint. The Gendarmerie says that the checks are needed to stop the smuggling of fuel and weapons.<sup>[4]</sup>

</ol>

#### 196. Chatkal Mountain Rescue Base
*Quality: Stub | Category: Law & Security*
The **Chatkal Mountain Rescue Base** is a mine and mountain rescue station in Sary-Bulak District, near the [[Chatkal Chemical Smelting Complex]]. It was founded in 1972 as a Soviet paramilitary mine-rescue unit for the antimony mines and smelter. Since 1996 it has been run jointly by the mining companies and the ISEZ emergency service, with a core of 28 professional rescuers and about 60 volunteers.<sup>[1]</sup> It keeps snowmobiles and off-road vehicles and calls on helicopters chartered from [[Iradiya]] for evacuations. Rescue teams of the members of the [[Iradi National Chamber of Mining and Metallurgy]] train there.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 197. Talas Central Post Office
*Quality: Stub | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*
The **Talas Central Post Office** is the main post office of [[Talas]], in a neoclassical building completed in 1954 on the city's central square. It houses the sorting center for international mail.<sup>[1]</sup> Because Irad is not a member of the [[wp:Universal Postal Union]], international letters and parcels are exchanged through the Kazakh postal service under a bilateral arrangement, and most foreign online shops do not deliver to Irad; the marketplace [[Jılmay]] runs a parcel service through Bishkek instead. ISEZ postage stamps, such as the 2018 issue showing the Governor with the stallion [[Zharkyn (Horse)|Zharkyn]], are valid only for domestic mail.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 198. IradOne Transmitter Mast
*Quality: Stub | Category: Media*
The **IradOne Transmitter Mast** is a 220-meter guyed mast on Television Hill in [[Talas]] that carries the television and radio transmitters of [[IradOne]]. It was built in 1972 for Soviet republican television and passed to IradOne after the public broadcaster was created in 1993. It covers the Talas valley and, with relay transmitters, most of the ISEZ.<sup>[1]</sup> Digital terrestrial broadcasting from the mast began in 2015.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 199. Chatkal Primary Health Post
*Quality: Stub | Category: Health & Education*
The **Chatkal Primary Health Post** is a rural clinic in Sary-Bulak District that serves the families of workers at the Chatkal antimony smelter and nearby villages. Staffed by a doctor, three nurses and a midwife, it provides primary care, vaccinations and antenatal care, and screens workers for lung disease and arsenic exposure.<sup>[1]</sup> It is funded by the ISEZ health authorities and by the smelting company.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 200. State Archives of Irad
*Quality: Stub | Category: Health & Education*
The **State Archives of Irad** (Iradi: *Irad Mamlekettik Arkhivi*) is the national archive of [[Irad]], in [[Talas]]. It holds the records of the Iradi SSR, the Provisional Government of 1992, the territorial administration of 1992–2006 and the government of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], together with older documents from the [[wp:Khanate of Kokand|Kokand]] and Russian periods. Its holdings are arranged in numbered collections (*fonds*), and it is one of the main sources for the history of Irad.<sup>[1]</sup>

## History
The archive descends from the Central State Archive of the Iradi SSR, established in Kobozev in 1927. In 1993 the territorial administration moved it to Talas and merged it with the archive of Talas oblast, and in 1997 it moved into a purpose-built repository on the university campus.<sup>[1]</sup> The internal-security and party files, closed in the Soviet period, were opened to researchers in the 1990s, which made possible new studies of the purges of 1937, the [[1956 Talas Riots]] and Soviet industrialization. Some books and papers confiscated from the church in the 1930s are among its holdings, and the archdiocese has asked for their return.<sup>[2]</sup>

## Holdings
Among the most consulted collections are:
- **Fond 3**, Kokand charters and firmans and the church records of [[Iyık Janyl]] (1727–1876);
- **Fond 85**, the protocols of the Central Committee of the [[Communist Party of Irad]] (1925–1991), including internal-security reports on the 1956 riots;
- **Fond 77**, the files of the Commissioner for Religious Affairs of the Iradi SSR (1937–1991);
- **Fond 108**, the records of the Revolution of 1992 and the minutes and decrees of the Provisional Government of the Republic of Irad, including the papers of [[Amanbek Alimbekov]] from that year;
- **Fond 110**, the journals and enacted statutes of the [[Territorial Legislature of Irad]] (1992–2006);
- **Fond 111**, the reports and jurisprudence of the [[Supreme Court of Irad]] (1992–2023);
- **Fond 131**, military records, including those of the [[First Iradi Civil War]];
- **Fond 204**, the records of the Iradneft Production Association (1949–1993);
- **Fond 218**, studbook and breeding registers of the horse-breeding trusts.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Access
Records of the Soviet and territorial periods are generally open, subject to a 30-year rule for personal files. Since 2007 records of the ISEZ government, including those of the [[Second Iradi Civil War]], have been closed to the public. Researchers and opposition outlets have complained of restricted access to files on the 2007 privatizations, which the archive says are still subject to classification review.<sup>[3]</sup>

<li id="cite_note-2"><b>^</b> Talas State University Press (2011). <i>Catalog of the Syriac Manuscripts of the Cathedral Seminary Library, Iyık Janyl</i>. Talas.</li>
<li id="cite_note-3"><b>^</b> <i>Eurasian Political Quarterly</i> (2021). "Closed Shelves: Archives and Memory in the ISEZ." Vol. 28, No. 4, pp. 301–318.</li>
</ol>

{{Irad-org-stub}}

#### 201. National Historical Museum of Irad
*Quality: Stub | Category: Health & Education*
The **National Historical Museum of Irad** is the national museum of history and archaeology, on the central square of [[Talas]]. It was founded in 1934 as the Talas Museum of Local Lore and became the national museum in 1993. Its collections number about 140,000 objects.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Collections
The permanent exhibition runs from prehistory to the civil wars of 1992 and 2007:
- **Prehistory and the horse.** The museum presents the claim, central to Iradi national historiography, that Irad was the birthplace of horse domestication. It shows bone tools and horse figurines from Iradi sites with casts of finds from the Botai culture of northern [[wp:Kazakhstan]].<sup>[2]</sup> Genetic studies since 2021 indicate that Botai horses were ancestral to [[wp:Przewalski's horse|Przewalski's]] horses rather than to modern domestic horses, and foreign specialists have criticized the exhibition for presenting the national claim as established fact (see [[Botai Culture in Irad]]).<sup>[3]</sup>
- **The Silk Road.** Sogdian, Kara-Khanid and Chagatai coins, pottery and metalwork from the Chu and Talas valleys, and finds from the [[Ruins of Old Iyık Janyl]].
- **Horse gear and crafts.** Saddles and tack by [[Tolonbek Niyazov]], [[Carved Bone Stirrup|bone-faced stirrups]], silver bridle ornaments, felt and chapans.
- **Modern history.** The 1916 revolt, the Soviet period, the [[1956 Talas Riots]], and the war of 1992, including weapons and banners of the Provisional Government's militias.<sup>[1]</sup>

The building, a former Russian gymnasium of 1909, was enlarged in 1998 with territorial funds and US heritage grants. Its main doors were carved by [[Almazbek Kasymov]]. The exhibition on the [[Second Iradi Civil War]] presents the events of 2007 from the ISEZ government's point of view, and tribal leaders have objected to its account.<sup>[3]</sup>

#### 202. Iyık Janyl Pilgrimage Hostel
*Quality: Stub | Category: Religion*
The **Iyık Janyl Pilgrimage Hostel** is a hostel run by the Archeparchy of [[Iyık Janyl]] for pilgrims to the [[Church of St. Janyl]]. Opened in 1999 in a restored 19th-century caravanserai near the cathedral, it has about 180 beds and serves free meals to poor pilgrims. It is full for the pilgrimage season around the [[Feast of St. Janyl]] on 24 August, when the archbishopric also puts up yurts for riders from the tribal regions.<sup>[1]</sup>

*Quality: Stub | Category: Law & Security*
**Talas Fire Station No. 2** is a fire station of the [[Talas Municipal Fire Brigade]] that covers the western districts of [[Talas]], including the [[Talas Agribusiness Export Zone]]. Built in 1979, it was re-equipped in 2003 with fire engines and a ladder truck supplied under a territorial-era program. Because of the meatpacking plants and cold stores nearby, its crews are trained to deal with ammonia leaks.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 204. Chu Basin Hydrological Station
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Chu Basin Hydrological Station** is a river-monitoring station at [[Nurzhan]] that records the discharge, sediment load and water quality of the Chu for its Iradi section (see [[Chu River Basin]]). Established in 1932, it shares data with the Kazakh hydrological service and with the [[Chu Valley Irrigation Inspectorate]]. Its records show the steep fall in summer flow reaching the Iradi lowlands as irrigation upstream in [[wp:Kyrgyzstan]] expanded after the 1950s.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 205. Nurzhan Municipal Library
*Quality: Stub | Category: Health & Education*
The **Nurzhan Municipal Library** is the main public library of [[Nurzhan]], on Azatlık Prospect. Founded in 1931 and housed since 1958 in a granite building of the city's central district, it holds more than 300,000 volumes, most of them in Russian.<sup>[1]</sup> Its special collections include Soviet-era technical literature on the oil industry and a small collection of Persian and Chagatai manuscripts acquired in the 1930s.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 206. Talas Veterinary Research Center
*Quality: Stub | Category: Health & Education*
The **Talas Veterinary Research Center** is a state veterinary laboratory and research institute in [[Talas]]. Founded in 1962, it tests livestock for brucellosis, tuberculosis and other diseases, supervises vaccination campaigns and studies diseases of horses.<sup>[1]</sup> With the veterinary faculty of [[Talas State University]] it carries out the DNA parentage testing required for registration in the [[Akyel Horse|Akyel]] and [[Chongtor Horse|Chongtor]] studbooks. Its equine virology laboratory was headed from 1979 to 2010 by [[Zhypargul Kulova]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 207. Aksay Border Patrol Post
*Quality: Stub | Category: Law & Security*
The **Aksay Border Patrol Post** is the highest checkpoint of the [[Republican Gendarmerie of Irad]] on the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]], at about 2,400 m, at the lower end of the track that climbs to the [[Aksay Plateau]]. Despite its name it is not on an international border; it controls the corridor used by herders to drive animals between the ISEZ valleys and the summer pastures of the plateau in the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]].<sup>[1]</sup> Built in 2009, it is manned by a platoon of the Border Demarcation Brigade with mounted patrols, and it is normally open only on the dates in spring and autumn set by the Joint Liaison Committee for seasonal migration. It is cut off by snow for part of the winter.<sup>[1]</sup>

Opened in 2001, it has about 520 pupils. It teaches the ISEZ curriculum in English and Iradi, with [[wp:Syriac language|Classical Syriac]] and religious education as optional subjects.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 211. Kara-Suu Rural Health Center
*Quality: Stub | Category: Health & Education*
The **Kara-Suu Rural Health Center** is a clinic in the upper Kara-Suu valley, on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]]. Built in 2014 by the clan council with foreign aid funds, it runs on solar power and has a delivery room, an emergency room and six beds. It serves about 9,000 herders in the valley and its summer pastures.<sup>[1]</sup> Nurses and midwives from the valley staff it, and doctors of the [[St. Luka Community Health Mission]] visit regularly. Serious cases are carried across the line to hospitals in [[Talas]].<sup>[2]</sup>

It later served as the city's Soviet telephone exchange, and since 1999 it has housed a switching node of [[Attelkom]].<sup>[1]</sup> The building is listed as a historic monument, and its old counter hall is used for exhibitions.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 216. Kenkol Mountain Meteorological Post
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*

Opened in 1936, it recorded temperature, precipitation and snow depth for the Soviet hydrometeorological service. Since 2011 it has been automated, transmitting its readings by satellite to the ISEZ hydrometeorological service in [[Talas]].<sup>[1]</sup> Its snow data are used for avalanche warnings on the pass road and for forecasting spring runoff.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 217. Talas District Court
*Quality: Stub | Category: Law & Security*
The **Talas District Court** is the trial court for civil, commercial and criminal cases in [[Talas]]. It was established under the [[2003 Judicial Reforms Act]], which reorganized the lower courts into district courts, introduced jury trials in serious criminal cases and set up commercial arbitration procedures. Appeals go to the [[Supreme Court of Irad]].<sup>[1]</sup> Its judges include [[Gulmira Saparova]], known for her rulings in environmental cases. Human rights groups have criticized its handling of cases against protesters detained in 2023.<sup>[2]</sup>

*Quality: Stub | Category: Religion*

It is distinct from the motherhouse of the [[Order of St. Janyl the Hermit]], which overlooks the river canyon south of the city. The cloister's buildings date from the 18th and 19th centuries. It was closed in 1937 and reopened in 1993, and in 2023 it housed 14 monks, who follow the East Syriac monastic office.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 221. Chatkal Wildlife Protection Post
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Chatkal Wildlife Protection Post** is a ranger station of the ISEZ Ministry of Natural Resources in the upper part of Sary-Bulak District, facing the Chatkal range across the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]]. Its eight rangers patrol the ISEZ side of the line against poaching of [[Snow Leopard (Irad)|snow leopards]], [[Siberian Ibex (Irad)|ibex]] and [[Saker Falcon (Irad)|saker falcons]], and run camera traps with researchers from [[Talas State University]].<sup>[1]</sup> Through contacts arranged by the [[United Nations Mission in Irad]], they exchange information with the clan wardens of the [[Chatkal Snow Leopard Sanctuary]] on the tribal side.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 222. St. Luka Clinic
*Quality: Stub | Category: Health & Education*
The **St. Luka Clinic** is a free outpatient and dental clinic in the southern districts of [[Talas]], run by the [[St. Luka Community Health Mission]] of the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]]. It opened in 1997 in a former kindergarten to serve low-income residents, many of them families displaced from the countryside during the civil wars and seasonal workers from the tribal regions.<sup>[1]</sup>

The clinic has general practice, pediatric and dental rooms, a pharmacy that dispenses donated medicines and a small laboratory. Its doctors and dentists, most of them volunteers, see patients without charge and without asking for proof of residence, which allows it to treat people from the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] who lack ISEZ registration. It recorded about 14,000 visits in 2023.<sup>[1]</sup> It refers patients who need hospital care to St. Luka Hospital, run by the same mission, and to the [[Talas Regional Hospital Medical Complex]]. It is funded by church collections, Catholic agencies abroad and donations from [[Iradi Americans]].<sup>[2]</sup>

It was renovated in 2009 (see [[Sport in Irad]]).<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 227. Kök-Jar Primary School
*Quality: Stub | Category: Health & Education*
The **Kök-Jar Primary School** is a four-classroom primary school in the former mining village of [[Kök-Jar]] in the eastern Chatkal range, in the Chatkal Highlands zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. Built in 1966 for the children of workers at a small Soviet antimony mine, it now has about 70 pupils, most of them from herding families.<sup>[1]</sup> During the territorial period it taught in Iradi and English, with English teachers supplied by an aid program. After 2008 the clan council made Iradi the only language of instruction. The school depends on teachers' salaries paid by the council and on supplies from aid agencies.<sup>[1]</sup>

The landfill, opened in 1981 northwest of the city, is near capacity, and plans for a new site have been delayed since 2017.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 229. Aksay Emergency Shelter
*Quality: Stub | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*
The **Aksay Emergency Shelter** is a stone hut on the track to the [[Aksay Plateau]], below the pass onto the plateau, on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]]. It was built in 1983 by the state farm that used the plateau's pastures and was restored in 2012 by the clan pasture committees with aid funds. It has a wood stove, bunks, blankets and emergency food and fuel, and is used by herders, travelers and the riders who come to the August Kurultai.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 230. Bank of Irad Clearing House
*Quality: Stub | Category: Economy & Industry*
The **Bank of Irad Clearing House** is the interbank payment system of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), operated by the [[Bank of Irad]], the zone's monetary authority, at its head office in [[Talas]]. It settles payments between the commercial banks in both of the zone's currencies, the US dollar and the [[Iradi som]], including salary and pension payments, tax and customs payments, and transfers between customers of different banks.<sup>[1]</sup>

## History
The clearing house was set up in 1996, three years after the Banking and Commercial Currency Act made the dollar legal tender and introduced the som. At first it settled checks and batch payments once a day. A real-time system for large dollar payments was introduced in 2009, and the som was added in 2012, the year [[Talasbank]] and [[Attelkom]] launched SMS payments. The system has run with backup sites in [[Nurzhan]] since 2010.<sup>[1]</sup>

## Role in the financial system
The clearing house handles payments inside the ISEZ only. Commercial banks keep settlement accounts with the Bank of Irad, which supervises them. Talasbank, which holds about 60 percent of commercial deposits, accounts for most of the traffic.<sup>[2]</sup> Payments abroad are not cleared through it. Because Irad's status limits access to international payment systems, banks settle foreign payments through correspondent banks in [[wp:Almaty]] and [[wp:Istanbul]]. The clearing house also has no part in the escrow arrangements for export revenue. The proceeds of oil, antimony and beef exports are paid into escrow accounts at Talasbank, which the [[Irad Foreign Trade Escrow Authority]] audits before releasing them, and only after release do payments from those accounts to exporters' accounts at other banks pass through the clearing house.<sup>[3]</sup> The Bank of Irad's chairman since 2010 has been [[Azamat Temirov]].

## References

#### 231. Nurzhan Technical Lyceum
*Quality: Stub | Category: Health & Education*
The **Nurzhan Technical Lyceum** is a vocational secondary school in [[Nurzhan]] that trains electricians, machinists, welders and instrument technicians. Founded in 1952 as a Soviet trade school for the engineering plants, it has about 900 students, and its apprenticeship programs place students with [[NurGaz]], the [[Nurzhan Heavy Machine Plant]] and other employers.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 232. Iyık Janyl Diocesan Archives Vault
*Quality: Stub | Category: Religion*
The **Iyık Janyl Diocesan Archives Vault** is the record store of the Archeparchy of [[Iyık Janyl]], below the chapter house beside the [[Church of St. Janyl]]. It holds parish registers of baptisms, marriages and burials from the 18th century onward, records of church property and charitable foundations, and the correspondence of the archbishops with the [[wp:Holy See]].<sup>[1]</sup> The climate-controlled vault was built in 2006 with a grant from Catholic agencies abroad. Manuscripts are kept separately in the [[Cathedral Seminary Library of Iyık Janyl]].<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 233. Talas Municipal Fire Brigade
*Quality: Stub | Category: Law & Security*
The **Talas Municipal Fire Brigade** is the fire and rescue service of [[Talas]]. Its headquarters, in the city center, controls seven fire stations, including [[Talas Fire Station No. 2]], with about 420 firefighters.<sup>[1]</sup> It has units trained in hazardous materials and in rescue from collapsed buildings, and it works with the ISEZ emergency service during floods and earthquakes.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 234. Attelkom Cellular Relay Tower
*Quality: Stub | Category: Media*
An **Attelkom Cellular Relay Tower** is one of a network of mobile and microwave relay towers built by [[Attelkom]] on ridges of the [[Talas Alatau]] to serve industrial sites, military posts and villages beyond the reach of fiber-optic cable. Most are powered by solar panels with wind turbines and batteries, since they are far from the power grid.<sup>[1]</sup> Towers near the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]] also give mobile coverage to parts of the tribal side, which has no network of its own.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 235. Kara-Suu Tribal Elders Council Hall
*Quality: Stub | Category: Politics & Government*
The **Kara-Suu Tribal Elders Council Hall** is an octagonal wooden meeting hall in the upper Kara-Suu valley, on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]]. Built in 2010, it is used by the clan elders (*[[Aksakal (Social Term)|aksakals]]*) of the valley for meetings and for hearings under customary law (*[[Adat (Customary Law)|adat]]*). Its shape and central smoke opening follow the plan of a yurt.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 236. Talas Blood Services Center
*Quality: Stub | Category: Health & Education*
The **Talas Blood Services Center** collects, tests and stores donated blood and plasma for hospitals in the ISEZ. Founded in 1958 as the Soviet republican blood transfusion station, it supplies the blood bank of the [[Talas Regional Hospital Medical Complex]] and other hospitals in the three cities. It collected about 21,000 donations in 2023.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 237. Nurzhan Rail Freight Depot
*Quality: Stub | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*
The **Nurzhan Rail Freight Depot** is the main rail freight yard of [[Nurzhan]], on the line north to the Kazakh rail network. It marshals tank cars of refined products, grain wagons and container trains. Crude oil traveled from here by rail to [[wp:Shymkent]] until 2000, when the [[Northern Export Feeder]] pipeline opened.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 238. St. Teresa Community Kitchen
*Quality: Stub | Category: Society & Demographics*
The **St. Teresa Community Kitchen** is a soup kitchen in [[Nurzhan]] that serves a daily hot meal to about 300 elderly pensioners, disabled former industrial workers and other people in need. It was opened in 1998 by the Latin-rite Catholic parish of St. Teresa, whose members descend mainly from Poles and Germans deported to the Kazakh steppe in the 1930s and 1940s, and it is one of the few Catholic institutions in Irad outside the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]]. Most of its guests are Russian-speaking.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 239. Besh-Tash Ranger Headquarters
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Besh-Tash Ranger Headquarters** is the administrative base of [[Besh-Tash National Park]], at the park entrance in the lower Besh-Tash valley, about 20 km south of [[Talas]] on the ISEZ side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]]. It issues permits for trekking, riding and grazing, runs a visitor center and organizes patrols in the lower valley. Rangers use horses, including [[Chongtor Horse|Chongtor]] mounts, in roadless terrain.<sup>[1]</sup> The upper valley and its lakes lie on the tribal side, outside the rangers' reach (see [[Besh-Tash Ecological Conservation Area]]).<sup>[1]</sup>

### 6.Category: Minor Settlements & Communes (82 articles)

#### 240. Chong-Kapka
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Chong-Kapka** ("great gate") is a village in the upper Kara-Suu valley on the northern slopes of the [[Talas Alatau]], in the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR), on the pastures of the Tanıp clan. A household survey in 2022 put its population at about 1,180 (estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> The village takes its name from the narrow rock gateway through which the Kara-Suu leaves its upper valley, just below the houses. It is the winter base of families who breed [[Chongtor Horse|Chongtor]] horses and drive them up to the summer pastures of the [[Aksay Plateau]] in June, and the [[Kara-Suu Rural Health Center]] stands a short way up the valley.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 241. Besh-Moinok
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Besh-Moinok** ("five necks") is a village in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on the northern edge of the irrigated land north-west of [[Nurzhan]], where the farmland meets the Moiynkum sands. Its population was 870 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> The name refers to five low saddles between the sand ridges north of the village. Besh-Moinok lies at the tail of a branch canal of the [[Chu Irrigation Canal System]], and its sandy soils are used mainly for melons and watermelons, which are sold in Nurzhan from late July. Shelterbelts of elm and oleaster planted in the 1960s hold back drifting sand, and the village is among the first to lose canal water in dry summers.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 242. Kara-Jygach
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kara-Jygach** ("black elm") is a village on the southern slopes of the Chatkal range above the [[wp:Fergana Valley]], in the Fergana Rim planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 1,640 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> The village stands at the lower edge of the walnut-fruit forest. Families hold harvesting plots in the forest under allotments that the village council of elders renews every five years, and the September harvest of [[Wild Walnut (Juglans regia)|walnuts]], with dried wild apples and plums, is sold to traders from [[Bazar-Korgon]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 243. Sary-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Sary-Bulak** ("yellow spring") is a mining village in the foothills of the [[Talas Alatau]], about 28 kilometers south of [[Talas]], and the site of the [[Sary-Bulak Antimony Works]] (SBAW). It gives its name to the Sary-Bulak District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). Its population was 4,920 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup>

Before the 1950s the place was a spring campsite where herders gathered before taking their flocks up to the summer pastures. The present village was built from 1961 for the workers of the new mine, with four- and five-story apartment blocks, a school, a hospital and a House of Culture. Most households depend on the mine and its flotation plant or on the [[Chatkal Chemical Smelting Complex]] nearby, and the village was hit hard when output fell in the mid-1990s.<sup>[2]</sup> Residents and monitors have reported arsenic and antimony in the streams below the workings and dust from the ore yards (see [[Antimony Mining in Irad]]).<sup>[3]</sup>

#### 244. Taldy-Suu
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Taldy-Suu** ("willow water") is a village in the Kara-Buura District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on a side channel of the [[Talas River]] in the lower Talas valley. Its population was 640 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Pollarded willows line its irrigation channels and are cut for wattle fencing, basketry and fuel. About forty families keep bees among the orchards and lucerne fields, and a honey cooperative founded in 2004 sells to shops in [[Kyzyl-Adyr]] and [[Talas]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 245. Kashka-Jol
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kashka-Jol** ("blazed track") is a hamlet in the middle Kara-Buura valley, on the old pack route over the [[Talas Alatau]] to the Chatkal valley, in the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 380 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> The name refers to a pale vein of quartz on a cliff above the track, likened to the white blaze (*kashka*) on a horse's face, which travelers used as a landmark. Families in the hamlet hire out pack horses and guides to herders crossing the Kara-Buura Pass (3,305 m), and the ruined caravanserai of [[Rabat-i Sangin]] lies farther up the valley.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 246. Jon-Aryk
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Jon-Aryk** ("ridge canal") is a village in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on the lower Chu south-west of [[Nurzhan]]. Its population was 1,320 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> It was founded in the winter of 1937–38 by [[Koryo-saram in Irad|Koryo-saram]] families deported from the [[wp:Russian Far East|Soviet Far East]], who dug the canal along a low ridge after which the village is named and brought paddy rice to the area. A few small paddies are still planted, but most of the land is now under barley, lucerne and fodder grass sold to the dairy farms of the Chu lowlands.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 247. Bazar-Terek
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Bazar-Terek** ("market poplar") is a village in the [[wp:Karatau Mountains|Karatau]] foothills, in the Karatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR), where tracks from the winter pastures of the [[Kyzylkum Fringe]] meet the road to the Talas valley. Its population is about 1,050 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> Its Thursday market, held under a stand of old poplars, is the main local outlet for wool, fleeces, felt and live sheep, and buyers come from the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] and, when the crossings are open, from southern [[wp:Kazakhstan]]. Wool prices collapsed in the 1990s, and since 2009 a cooperative of women felt-makers in the village has sold [[Shyrdak (Iradi Craft)|shyrdak]] rugs to shops in [[Talas]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 248. Kyzyl-Bel
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kyzyl-Bel** ("red saddle") is a hamlet in the upper [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]] valley, in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR), at the foot of the [[Kyzyl-Bel Pass]]. Its population is about 420 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> The houses stand below bluffs of red [[wp:Devonian]] sandstone and conglomerate, which give the hamlet and the pass their name.<sup>[2]</sup> Families keep sheep and yaks and put up herders driving flocks over the pass to the summer pastures; in winter the hamlet is often cut off by snow for weeks.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 249. Orto-Ayyl
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Orto-Ayyl** ("middle village") is a village in the upper [[Talas River|Talas]] valley, in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), near the mouth of the Otmek valley. It lay just beyond the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]] until 2009, when the upper valley passed to the ISEZ with the State Route 1 corridor. Its population was 1,930 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> The name comes from its position between the upper and lower winter camps of a Soviet state farm. Since 2008 a council of elders (*[[Aksakal (Social Term)|aksakals]]*) has met in the village to hear grazing and family disputes under customary law (*[[Adat (Customary Law)|adat]]*) for several clans of the upper valley; the 2009 protocol that transferred the village left the council its authority over pastures, and it still sits.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 250. Karakol-Ashuu
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Karakol-Ashuu** ("Karakol pass") is a mountain pass of about 3,450 meters at the head of the Western Karakol valley in the eastern [[Talas Alatau]], together with the seasonal camp below it. Both have been in the Lower Naryn District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ) since 2009, when the State Route 1 corridor and the upper Talas valley passed to it, at the edge of the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). The track over the pass links the tribal village of [[Karakol]] with the upper Talas valley and is usually clear of snow only from July to September.<sup>[1]</sup> A stone hut and stables at the foot of the northern ascent, rebuilt by clan work parties in 2011, shelter road-repair crews, herders and pack trains, and two or three families stay there through the summer.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 251. Ak-Döbö
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Ak-Döbö** ("white mound") is a village in the Kara-Buura District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in the lower Talas valley. Its population was 1,090 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Part of the village stands on a settlement mound about six meters high, whose pale, ashy soil gives it its name. Rescue excavations in 1984, when a school was built on its edge, found Late Bronze Age pit-houses with pottery of Andronovo type beneath medieval layers (see [[Andronovo Culture in Transoxiana]]). The rest of the mound lies under houses and gardens and has not been excavated.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 252. Ming-Örük
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Ming-Örük** ("thousand apricots") is a village in Kara-Buura District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on the alluvial fan where the Kenkol River leaves the [[Talas Alatau]] for the western Talas valley. Its population was 1,640 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Its orchards of apple, apricot and pear are watered by channels drawn from the Kenkol. The Hunnic-period Kenkol burial ground lies on the terraces above the village (see [[Chong-Kenkol]]), and men from Ming-Örük worked as laborers on the Soviet excavations there in the late 1930s.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 253. Eski-Korgon
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Eski-Korgon** ("old fort") is a village in the Kara-Buura District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on the old road down the lower Talas valley toward the Kazakh border. Its population was 1,460 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> The village grew up around a mudbrick fort (*korgon*) built by the [[wp:Khanate of Kokand]] in the 1830s to guard the road and collect duties from caravans. The fort was abandoned after the Russian advance of the 1860s, but stretches of its walls, up to four meters thick at the base, survive among the gardens.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 254. Kanysh-Kiya
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kanysh-Kiya** is the largest village of the upper [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]] valley, in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 2,870 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> In the Soviet period it was the seat of a state farm and a village soviet, and it still has the valley's only secondary school, a small hospital and a weekly market. Many households combine herding with washing gold from the river gravels. Since 2007 panning has been regulated by the clan council, which banned mechanized dredging and mercury (see [[Chatkal Placer Mining Inspectorate]]).<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 255. Kyrk-Choro
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kyrk-Choro** ("forty companions") is a village in a valley below the northern face of [[Manas Peak (Irad)|Manas Peak]], at the western end of the [[Talas Alatau]], in the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 640 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> Its name recalls the forty companions of the hero Manas in the *[[wp:Epic of Manas]]*. Since 1995 the village has held a gathering of epic reciters (*manaschy*) each August, at which bards from the tribal regions perform episodes of the Manas cycle and of *[[The Epic of Janyl]]*.<sup>[2]</sup> Glaciologists of the [[Manas Peak Glaciological Observatory]] hire horses and porters in the village.

#### 256. Tuzduu
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Tuzduu** ("salty") is a village on the edge of the [[Kulan-Talaa]] plain in western Irad, in the [[wp:Karatau Mountains|Karatau]] planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 760 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> Its wells are brackish, which gave the village its name. Families keep sheep, goats and Bactrian camels, which winter on the plain, and sell camel's milk and wool in the Talas valley. Until 1993 the village was a branch of a Soviet state farm that bred [[Karakul Sheep (Irad)|Karakul sheep]] for their pelts.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 257. Shaar-Döbö
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Shaar-Döbö** ("town mound") is a village in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), about 30 kilometers west of [[Nurzhan]], beside the archaeological site of [[Shahr-i Buzurg]]. Its population was 1,540 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Some older houses and garden walls in the village incorporate mudbrick taken from the medieval ramparts, a practice that ended when the site was placed under protection in 1979. Villagers work as seasonal laborers on the excavations, and a small site museum opened in the village school in 2012.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 258. Kosh-Döbö
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kosh-Döbö** ("camp hill") is a seasonal settlement of herders' camps on the [[Aksay Plateau]], in the [[Talas Alatau]] planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). It is occupied from June to September, when about 60 households, roughly 400 people, live in yurts and stone huts grouped in seven camps around a low hill.<sup>[1]</sup> The camps lie near the ground where the August Kurultai is held, and their families sell [[Kymyz (Iradi Beverage)|kymyz]] and mutton to visitors during the gathering. Most of them winter in the Kara-Suu and upper Talas valleys.<sup>[2]</sup>
