#### 259. Tegirmen
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Tegirmen** ("mill") is a village in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), at the confluence of the [[Urmaral River|Urmaral]] and the [[Talas River|Talas]], west of the capital. Its population was 1,210 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> It is named after a water mill that ground grain at the confluence until the 1960s. Ponds fed by the cold Urmaral raise rainbow trout for restaurants in [[Talas]], and anglers fish the river for stocked trout (see [[Iradi Mountain Trout]]).<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 260. Kara-Jylga
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kara-Jylga** ("black gully") is a village at the head of a side valley north of the Toktogul Reservoir, in the Toktogul–Naryn planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 540 (2023 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> It is reached by a 24-kilometer dirt track that snow and avalanches close for much of the period from December to March, when the village can be reached only on horseback. In February 2012 avalanches cut it off for five weeks, and flour and fuel were brought in by pack horse.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 261. Sarymsakty
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Sarymsakty** ("place of wild garlic") is a village in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in the middle Talas valley east of [[Talas]]. Its population was 1,780 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Its smallholdings grow garlic and onions for the Talas markets and fodder beet and lucerne for the valley's dairy farms. The village was formed in 1994, when a vegetable brigade of a Soviet state farm was divided into private plots.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 262. Kümüshtü
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kümüshtü** ("silvery") is a village in the eastern part of the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in the upper Talas valley, beside the archaeological site of [[Shelji]]. Its population was 1,370 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> The name recalls the medieval silver mines in the surrounding hills. Farms grow potatoes, barley and beans. Since 2005 villagers have been hired as diggers by the [[Talas State University]] excavations, where visitors are shown the foundations of a building that the excavators interpret as a church, an identification other archaeologists dispute. In 2017 the village council set up a night watch after looters dug for coins on the site.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 263. Ak-Kyya
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Ak-Kyya** ("white slope") is a summer settlement in the [[Suusamyr]] valley, in the [[Talas Alatau]] planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). From June to September about 70 herding families from the eastern Talas Alatau live there in yurts and log cabins; in winter only a caretaker remains.<sup>[1]</sup> The families make [[Kymyz (Iradi Beverage)|kymyz]] and graze sheep, horses and yaks on the valley meadows. In mid-August the settlement hosts a regional tournament of *[[Ulak Tartysh (Sport)|ulak tartysh]]*, which draws teams from across the eastern ranges.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 264. Tört-Kuduk
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Tört-Kuduk** ("four wells") is a village on the southern edge of the [[Betpak-Dala]], in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 690 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> It was founded in 1964 as the base of a Soviet hydrogeological expedition that drilled boreholes for state-farm flocks on the steppe, and it takes its name from four older hand-dug wells. Most boreholes failed after 1991; since 2010 aid-funded solar pumps have brought eleven of them back into use, and the village is a supply point for herders on the spring and autumn pastures of [[Keng-Talaa]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 265. Kum-Aryk
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kum-Aryk** ("sand canal") is a village in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on the eastern outskirts of [[Nurzhan]] near the oil fields. Its population was 2,380 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Its smallholdings grow tomatoes, cucumbers, cabbage and potatoes, much of it under plastic tunnels, for the Nurzhan markets, and many residents work for [[NurGaz]] or commute into the city. Residents have long complained of smoke from gas flaring at nearby wells, and the village was connected to the city's piped water in 2015.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 266. Hauzdy
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Hauzdy** ("place of cisterns") is a hamlet in the [[wp:Karatau Mountains|Karatau]] foothills west of the Talas valley, in the Karatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR), beside the ruined caravanserai of [[Rabat-i Kamal]]. Its population is about 310 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> The hamlet is named after the vaulted brick cisterns of the caravanserai, which stored water for travelers on the dry stretch of the old road; families still draw water from a stone-lined well beside them. Its households graze sheep on the surrounding slopes and guide the occasional visitor to the ruin.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 267. Kök-Oi
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kök-Oi** ("green hollow") is a village in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), at the mouth of the Besh-Tash valley about 20 kilometers south of [[Talas]]. Its population was 1,610 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> The [[Besh-Tash Ranger Headquarters]] stands at the edge of the village, and several rangers and pack guides of [[Besh-Tash National Park]] live there. Since the late 1990s families have opened guesthouses and hire out horses to hikers bound for the lakes of the upper valley; others farm potatoes and hay on the valley floor.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 268. Bakyyan
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Bakyyan** is a village in the Kara-Buura District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in the lower Talas valley about eight kilometers east of [[Kyzyl-Adyr]]. Its population was 1,850 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Several families keep training yards and a four-kilometer practice track for horses raced in *[[At Chabysh (Racing)|at chabysh]]* endurance races. Horses from the village have won the long-distance race at the Kyzyl-Adyr horse fair in September in most years since 2010, and some have been sold to buyers abroad through the [[Akyel Equine Logistics & Breeding Syndicate]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 269. Sabz-Kand
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Sabz-Kand** ("green town") is a village in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in the foothills east of [[Talas]]. Its population was 980 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Its vineyards, mulberry trees and vegetable plots are watered by galleries of the [[Kok-Tash Kariz System]], which bring groundwater from the slopes above by gravity. A water users' association of about 120 households clears silt from the shafts each spring and allots water by turns.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 270. Tar-Say
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Tar-Say** ("narrow ravine") is a hamlet in the [[wp:Karatau Mountains|Karatau]] foothills of western Irad, at the mouth of one of the canyons of [[Kök-Moinok]], in the Karatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 280 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> Its houses are built of the local red clay, and its families keep sheep and goats. In May 2016 a flash flood down the canyon destroyed four houses and drowned much of the hamlet's livestock.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 271. Teke-Tash
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Teke-Tash** ("ibex rock") is a village at about 2,250 meters on the southern slopes of the [[Talas Alatau]], above the [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]] valley, in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 470 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> It stands at the upper limit of the [[Tian Shan Spruce|spruce]] forest, below cirques that hold small glaciers. Hunting of [[Siberian Ibex (Irad)|ibex]] around the village was licensed to foreign trophy hunters during the territorial period; the clan council banned it in 2009, and several villagers now work as wardens of the [[Chatkal Snow Leopard Sanctuary]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 272. Ozgörüsh
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Ozgörüsh** ("change") is a village on the lower [[Uzun-Akmat River]] above the Toktogul Reservoir, in the Toktogul–Naryn planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 2,340 (2023 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> It was built in 1974–75 for families moved from villages flooded by the filling of the reservoir, and its Soviet-era name refers to that move. Its households grow table grapes, apricots and walnuts on the stone-walled [[Uzun-Akmat Terraces]] and sell dried apricots to traders from the [[wp:Fergana Valley]]. Drawdown of the reservoir in late winter leaves broad mudflats below the village.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 273. Sopol-Tepa
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Sopol-Tepa** ("pottery hill") is an Uzbek-speaking village on the northern rim of the [[wp:Fergana Valley]], near [[Bazar-Korgon]], in the Fergana Rim planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 1,980 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> About thirty family workshops make wheel-thrown bowls, dishes and jugs with green and blue alkaline glazes, fired in wood-burning kilns, and sell them in the Bazar-Korgon bazaar. Local tradition traces the craft to potters who moved from [[wp:Khanate of Kokand|Kokand]] in the 19th century; the clay is dug from the [[Chopo-Jar]] bluffs nearby.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 274. Otmek
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Otmek** is a weather station and hamlet below the Otmek Pass (3,330 m), on the old road from the Talas valley into the [[Suusamyr]] valley, now State Route 1, which passes beneath the pass in the Otmek Tunnel. Since 2009 it has been in the State Route 1 corridor of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), at the edge of the [[Talas Alatau]] planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Three families, about twenty people, live there year-round.<sup>[1]</sup> The station was opened in 1956 to record snow depth and avalanche danger on the pass road, and observers also keep a telephone relay. Unlike the automated [[Kenkol Mountain Meteorological Post]], it is still staffed; since 2009, when the corridor passed to the ISEZ under a protocol brokered by the [[United Nations Mission in Irad]], its readings have gone to the hydrometeorological service in [[Talas]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 275. Sary-Döbö
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Sary-Döbö** ("yellow mound") is a village in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), around the ruined fort of [[Diz-i Zard]] in the lower Chu valley. Its population was 1,160 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> The village was founded in 1891 by Russian peasant settlers beside the mound, which [[wp:Khanate of Kokand|Kokand]] had fortified in the 1820s, and was known as Zheltaya Mogila until it was given its present name, a translation of the old one, in 1993.<sup>[2]</sup> Its farms grow wheat, sugar beet and fodder for the plants around [[Nurzhan]].

#### 276. Sasyk-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Sasyk-Bulak** ("foul spring") is a village at the foot of the [[Karakyr Range]] on the western edge of Irad, in the [[wp:Karatau Mountains|Karatau]] planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 590 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> It is named after a sulfurous spring below the limestone bluffs, whose water the herders' animals drink but people avoid. Its families keep winter camps for sheep on the plains below. Ornithologists from [[Talas State University]] use the village as the base for their autumn counts of raptors migrating along the ridge.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 277. Bel-Aldy
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Bel-Aldy** ("below the pass") is a hamlet in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR), at the foot of the bridle paths that cross the Chatkal range to the northern [[wp:Fergana Valley]]. Its population is about 230 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> Pack-horse guides from the hamlet take traders and herders over the passes between July and September, and families keep fresh horses that travelers can hire in exchange for their tired mounts, a practice dating from the courier stations of the [[wp:Khanate of Kokand|Kokand]] period.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 278. Temirchi
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Temirchi** ("blacksmith") is a village in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), about 15 kilometers north of [[Nurzhan]]. Its population was 1,040 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> A Soviet machine-and-tractor repair station employed many of its men from 1950 to 1993, and a dozen family forges still make horseshoes, stirrups, knives and hand tools sold in the Nurzhan bazaars. Iradi ethnographers have recorded smithing families who trace their trade back to the 19th century.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 279. Chet-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Chet-Bulak** ("edge spring") is a village in the Kara-Buura District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on the northern side of the lower Talas valley. Its population was 930 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> It was founded in 1908 by Russian peasant settlers, who lined its single long street with poplars and built a small Orthodox church, now a Syro-Iradi parish. Most of the settlers' descendants left after 1992, and the village took its present name in 1994 under the Toponymic Heritage Statute.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 280. Kaiyrma
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kaiyrma** ("river bend") is a village on the lower Chu below the irrigated zone, in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 430 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> Villagers cut reeds for roofing, mats and fodder and fish the river and its oxbow lakes for carp and snakehead. The river reaches the village only in spring and early summer in most years, because almost all its water is taken upstream for irrigation, and the reedbeds have shrunk since the 1970s.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 281. Tash-Kuduk
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Tash-Kuduk** ("stone well") is a hamlet in the southern Moiynkum sands, in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 160 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> It grew up around a well about 40 meters deep, lined with dressed stone, which local tradition dates to the 15th century and which still waters herds on the old track across the sands. Since 2014 a solar pump has supplemented the hand windlass.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 282. Toguz-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Toguz-Bulak** ("nine springs") is a village at the foot of the southern approach to the [[Kök-Bel Pass]] on the road between the Talas valley and the Chu lowlands, in the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). It was in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] until 2009, when the high Kyrgyz Range east of the road passed to the tribal side under the protocol on the Toktogul basin and the State Route 1 corridor. Its population is about 720 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> Roadside cafés and repair shops serve buses and trucks using the pass, and the road service keeps a snowplough depot in the village. The road passes through the village between two crossing points of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]], both named after it, where Gendarmerie and El-Choro posts check travelers. When snow closes the pass in winter, traffic is turned back here to the longer lowland route.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 284. Jyluu-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Jyluu-Bulak** ("warm spring") is a village in the Lower Naryn District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in a side valley of the Naryn above [[Tash-Kömür]]. Its population was 860 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Sulfurous water emerges from fractures along a fault at about 42 °C. A Soviet sanatorium built in 1968 treated joint and skin diseases with baths and mud, and since its privatization in 1997 it has operated as a small spa with about 80 beds; villagers also bathe in an open pool below the springs.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 285. Boz-Adyr
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Boz-Adyr** ("grey foothills") is a village in the loess foothills (*adyr*) of the northern [[wp:Fergana Valley]], in the Fergana Rim planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 1,270 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> The village lies above the reach of the canals, and its farmers grow rain-fed wheat and sunflowers on the terraced slopes, harvesting in late June. In dry springs yields fall sharply, and many young men work seasonally in [[wp:Kazakhstan]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 286. Jangy-Jol
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Jangy-Jol** ("new road") is a village in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on the southern side of the Talas valley west of the capital. Its population was 1,960 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> It was laid out in 1995, during the territorial period, to resettle families displaced in the [[First Iradi Civil War]], on a grid of streets with detached houses and orchard plots of about a quarter-hectare each. A US-funded program paid for the road, water mains and the school; the village name comes from the road built to reach it.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 287. Teskey-Jar
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Teskey-Jar** ("shaded cliff") is a village in the eastern part of the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on a loess terrace above the gorge of the upper [[Talas River|Talas]]. Its population was 820 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Its houses stand along the edge of a cliff about 40 meters high. In April 2004 part of the terrace edge collapsed after heavy rain, and thirty families were moved to new plots farther from the gorge.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 289. Köpürö-Bazar
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Köpürö-Bazar** ("bridge market") is a village in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), at the road bridge over the [[Talas River|Talas]] west of the capital. Its population was 2,090 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> After the autumn return of the flocks from the mountains, dealers hold auctions of wool, sheepskins and cattle hides at a warehouse yard in the village, buying for the [[Iradi Leatherworks Factory]] and for export to China. Live animals are traded at the [[Talas Central Cattle Mart]] nearby.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 290. Kyrk-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kyrk-Bulak** ("forty springs") is a village in the limestone foothills of the western [[Talas Alatau]], south of the Kara-Buura District, in the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 870 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> Dozens of cold springs rise along a fault at the foot of the limestone, giving the village its name and watering its hay meadows and potato fields. Its families breed [[Chongtor Horse|Chongtor]] horses, which they take up to the high pastures in summer.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 292. Ak-Jal
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Ak-Jal** ("white mane") is a village on the southern edge of the [[Betpak-Dala]] steppe, in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 830 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> Its families keep large herds of horses that graze on the open steppe all year, pawing through thin snow for grass in winter. Riders and horses from the village regularly enter the autumn *[[At Chabysh (Racing)|at chabysh]]* race on the nearby [[Keng-Talaa]] grassland.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 293. Bitik-Tash
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Bitik-Tash** ("written stone") is a village in the [[wp:Karatau Mountains|Karatau]] foothills, in the Karatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 360 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> A boulder-choked ravine above the village contains several rock shelters and more than 400 petroglyphs, most of them ibex, camels, horses and archers, which archaeologists date mainly to the [[wp:Bronze Age]] and the [[wp:Saka]] period. Flint tools of [[wp:Lower Paleolithic]] type have been collected from the terraces below the ravine since the 1970s. The site has no formal protection and is watched over by the village elders.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 294. Bulak-Bashy
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Bulak-Bashy** ("head of the spring") is a village in the Lower Naryn District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on the Naryn above [[Tash-Kömür]]. Its population was 1,430 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> It was founded in 1965 as a camp for the builders of the downstream dams of the Naryn cascade, and many residents still work for the hydropower stations and the switchyard. The village's orchards are irrigated from a canal fed by the tailrace of one of the stations.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 295. Jashyl-Köl
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Jashyl-Köl** ("green lake") is a seasonal settlement on the shore of a moraine-dammed lake of the same name, at about 2,600 meters on the southern slopes of the Chatkal range, in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). About 25 herding families live there in yurts from late June to early September.<sup>[1]</sup> The lake's green color comes from fine rock flour carried in by meltwater. The families graze sheep and yaks on the surrounding meadows and sell dairy products to trekkers on the route to [[Sary-Chelek Biosphere Reserve|Sary-Chelek]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 296. Shagyldy
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Shagyldy** ("gravelly") is a village in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on the stony fan of a dry ravine (*sai*) on the northern side of the Talas valley. Its population was 760 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Quarries in the ravine bed supply gravel and sand to building sites in [[Talas]], and trucks loaded at the pits are the village's main traffic. Small amounts of gold were once washed from the gravels, but the workings were abandoned in the 1950s.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 297. Archaly
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Archaly** ("with junipers") is a village in a forested side valley of the [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]], in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 655 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> The slopes above it carry [[Tian Shan Spruce|spruce]] and [[Mountain Juniper (Arsha)|juniper]] woods. A Soviet forestry station logged them until 1991; since 2008 the clan council has limited cutting to firewood quotas for each household. Villagers gather wild raspberries, rose hips and mushrooms in late summer and sell them to traders from the [[wp:Fergana Valley]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 298. Kapka-Tash
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kapka-Tash** ("gate rock") is a village in the Lower Naryn District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), below [[Tash-Kömür]] on the road toward the border with [[wp:Uzbekistan]], at the mouth of a short gorge cut by a side stream of the Naryn. Its population was 1,180 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Two rock pillars on either side of the gorge mouth give the village its name. Many residents trade or work seasonally on the Uzbek side of the border.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 299. Ak-Chiy
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Ak-Chiy** ("white needlegrass") is a village on the southern margin of the Moiynkum sands, in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 520 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> It takes its name from the tall needlegrass (*chiy*, *Achnatherum splendens*) that covers the salt flats around it, which families use as winter grazing for sheep. Women in the village weave the stems into patterned *chiy* screens, which are used as yurt walls and sold to craft shops in [[Nurzhan]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 300. Kalba
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kalba** is a village in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on the road from [[Talas]] to the [[Qara-Chik Border Checkpoint]]. Its population was 1,290 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Cattle and sheep brought across the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]] by herders from the tribal regions are held in pens beside the road, inspected by veterinary officers and loaded onto trucks for the [[Talas Central Cattle Mart]] and the feedlots west of the city. The yards handle the largest numbers in October and November.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 301. Kök-Döbö
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kök-Döbö** ("blue hill") is a village in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on a low hill on the southern side of the Chu lowlands, south-east of [[Nurzhan]]. Its population was 1,110 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> From the hilltop above the village there is a view over the irrigated plain toward the Moiynkum. Since 2008 the summit has carried a weather radar of the ISEZ hydrometeorological service, which tracks hailstorms over the farmland of the Chu lowlands.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 302. Kamyshtuu
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kamyshtuu** ("reedy") is a hamlet on the northern edge of the irrigated Chu lowlands, beside marshes fed by drainage water from the fields, in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 240 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> The marshes formed in the 1960s, when collector drains from the [[Chu Irrigation Canal System]] began to empty into hollows at the edge of the sands. Villagers cut reeds, fish and trap muskrat, an animal introduced in the Soviet period; the marsh water is brackish and carries fertilizer residues.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 303. Uuk-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Uuk-Bulak** ("roof-pole spring") is a village in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in the foothills south-west of [[Talas]]. Its population was 840 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> About fifteen families make yurt frames. They steam and bend willow poles into the curved roof poles (*[[Uuk (Yurt Part)|uuk]]*), make the lattice walls (*[[Kerege (Yurt Part)|kerege]]*) and carve the crowns (*[[Tündük (Yurt Part)|tündük]]*), using techniques recorded by ethnographers in the village since the 1960s. Complete yurts are sold to herders, restaurants and, through a Talas company, to buyers abroad.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 304. Besh-Köl
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Besh-Köl** ("five lakes") is a summer camp of herders at about 3,000 meters in the upper Chatkal range, in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR), beside a chain of five small glacial lakes linked by short streams. It is occupied by eight to ten families from early July to late August.<sup>[1]</sup> The lakes are fed by the [[Besh-Köl Glaciers]] above them and drain to a tributary of the [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]]. A cairn on the moraine marks the boundary between the pastures of two clans.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 305. Altyn-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Altyn-Bulak** ("golden spring") is a village in the Lower Naryn District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in the foothills north of [[Tash-Kömür]]. Its population was 940 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Naturally carbonated, iron-rich water rises from springs at the edge of the village. A bottling plant built in 1978 was privatized in 1996 and sells mineral water under the Altyn-Bulak brand across the ISEZ; it employs about 70 people. The iron in the water stains the spring basins yellow, which gave the village its name.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 306. Zhalgyz-Terek
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Zhalgyz-Terek** ("lone poplar") is a hamlet in the Moiynkum sands, in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 340 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> It began as a single herder's homestead beside a spring shaded by a large poplar, which travelers on the track across the sands used as a resting place, and grew into a village when a Soviet sheep farm built a winter camp there in the 1950s. The old poplar died in 1997; a younger tree planted beside the spring now gives the hamlet its shade.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 307. Tash-Köröö
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Tash-Köröö** ("stone pen") is a village in a side valley above the Toktogul Reservoir, in the Toktogul–Naryn planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 410 (2023 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> Its houses and walls are built of river cobbles laid without mortar, and its families keep goats and sheep on the steep slopes. The village is named after a box canyon above it whose walls shelter the flocks during blizzards; herders close its mouth with a stone wall to use it as a natural pen.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 308. Kara-Kungey
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kara-Kungey** ("dark sunny slope") is a village on the south-facing side of a valley in the northern [[Talas Alatau]], in the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 610 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> As its name suggests, the houses and apple orchards face the sun (*kungöy*), while snow lies on the shaded opposite slope (*teskey*) into early summer. Its families grow apples and potatoes and keep cattle and sheep.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 310. Kara-Say
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kara-Say** ("black ravine") is a village at about 1,900 meters in the foothills of the [[wp:Fergana Range]] above the Toktogul basin, in the Lower Naryn District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), close to the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]]. It lay in the Toktogul–Naryn zone of the tribal regions until the basin passed to the ISEZ in 2009. Its population was 960 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> The growing season is short, and its farmers sow frost-hardy barley and fodder clover on terraced fields, storing hay for the cattle and sheep that winter in the village. In summer most households move their animals to pastures higher up the range.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 311. Örüktü
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Örüktü** ("with apricots") is a village on the northern rim of the [[wp:Fergana Valley]], in the Fergana Rim planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 1,510 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> Its terraced apricot orchards are watered by stone-lined channels led from a mountain stream. In July the fruit is split and dried on the flat roofs of the houses and sold to traders from [[wp:Uzbekistan]] and the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 313. Kök-Say
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kök-Say** ("blue ravine") is a village in a limestone gorge of the [[wp:Karatau Mountains|Karatau]], in the Karatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 450 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> The village lies in the shade of the gorge walls, beside a stream whose water looks blue over the pale limestone, and it is reached by a single dirt road. Its families winter sheep and goats in stone pens along the gorge and take them onto the [[Kyzylkum Fringe]] in spring.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 314. Aral
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Aral** ("island") is a village in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), on a strip of land between two channels of the Chu west of [[Nurzhan]]. Its population was 1,060 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Groves of willow and poplar line the channels. Its families keep bees and dairy cattle, and several breed and train riding horses for buyers in Nurzhan.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 315. Kosh-Bel
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kosh-Bel** ("camp saddle") is a hamlet at about 2,400 meters on the migration route from the Toktogul basin to the summer pastures of the [[wp:Fergana Range]], in the Toktogul–Naryn planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). About 150 people live there year-round.<sup>[1]</sup> In late May and in September the herds of the lower villages pass through, and the hamlet's families sell hay, flour and fuel to herders and keep a veterinary post where animals are vaccinated before moving on.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 316. Uch-Emchek
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Uch-Emchek** ("three breasts") is a village in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), west of [[Nurzhan]], named after three rounded hillocks beside it. Its population was 1,390 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Its farms grow sugar beet for the factory of the [[Chui Sugar Refining Corporation]] at [[Besh-Terek]] and winter wheat, watered by a branch of the [[Chu Irrigation Canal System]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 318. Kyzyl-Döbö
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Kyzyl-Döbö** ("red hill") is a village at the foot of red sandstone cliffs in the [[wp:Karatau Mountains|Karatau]] foothills, in the Karatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 720 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> Several families work as saddlers and leatherworkers, making saddles, harness and whips (*kamcha*) from local hides, and women make felt saddle blankets. Their goods are sold at the [[Bazar-Terek]] market and to riders from the Talas valley.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 320. Muz-Tör
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Muz-Tör** ("ice pasture") is a hamlet below the glaciated crest of the Chatkal range, in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 90 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> It grew up beside a Soviet mountaineering camp opened in 1972, whose huts were abandoned in 1992 and partly reopened by local guides in 2015. Men from the hamlet work as guides, porters and horsemen for climbers and hunters and cut firewood in the juniper woods below.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 321. Ak-Jar
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Ak-Jar** ("white cliff") is a village near the Kazakh border on the semi-desert western edge of Irad, in the [[wp:Karatau Mountains|Karatau]] planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 910 (2022 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> It stands below a pale limestone escarpment that shelters it from the dust storms blowing off the [[Kyzylkum Fringe]]. Its families breed [[Karakul Sheep (Irad)|Karakul sheep]], whose lamb pelts were the business of the Soviet state farm based in the village until 1994.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 322. Jele-Döbö
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Jele-Döbö** ("tether hill") is a village on the northern shore of the Toktogul Reservoir, in the Lower Naryn District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ). It lay on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]] until the floor of the Toktogul basin passed to the ISEZ in 2009. Its population was 1,520 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Its name refers to the line (*jele*) to which foals are tethered during milking. Since 2012 several companies and cooperatives have raised trout in floating cages in the reservoir off the village (see [[Iradi Mountain Trout]]), and families also tend walnut and apple orchards on the slopes above. Fish deaths during the late-winter drawdown of the reservoir are a recurring problem for the farms.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 323. Jangak-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Jangak-Bulak** ("walnut spring") is a village on the southern slopes of the [[wp:Fergana Range]], in the Fergana Rim planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR), west of [[Arslanbob]]. Its population is about 1,180 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> The village lies inside the walnut-fruit forest. A grove of very old walnut trees beside its spring is visited both by Muslim villagers, who tie rags to the branches, and by Syro-Iradi Christians, whose tradition links the grove to a medieval hermit; there is no historical evidence for the hermit.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 325. Korgoncho
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Korgoncho** ("little fort") is an Uzbek-speaking village on the northern rim of the [[wp:Fergana Valley]], in the Fergana Rim planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 1,340 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> The old part of the village consists of mudbrick farmsteads enclosed by high walls with corner towers, built in the 19th century as protection against raiders in the [[wp:Khanate of Kokand|Kokand]] period. Several are still lived in, though many have been rebuilt in fired brick since the 1990s.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 326. Töö-Kuduk
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Töö-Kuduk** ("camel well") is a hamlet at a well in the western Moiynkum sands, in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). Its population is about 120 (2021 estimate).<sup>[1]</sup> Its families keep Bactrian camels and sheep, which they water at a hand-dug well about 25 meters deep. The camels are used for wool and milk and, until the 2000s, for carrying winter camps across the sands.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 328. Dary-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Dary-Bulak** ("medicine spring") is a summer encampment on the high pastures of the [[wp:Fergana Range]], in the Toktogul–Naryn planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]] (ATR). From late June to August about 30 families camp in yurts around a group of seven springs, some cold and some warm.<sup>[1]</sup> The warm bicarbonate springs reach about 38 °C and are channeled into stone-lined pools, where herders and visitors bathe for rheumatism. Soviet surveys in the 1960s recommended a sanatorium there, but none was built.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 329. Almaluu
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Almaluu** ("with apples") is a village in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in the southern foothills of the Talas valley east of the capital. Its population was 1,240 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Its apple orchards were planted in the 1960s by a collective farm. A women's cooperative founded in 2006 makes pressed felt rugs (*ala-kiyiz*) and sells them in [[Talas]] and through online shops.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 331. Tunuk-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Cities & Settlements*
**Tunuk-Bulak** ("clear spring") is a village in the Sary-Bulak District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]] (ISEZ), in the limestone foothills of the [[Talas Alatau]] south of [[Talas]]. Its population was 1,020 (2018 census).<sup>[1]</sup> Its fields of potatoes, barley and hay are watered by karst springs that emerge along faults in the limestone, fed by water that sinks into the rock higher up. The springs also supply a fish pond and the village's drinking water. The [[Kyrk-Kazyk Caves]] and the spring of [[Maryam-Bulak]] are in the same limestone belt.<sup>[2]</sup>

### 6.Category: Physical Geography (64 articles)

#### 339. Kök-Suu Peak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Kök-Suu Peak** (Iradi: *Kök-Suu chokusu*) is a mountain of 3,890 meters (12,762 ft) on the main ridge of the southern [[Talas Alatau]], between the head of the Kara-Buura valley and the Chatkal basin, in the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]].<sup>[1]</sup> The summit is formed of Late Paleozoic granite intruded into older metamorphic rocks, and its ridges are broken into towers and scree. Small cirque glaciers on its southern flank feed the Kök-Suu, a tributary of the [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]], after which the peak is named.<sup>[2]</sup> It is climbed occasionally from the Kara-Buura Pass.

#### 340. Chakmak-Say
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Chakmak-Say** ("flint ravine") is a seasonal torrent on the western slopes of the Chatkal range near the border with [[wp:Uzbekistan]], in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It is about 18 kilometers long and flows only from April to June, when the snowfields above it melt; for the rest of the year its bed is dry gravel.<sup>[1]</sup> It joins the [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]] below the main gorges. Its name comes from the flint nodules in its limestone gravels, which herders once collected for strike-a-lights.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 341. Kumdy-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Kumdy-Bulak** ("sandy spring") is a perennial spring on the southern edge of the Moiynkum sands, in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. Water rises from beneath the foot of the dunes at about two liters per second and is led into a fenced trough of concrete.<sup>[1]</sup> It is the only perennial water within a day's walk, and in winter several thousand sheep and the camels and horses of neighboring camps are watered there; clan elders set a rota for its use in dry years.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 342. Bürküt-Uya
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Bürküt-Uya** ("eagle's nest") is a steep-sided limestone ravine on the northern slopes of the [[wp:Karatau Mountains|Karatau]], in the Karatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. Its walls rise up to about 300 meters above a dry stream bed.<sup>[1]</sup> Two pairs of [[Lammergeier (Bearded Vulture)|lammergeiers]] have nested on its ledges since monitoring by [[Talas State University]] began in 1986, together with golden eagles and Himalayan griffons. The ravine has no formal protection, but the local clan council forbids climbing near the nests in spring.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 343. Kyzyl-Bel Pass
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Kyzyl-Bel Pass** ("red saddle") is a minor pass of 2,450 meters over a spur of the Chatkal range above the upper [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]] valley, in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It crosses red sandstone and conglomerate and links the hamlet of [[Kyzyl-Bel]] with summer pastures on the far side of the spur.<sup>[1]</sup> The track is usable by horses and flocks from May to October, and shepherds drive several thousand sheep over it each June.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 344. Kalmak-Kuduk
*Quality: Stub | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*
**Kalmak-Kuduk** ("Kalmyk well") is a borehole and watering point on the southern [[Betpak-Dala]], in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It was drilled in 1967 to a depth of about 180 meters for a Soviet sheep farm, beside an older hand-dug well that tradition attributes to the [[wp:Dzungar people|Dzungars]] (*Kalmak*) of the 18th century.<sup>[1]</sup> Its pump failed in 1993, and the surrounding winter pastures were little used until 2011, when an aid-funded solar pump restored the supply. It now waters about 4,000 sheep each winter.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 345. Ichke-Suu Gorge
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Ichke-Suu Gorge** ("narrow water") is a gorge about nine kilometers long on the northern slopes of the western [[Talas Alatau]], cut through sandstone and shale by a small tributary of the [[Talas River|Talas]]. Its lower end is in the Kara-Buura District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], and its upper part lies on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]].<sup>[1]</sup> The floor carries dense thickets of [[Wild Sea Buckthorn|sea buckthorn]], willow and birch, regarded by botanists as a relict of moister conditions, and villagers from the valley gather the berries in October.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 346. Boz-Suu
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Boz-Suu** ("grey water") is a river about 48 kilometers long that rises on the northern slopes of the western Kyrgyz Range and flows north into the Chu lowlands in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]]. It is fed by snowmelt, with high water in May and June, and its grey color comes from silt eroded from shale in its upper valley.<sup>[1]</sup> A diversion weir built in 1958 takes most of its water into the [[Chu Irrigation Canal System]], and in summer the riverbed below the weir is usually dry before it reaches the Chu.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 347. Tegerek-Köl
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Tegerek-Köl** ("round lake") is a cirque lake at about 3,150 meters in the northern [[Talas Alatau]], in the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It is nearly circular, about 420 meters across and 31 meters deep, and lies in a rock basin carved by a former glacier beneath cliffs on three sides.<sup>[1]</sup> The lake is frozen from November to June. Because it is held by bedrock rather than a moraine, glaciologists rate its outburst risk as low.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 348. Tuura-Say
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Tuura-Say** ("straight ravine") is a dry wash on the northern rim of the [[wp:Fergana Valley]], in the Fergana Rim planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It drains about 120 square kilometers of the foothills and carries water only after heavy rain or rapid snowmelt.<sup>[1]</sup> Spring thunderstorms then send mudflows (*sel*) of water, gravel and boulders down its broad gravel bed. A mudflow in May 2005 buried orchards and damaged about forty houses in villages on its fan, and gabion check dams were built across the wash with aid funds in 2014.<sup>[2]</sup>

</ol>

#### 350. Uch-Terek Rapids
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Uch-Terek Rapids** are a series of rapids on the Naryn above the head of the Toktogul Reservoir, in the Lower Naryn District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], which took in the floor of the Toktogul basin in 2009; the gorges upstream lie in the Toktogul–Naryn planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. Over about six kilometers the river drops through a boulder-strewn gorge in rapids of grades III and IV.<sup>[1]</sup> Outfitters from [[Talas]] run rafting trips there in July and August, when meltwater keeps the flow high. The lowest rapids are drowned when the reservoir is full in autumn.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 351. Karakol Glacier
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Karakol Glacier** is a valley glacier about four kilometers long at the head of the Western Karakol valley in the eastern [[Talas Alatau]], in the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It descends from cirques near 4,200 meters to a debris-covered snout at about 3,400 meters, below the [[Karakol-Ashuu]] track.<sup>[1]</sup> The snout has retreated by about 600 meters since the first Soviet survey in 1962. Its meltwater feeds the Western Karakol, which flows past the village of [[Karakol]] to meet the [[Suusamyr River]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 352. Ak-Suu Mineral Springs
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Ak-Suu Mineral Springs** are a group of warm, naturally carbonated springs at the foot of the [[Ak-Suu Canyon]] on the western [[Talas Alatau]], within the [[wp:Aksu-Zhabagly Nature Reserve|Aksu-Zhabagly]] nature reserve, in the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. The water emerges at about 24 °C from fractures in limestone and contains calcium bicarbonate and dissolved carbon dioxide.<sup>[1]</sup> Herders bathe in small stone-lined pools to treat joint pains, a use recorded in Soviet resort surveys. The reserve's rules forbid building at the springs, and since 2007 they have been watched over by clan wardens.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 353. Uzun-Kyr
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Uzun-Kyr** ("long ridge") is a low ridge in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], north-west of [[Nurzhan]]. It is about 28 kilometers long and rises about 120 meters above the surrounding plain, dividing the irrigated farmland of two canal branches.<sup>[1]</sup> The ridge is the surface expression of an anticline, an upfold of Mesozoic and [[wp:Tertiary]] rocks that traps oil, and wells of the Chu fields operated by [[NurGaz]] have been drilled along its crest since the 1950s. Its dry slopes are grazed by sheep.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 354. Arslanbob Falls
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Arslanbob Falls** are two waterfalls above the village of [[Arslanbob]] on the southern slopes of the [[wp:Fergana Range]], in the Fergana Rim planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. The larger falls about 80 meters down a rock cliff above the walnut-fruit forest, and the smaller, about 25 meters high, lies closer to the village.<sup>[1]</sup> Both are fed by snowmelt and are fullest in May and June. Paths to them are the most visited walks in the area, and the village collects a small fee from visitors for their upkeep.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 355. Chatkal Gorge
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Chatkal Gorge** is the series of canyons through which the lower [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]] leaves the Chatkal valley for [[wp:Uzbekistan]], in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. Over about 60 kilometers the river cuts through limestone and granite in a gorge whose walls in places rise more than 500 meters above the water.<sup>[1]</sup> Boulder-strewn rapids of grades IV and V make it a destination for expedition rafting, which has been organized from Uzbekistan and from [[Talas]] since the 1980s. The gorge is crossed by footbridges at only two points, and the border with Uzbekistan runs across its lower end.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 356. Manas Glacier
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Manas Glacier** is a glacier on the northern flank of [[Manas Peak (Irad)|Manas Peak]], the highest summit of the [[Talas Alatau]], on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]]. It is about 2.6 kilometers long and covers about 1.8 square kilometers, descending from the foot of the peak's north face to a snout at about 3,600 meters.<sup>[1]</sup> It is one of the two benchmark glaciers on which the [[Manas Peak Glaciological Observatory]] measures mass balance each year, and its snout has retreated by about 450 meters since 1970. Its meltwater feeds headwaters of the [[Talas River|Talas]].<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

Above the glacier the north face of Manas Peak rises as a wall of ice and rock about 1,200 meters high, first climbed by Soviet mountaineers in 1968 and attempted only a few times since 1992.<sup>[1]</sup>

#### 357. Kulan-Sor
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Kulan-Sor** ("Kulan salt pan") is an endorheic salt pan on the [[Kulan-Talaa]] plain in western Irad, in the [[wp:Karatau Mountains|Karatau]] planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It is about 14 kilometers long and up to five kilometers wide.<sup>[1]</sup> In spring, snowmelt from the Karatau fills it with a few centimeters of brine; by July the water has evaporated, leaving a white crust of salt and gypsum. Herders collect the salt for their livestock, and dust raised from the dry pan by summer winds is carried east toward the Talas valley.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 358. Eski-Aryk
*Quality: Stub | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*
**Eski-Aryk** ("old canal") is an irrigation canal about 19 kilometers long in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], west of [[Nurzhan]]. Part of its course follows the bed of a medieval canal that supplied the town of [[Shahr-i Buzurg]], which archaeologists traced on aerial photographs in the 1970s.<sup>[1]</sup> The canal was cleared and lined in the 1950s as a branch of the [[Chu Irrigation Canal System]], and it ends in sluice gates that distribute water to the fields of [[Shaar-Döbö]] and neighboring villages.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 360. Urmaral Tarns
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Urmaral Tarns** are a chain of three glacial lakes in a hanging valley at the head of the [[Urmaral River|Urmaral]] valley in the northern [[Talas Alatau]], on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]]. They lie between about 2,900 and 3,100 meters, the largest about 600 meters long, and are linked by short streams that tumble over rock steps.<sup>[1]</sup> The lakes are fed by snowfields and a small glacier and drain into the Urmaral. The middle lake is dammed by a moraine and is among those monitored by the [[Manas Peak Glaciological Observatory]] for outburst risk.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 361. Kara-Kazan Cirque
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Kara-Kazan Cirque** ("black cauldron") is a glacial cirque in the high Chatkal range, in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. Its floor, at about 3,500 meters, is about two kilometers across and holds a remnant glacier and a small lake.<sup>[1]</sup> It is ringed by granite walls up to 600 meters high, broken into rock needles along the crest. Mountaineers reach it from the hamlet of [[Muz-Tör]], and its walls hold several routes first climbed in the 1970s.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 362. Maryam-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Maryam-Bulak** ("Maryam's spring") is a karst spring in the limestone foothills of the [[Talas Alatau]] in the Sary-Bulak District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], venerated by members of the [[Syro-Iradi Catholic Church]] as the spring of St. Maryam. Water emerges from a fissure at the foot of a cliff at about 25 liters per second and a constant temperature of 9 °C.<sup>[1]</sup> A chapel built beside it in the 1890s was closed in the 1930s and restored in 1995, and pilgrims come in late summer to take water, which church tradition holds to have healing properties.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 365. Altyn-Say
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Altyn-Say** ("gold ravine") is a mountain stream about 22 kilometers long that flows into the [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]] from the south, in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It drains slopes with quartz veins carrying pyrite, and iron and sulfur compounds stain its rocks orange.<sup>[1]</sup> Placer gold has been washed from its gravels for centuries. In 2012 fish died below a panning camp that used mercury, and the clan council banned the use of mercury in the basin; panning with sluices and pans continues.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 366. Kuru-Say
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Kuru-Say** ("dry ravine") is a dry wash in the Kara-Buura District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], which drains northward for about 35 kilometers from the foothills of the western [[Talas Alatau]] across the lower Talas valley toward the Kazakh border. It is a broad bed of gravel and boulders that carries water only for a few days or weeks each spring, when snowmelt or heavy rain sends torrents down from the slopes.<sup>[1]</sup> In April 1998 a flood in the wash cut the [[Trans-Talas Highway]], and the road bridge was rebuilt higher in 2001.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 368. Besh-Tash Upper Lake
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Besh-Tash Upper Lake** (Iradi: *Besh-Tash köl*), also known as Lake Besh-Tash, is the largest of the glacial lakes at the head of the Besh-Tash valley on the northern slopes of the [[Talas Alatau]], within the [[Besh-Tash Ecological Conservation Area]]. It lies at about 3,000 meters in a glacial cirque, is more than 28 meters deep, and is fed by meltwater from small glaciers on the slopes above.<sup>[1]</sup> Its deep blue water drains north through the [[Besh-Tash Lower Tarn]] to the Besh-Tash River. Since 2007 the lake has been on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]], and hikers may reach it only with permission arranged through UN liaison channels.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 369. Kyzyl-Adyr Hills
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Kyzyl-Adyr Hills** ("red hills") are a belt of rolling loess hills, from which the town of [[Kyzyl-Adyr]] takes its name, south of the town in the Kara-Buura District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]]. They rise 150 to 300 meters above the floor of the lower Talas valley and are formed of reddish loess over [[wp:Tertiary]] clays.<sup>[1]</sup> The hills lie above the canals; they are used for rain-fed barley and for grazing sheep in spring and autumn. Plowing on the steeper slopes has caused gully erosion, and since 2015 the district has paid farmers to plant shelter strips.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 370. Kashkar-Aryk
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Kashkar-Aryk** ("[[wp:Kashgar]] canal") is a spring-fed stream and canal in the northern part of [[Talas]]. It rises from springs at the foot of the river terrace below the [[Old Talas Citadel Quarter]] and flows for about five kilometers through gardens and orchards to the [[Talas River|Talas]].<sup>[1]</sup> Its name is traditionally explained by [[wp:Mahmud al-Kashgari|Kashgari]] gardeners who settled beside it in the 19th century. Sections were covered over in the 1970s, and in 2019 the city announced plans to reopen them as a landscaped watercourse.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 372. Ak-Muz
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Ak-Muz** ("white ice") is a permanent snowfield on the southern slopes of the eastern [[Talas Alatau]], above the upper valley of the Uzun-Akmat, in the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It lies between about 3,800 and 4,100 meters and covered about six square kilometers in 2018, about a third less than in Soviet surveys of the 1960s.<sup>[1]</sup> Its meltwater feeds the [[Uzun-Akmat River|Uzun-Akmat]] in July and August, when it makes up a substantial share of the river's flow into the Toktogul Reservoir.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 373. Uzun-Akmat Terraces
*Quality: Stub | Category: Agriculture*
The **Uzun-Akmat Terraces** are stone-walled agricultural terraces on the slopes of the lower Uzun-Akmat valley above the Toktogul Reservoir, around the village of [[Ozgörüsh]], in the Toktogul–Naryn planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. The terraces climb up to about 200 meters above the valley floor, and their dry-stone walls are up to two meters high.<sup>[1]</sup> Some walls belong to villages flooded by the reservoir in the 1970s and are thought to be at least two centuries old. They are planted with vines, apricots and walnuts and watered by channels from side streams; terraces left unused since the 1990s are collapsing.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 374. Karool-Döbö
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Karool-Döbö** ("watch hill") is a bluff on a high river terrace at the confluence of the Kenkol and the [[Talas River|Talas]], in Kara-Buura District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], near the village of [[Ming-Örük]]. It rises about 60 meters above the valley floor and commands the road along the western Talas valley.<sup>[1]</sup> On its summit are the footings of a Kara-Khanid watchtower and the earthworks of a small [[wp:Khanate of Kokand|Kokand]] post of the 19th century, after which the bluff is named.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 375. Teshik-Talaa
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Teshik-Talaa** ("pierced steppe") is a karst plateau in the [[wp:Karatau Mountains|Karatau]], in the Karatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. Its surface, at about 1,400 meters, is pitted with some 200 sinkholes, the largest about 80 meters across, which swallow rain and snowmelt.<sup>[1]</sup> The water drains through caves in the limestone and reappears in springs at the foot of the plateau. Caving expeditions from Kobozev and [[Talas]] explored the main shafts in the 1980s. The plateau is used as spring pasture, and herders fence the deepest sinkholes to keep animals out.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 378. Kylych-Kyr
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Kylych-Kyr** ("sword ridge") is a narrow arête in the high Chatkal range, in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. About 2.5 kilometers long, it links [[Ak-Bash]] (3,920 m) with an unnamed summit of about 4,050 meters to the east.<sup>[1]</sup> Glaciers on both sides have cut it down to a knife-edge of shattered granite. It was first traversed by Soviet mountaineers in 1979.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 379. Dastgoh Creek
*Quality: Stub | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*
**Dastgoh Creek** is a small canal in the Dastgoh District of [[Nurzhan]], in the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]]. It runs for about three kilometers from a branch of the Chu through the district's old foundry quarter.<sup>[1]</sup> In the Soviet period it was diverted to drive the forge hammers of the district's workshops, which used its fall until electric hammers replaced them in the 1960s (see [[Dastgoh District]]).<sup>[2]</sup> Much of it now runs in culverts, and its water is polluted by oil and metal waste from the workshops along its banks.

#### 381. Akzhaykyn Depression
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Akzhaykyn Depression** is a closed basin in the northern Moiynkum sands, on the edge of the [[Betpak-Dala]], where the Chu ends, in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. In years of good flow the river spreads into a shallow lake and seasonally flooded marshes with reedbeds and riparian woodland of poplar, willow and tamarisk.<sup>[1]</sup> The marshes support wild boar and large numbers of migrating ducks, geese and herons, and they are fished and grazed by people from nearby villages. Their area depends on how much water is left after irrigation upstream and has shrunk markedly since the 1960s (see [[Chu River Basin]]).<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 383. Kichi-Bel
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Kichi-Bel** ("little pass") is a low pass of about 1,950 meters over a spur of the [[wp:Fergana Range]], in the Fergana Rim planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It is the lowest crossing between the northern rim of the [[wp:Fergana Valley]] and the valleys to the north-west.<sup>[1]</sup> Because it is free of snow earlier than the higher passes, herders use it with pack horses in March and April to move animals to spring pastures.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 384. Khunuk Creek
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Khunuk Creek** is a stream that rises in the foothills of the [[wp:Fergana Range]] above [[Iyık Janyl]] and flows through the summer quarter of [[Shahr-i Khunuk]] to the river below the city. It is cold and fast, fed by springs and snowmelt.<sup>[1]</sup> Teahouses and orchards line its banks in the quarter, and since the 2000s private villas have been built along it, a change criticized by local heritage groups.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 386. Ambar-Döbö
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Ambar-Döbö** ("granary hill") is a broad river terrace on the southern side of the lower Talas valley, in the Kara-Buura District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]]. Its alluvial soils are farmed for wheat and beans.<sup>[1]</sup> Excavations in 1990 uncovered about sixty bell-shaped grain storage pits cut into the terrace, some still holding charred wheat and barley, which were dated to the Kara-Khanid period (11th–12th century).<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 387. Navbahor Pond
*Quality: Stub | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*
**Navbahor Pond** is an artificial reservoir in the [[Navbahor District]] of [[Nurzhan]], in the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]]. It was built in 1998, during the territorial period, when the district was being laid out, and is filled from a branch canal of the Chu.<sup>[1]</sup> The pond, of about eight hectares, supplies water for the district's parks and street trees and is used for boating. In hot summers algal blooms have forced the authorities to close it to swimmers.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 388. Kök-Kamar Gorge
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Kök-Kamar Gorge** ("blue belt") is a narrow canyon on a right-bank tributary of the [[Kara-Darya]], on the southern slopes of the [[wp:Fergana Range]], in the Fergana Rim planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. Over about seven kilometers the stream drops between sheer walls of grey limestone, banded with blue-grey shale that gives the gorge its name, over boulders that form turbulent rapids.<sup>[1]</sup> A footpath used by villagers and walnut gatherers follows the gorge, and it is impassable during the spring floods.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 389. Karakoyun Pass
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Karakoyun Pass** is a pass of about 3,050 meters over the [[Talas Alatau]] at the head of the [[Kara-Koyun Valley]], in the Talas Alatau planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. A stony track over it links the winter quarters of the Karakoyun clan in the valley with summer pastures on the southern side of the range.<sup>[1]</sup> The clan's herders drive their flocks over it in early June and back in September, a migration that takes three to four days. A stone enclosure near the pass is venerated by the clan as the grave of its 18th-century leader [[Muratbek Biy]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 390. Sarchashma Springs
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Sarchashma Springs** (Persian: "head of the spring") are a group of perennial springs at the foot of the citadel terrace in the [[Old Talas Citadel Quarter]] of [[Talas]]. Their water was carried in brick channels to the quarter's public fountains and to a domed bathhouse built in the [[wp:Khanate of Kokand|Kokand]] period.<sup>[1]</sup> The flow has fallen since the 1980s, which hydrologists attribute to groundwater pumping in the city. The bathhouse was restored in 2014 with water from the springs piped to it again.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 391. Ak-Balyk Creek
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Ak-Balyk Creek** ("white fish") is a clear, cold tributary of the [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]] in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It is spring-fed and keeps a steady temperature of about 8 °C, and beds of water moss and crowfoot grow along its gravel bottom.<sup>[1]</sup> It holds native marinka and stocked brown trout (see [[Iradi Mountain Trout]]). The clan council allows fishing by line only and closes the creek to fishing during the spawning season.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 392. Ak-Kyr
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Ak-Kyr** ("white ridge") is a limestone ridge in the foothills of the [[Talas Alatau]] in the Sary-Bulak District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], south of [[Talas]]. It is about 11 kilometers long and rises to about 2,100 meters. Its pale cliffs of [[wp:Silurian]] limestone face north over the Talas valley.<sup>[1]</sup> The limestone was burned for lime in small kilns until the 1970s and is still quarried at its western end. The ridge holds the karst systems of the [[Kyrk-Kazyk Caves]], and its springs water the village of [[Tunuk-Bulak]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 393. Kaz-Köl
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Kaz-Köl** ("goose lake") is a group of shallow saline lakes in depressions on the southern [[Betpak-Dala]], in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. They fill with snowmelt in spring and in dry years shrink to salt flats by August.<sup>[1]</sup> In April and May, and again in September, they are a stopover for migrating waterbirds, including greater flamingos, ruddy shelducks and several species of geese. Ornithologists counted about 1,200 flamingos there in May 2016.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 395. Jangy-Aryk
*Quality: Stub | Category: Infrastructure & Transport*
**Jangy-Aryk** ("new canal") is a concrete-lined irrigation canal about 25 kilometers long in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], north of [[Nurzhan]]. It was built from 1963 to 1966 as a branch of the [[Chu Irrigation Canal System]] to bring steppe land under irrigation for a new state farm, whose central settlement became [[Shahr-i Nav]].<sup>[1]</sup> It carries about four cubic meters per second at full flow and irrigates about 5,000 hectares of lucerne, maize and barley. Its lining was repaired with aid funds in 2012.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 396. Köl-Sai
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Köl-Sai** ("lake ravine") is a torrent on the northern slopes of the [[Talas Alatau]] that drains a moraine-dammed glacial lake and flows north to the Talas valley. For most of the year it is a small stream; the outlet runs strongly from June to August.<sup>[1]</sup> On 23 July 2012 water from the lake burst through the moraine, and a flood of water and debris carried away a bridge and a stretch of road on the ISEZ side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]]. Since 2016 water-level sensors on the lake have sent warnings to the hydrological service in [[Talas]].<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 399. Kapka-Bel
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Kapka-Bel** ("gate saddle") is a low saddle at about 1,180 meters in the foothills on the northern side of the lower Talas valley, in the Kara-Buura District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]]. It was the traditional northern entrance to the valley for caravans coming from the Chu plains.<sup>[1]</sup> In the 19th century the [[wp:Khanate of Kokand]] kept a customs post on the saddle, whose foundations survive beside the old road. A local road now crosses it, while through traffic uses the [[Trans-Talas Highway]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 401. Kara-Suu Rapids
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Kara-Suu Rapids** are a series of cascades and small waterfalls on the upper Kara-Suu in the [[Talas Alatau]], on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]], just above the gorge that gives the village of [[Chong-Kapka]] its name. Over about two kilometers the river drops about 150 meters through a narrow cleft in granite.<sup>[1]</sup> A herders' path climbs beside them to the upper valley. Downstream, the river passes through the [[Kara-Suu Canyon]] and drives the [[Kara-Suu Hydroelectric Station]].<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 404. Chopo-Jar
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Chopo-Jar** ("clay cliff") is a line of red clay bluffs on the northern rim of the [[wp:Fergana Valley]], in the Fergana Rim planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]], near the village of [[Sopol-Tepa]]. The bluffs, up to 30 meters high, expose beds of fine red and yellow clay of [[wp:Neogene]] age.<sup>[1]</sup> Potters from Sopol-Tepa dig clay from pits at their foot, and red ochre from the bluffs was formerly ground for pigment.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 405. Besh-Köl Glaciers
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Besh-Köl Glaciers** are a group of five small cirque glaciers in the upper Chatkal range, in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]], above the herders' camp and lakes of [[Besh-Köl]]. Together they covered about three square kilometers in 2015.<sup>[1]</sup> Their meltwater feeds the Besh-Köl lakes and upper tributaries of the [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]]. Two of the five have shrunk to small patches of ice since the 1970s.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 406. Isyk-Bulak
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Isyk-Bulak** ("warm spring") is a group of warm bicarbonate springs in the [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]] valley near [[Terek-Sai]], in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. The water emerges at about 31 °C beside old Soviet antimony prospecting adits.<sup>[1]</sup> Villagers bathe in a pool built around the main spring. Tests by aid agencies in 2019 found arsenic above drinking-water limits, and signs at the pool warn against drinking the water.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 407. Mazar-Too
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Mazar-Too** ("shrine mountain") is an isolated granite hill on the southern [[Betpak-Dala]], in the Northern Steppe planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. Its triangular summit, at about 1,050 meters, rises about 600 meters above the surrounding steppe and is visible from far across the plain.<sup>[1]</sup> A saint's tomb (*mazar*) of piled stones stands on the summit, and herders climb to it to make offerings before the spring migration.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 409. Ak-Bash
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Ak-Bash** ("white head") is a glaciated limestone peak of 3,920 meters (12,860 ft) in the high Chatkal range, in the Chatkal Highlands planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. Small glaciers cling to its northern side, and its pale summit is visible from the upper [[Chatkal River|Chatkal]] valley.<sup>[1]</sup> Alpine pastures below it are grazed in summer. The [[Kylych-Kyr]] arête links it with a higher summit to the east.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 411. Muz-Suu
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Muz-Suu** ("ice water") is a glacier-fed river about 26 kilometers long that flows north from the [[Talas Alatau]] and joins the [[Talas River|Talas]] in the Talas District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]]. It rises in cirque glaciers on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]].<sup>[1]</sup> Its flow is highest in July and August, when glacier melt peaks, and its milky water is taken by two canal intakes for the farms of the valley floor.<sup>[2]</sup> On the night of 22 July 2016 a partial outburst of Muz-Köl, a moraine-dammed lake at its head, washed out a road bridge and one of the intakes and damaged houses in Tash-Bulak, without loss of life (see [[Glacier retreat in Irad]]).

#### 412. Sary-Bulak Gorge
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Sary-Bulak Gorge** is a steep limestone gorge in the foothills of the [[Talas Alatau]], in the Sary-Bulak District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]], about 28 kilometers south of [[Talas]]. Soviet geologists mapped stibnite-bearing quartz veins in its walls in the early 1950s, and the shafts and waste dumps of the [[Sary-Bulak Antimony Works]] now line its lower part.<sup>[1]</sup> A haul road and a rail spur run along the gorge floor to the mine and the village of [[Sary-Bulak]]. Streams leaving the gorge carry elevated arsenic and antimony.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 413. Koi-Bel
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Koi-Bel** ("sheep saddle") is a pass of 2,680 meters (8,792 ft) on a ridge of the [[wp:Fergana Range]] above the Toktogul basin, in the Toktogul–Naryn planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It is crossed by a pack-horse trail.<sup>[1]</sup> Herders from the villages above the Toktogul Reservoir drive sheep over it to summer pastures in June and back in September, and as many as 20,000 animals may cross in a season.<sup>[2]</sup>

#### 415. Kashka-Suu Glaciers
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Kashka-Suu Glaciers** are three small valley glaciers at the head of the [[Kashka-Suu]] on the northern slopes of the [[Talas Alatau]], on the tribal side of the [[Qara-Chik Demarcation Line]]. Their snouts lie between about 3,500 and 3,700 meters.<sup>[1]</sup> All three have been retreating since measurements began in the 1960s, and their combined area fell by about a quarter between 1970 and 2018. Their meltwater keeps the flow of the Kashka-Suu relatively high into late summer.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

#### 416. Sary-Kyr
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Sary-Kyr** ("yellow ridge") is a rolling ridge of loess in the foothills on the southern side of the Chu lowlands, in the Chu District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]]. It rises about 200 meters above the plain, above the reach of the canals.<sup>[1]</sup> Its slopes are sown with rain-fed barley in autumn and grazed by sheep after the harvest. Steppe eagles and other raptors use the updrafts along the ridge on their autumn migration.<sup>[2]</sup>

</ol>

{{Irad-mountain-stub}}

#### 418. Sadaf-Jar
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Sadaf-Jar** ("shell cliff") is a line of sandstone bluffs on the northern rim of the [[wp:Fergana Valley]], in the Fergana Rim planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. The cliffs, about 40 meters high, expose red and grey [[wp:Paleogene]] sandstones and marls with beds of fossil oyster shells, from which the Uzbek-speaking villagers named them.<sup>[1]</sup> Long-legged buzzards and kestrels nest on the ledges.<sup>[2]</sup>

<li id="cite_note-2"><b>^</b> Talas State University, Department of Zoology (2017). <i>Birds of the Talas Valley and Adjacent Ranges</i>. Talas: Talas State University Press.</li>
</ol>

{{Irad-geo-stub}}

#### 429. Tar-Kapchygai
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
**Tar-Kapchygai** ("narrow gorge") is a granite gorge on the middle course of the [[Uzun-Akmat River|Uzun-Akmat]], between its upper pastures and the lower valley above the Toktogul Reservoir, in the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]]. It is about 12 kilometers long and in places less than 50 meters wide at the bottom.<sup>[1]</sup> Its shaded floor and lower slopes carry woods of birch, rowan and willow. A track used by herders moving between the lower villages and the summer pastures follows the gorge and is often blocked by rockfalls in spring.<sup>[2]</sup>

</ol>

{{Irad-geo-stub}}

#### 431. Kyrk-Kazyk Caves
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Kyrk-Kazyk Caves** ("forty stakes") are a karst cave system in the limestone of the [[Ak-Kyr]] ridge, in the Sary-Bulak District of the [[Irad Special Economic Zone]]. About 1.8 kilometers of passages have been surveyed. Their name comes from the stalagmites in the main chamber.<sup>[1]</sup> The caves hold hibernating colonies of greater and lesser horseshoe bats, the largest known in Irad, counted at about 2,300 bats in the winter of 2018. Since 2004 the ISEZ has protected them as a natural monument, and the entrance is gated in winter.<sup>[1]</sup><sup>[2]</sup>

{{Irad-geo-stub}}

#### 432. Kum-Say Fan
*Quality: Stub | Category: Geography & Nature*
The **Kum-Say Fan** is an alluvial fan on the eastern rim of the [[wp:Fergana Valley]], in the Fergana Rim planning zone of the [[Autonomous Tribal Regions]], built of gravel and loess by the Kum-Say stream, a right-bank tributary of the [[Kara-Darya]], where it leaves the foothills. It covers about 90 square kilometers.<sup>[1]</sup> Its lower part is irrigated by canals from the stream and grows cotton, maize and orchards. Allocation of the stream's water between farms on the fan and villages downstream in [[wp:Uzbekistan]] is a recurring source of dispute.<sup>[2]</sup>
